Therefore the yellow car was a danger and another must be procured in its place. The trick was obvious, and French had heard of something like it before.

===Chinese===
那辆黄车的发动机究竟有什么毛病，他毫不怀疑：毛病正是哈克沃思所说的那个——压根儿没有。弗伦奇看得明白，这整出戏不过是布莱辛顿换车灭迹的一条计。黄色的阿姆斯特朗-西德利许多人都知道是他的，布莱辛顿要一辆他自以为查不出来历的车。他从报上想必已经看到切恩逃过了给他备下的下场，也一定会疑心这位受辱的年轻人会把知道的一切都交到警方手里。因此黄车是个祸根，必须另弄一辆来顶替。这把戏一望便知，弗伦奇以前也听说过类似的勾当。

===Original===
But though the main part of the scheme was clear to French, the details were not. From the statement of Mrs. Sproule, the invalid of Colton Street, the yellow car had left Sime’s house at about 1:45. According to this Hackworth it had reached the garage at a minute or so before two. Now, from Colton Street to the garage was a ten or twelve minutes’ drive, therefore Blessington must have gone practically direct. Moreover, when he left Colton Street Joan Merrill and the other members of the gang were in the car, but when he reached the garage he was alone. Where had the others dismounted?

===Chinese===
不过，这条计策的主干弗伦奇虽已看明，细节还没有。据科尔顿街那位卧病的斯普劳尔太太说，黄车约莫 1:45 离开赛姆家；据这位哈克沃思说，两点差一分上下到了车行。而从科尔顿街到车行，开车要十到十二分钟，可见布莱辛顿几乎是直奔而来的。况且，他离开科尔顿街时，琼·梅里尔和团伙其余的人都在车上，到车行时却只剩他一人。其余的人是在哪儿下的车呢？

===Original===
Another question suggested itself to French, and he thought that if he could answer it he would probably be able to answer the first as well. Why did Blessington select this particular garage? He did not know this Hackworth﻿—the man had said he had never seen Blessington before. Why then this particular establishment rather than one of the scores nearer Sime’s dwelling?

===Chinese===
另一个问题又浮上弗伦奇心头，他想，倘若能答出这一问，头一问多半也就跟着解了。布莱辛顿为什么偏偏挑中这家车行？他并不认识这个哈克沃思——那人自己说过，从前从未见过布莱辛顿。放着离赛姆住处更近的几十家不挑，为什么偏偏是这一家？

===Original===
For some minutes French puzzled over this point, and then a probable explanation struck him. There, just a hundred yards or more away, was a place admirably suited for dropping his passengers and picking them up again﻿—Waterloo Station. What more natural for Blessington than to pull up at the departure side with the yellow Armstrong Siddeley and set them down? What more commonplace for him than to pick them up at the arrival side with the black Napier? While he was changing the cars, they could enter, mingle with the crowds of passengers, work their way across the station and be waiting for him as if they had just arrived by train.

===Chinese===
这个疑点弗伦奇琢磨了好几分钟，随后想出了一个说得通的解释。就在一百码开外——也许还多——有一个放下乘客、再接他们上车的绝佳去处：滑铁卢车站。布莱辛顿驾着黄色的阿姆斯特朗-西德利，在出发一侧停下，把他们放下，岂不顺理成章？再驾着黑色的内皮尔，到到达一侧去接他们，岂不平常无奇？趁他换车的工夫，他们尽可以进站，混入旅客的人流，穿过车站，装作刚下火车的样子等他。

===Original===
Late as it was, French returned to the Yard and put a good man on to make inquiries at Waterloo in the hope of proving his theory. Then, tired and very hungry, he went home.

===Chinese===
夜虽然深了，弗伦奇还是回到苏格兰场，派了个得力人手去滑铁卢车站查问，指望证实他的推断。然后，他又累又饿，回家去了。

===Original===
But when he had finished supper and, ensconced in his armchair with a cigar, had looked through the evening paper, interest in the case reasserted itself, and he determined that he would have a look at the scrap of paper which he had found in the pocket of one of Dangle’s waistcoats.

===Chinese===
可是等他吃过晚饭，叼着雪茄在扶手椅里坐定，把晚报翻完，对案子的兴味又占了上风，他便决意把那片从丹格尔马甲口袋里找到的纸拿出来看看。

===Original===
As has been said, it was a list or memorandum of certain articles, written on the back of part of an old hotel bill. French reread the items with something as nearly approaching bewilderment as a staid inspector of the Yard can properly admit. Peaches, safety matches, the *Forsyte Saga*, pencil, fountain pen ink, and a sou’wester! What in the name of goodness could anyone want with such a heterogeneous collection? And the quantities! Three dozen tins of peaches, and six dozen boxes of matches! Enough to do a small expeditionary force, French thought whimsically, though he did not see an expeditionary force requiring the works of John Galsworthy, ink, and pencils.

===Chinese===
如前所说，那是一份物品清单，或者说备忘录，写在半张旧旅馆账单的背面。弗伦奇把各项重读一遍，心里那份近乎茫然的神气，已是一位稳重的苏格兰场探长所能容许的极限。桃子、安全火柴、《福尔赛世家》（*The Forsyte Saga*）、铅笔、自来水笔墨水，还有一顶油布雨帽（sou’wester）！老天爷在上，这么一锅大杂烩究竟有什么用处？还有那数目！三打罐头桃子，六打火柴！倒够一支小小的远征队受用了——弗伦奇异想天开地想，只是他没见过哪支远征队还需要约翰·高尔斯华绥（John Galsworthy）的著作、墨水和铅笔。

===Original===
And yet was this idea so absurd? Did not these articles, in point of fact, suggest an expedition? Peaches, matches, pencils, and ink﻿—all these articles were commonplace and universally obtainable. Did the fact that a quantity were required not mean that Dangle or his friends were to be cut off for some considerable time from the ordinary sources of supply? It certainly looked like it. And as he thought over the other articles, he saw that they too were not inconsistent with the same idea. The *Forsyte Saga* was distinguished from most novels in a peculiar and indeed a suggestive manner. It consisted of a number of novels, each full length or more than full length, but the point of interest was that the entire collection was published on thin paper in this one volume. Where could one get a greater mass of reading matter in a smaller bulk: in other words, where could one find a more suitable work of fiction to carry with one on an expedition?

===Chinese===
不过，这个念头当真如此荒唐吗？这些物品，事实上岂不正暗示着一场远征？桃子、火柴、铅笔、墨水——样样平常，处处可买。需要这么大的数量，岂不说明丹格尔或他的朋友们将有相当一段时日，同寻常的补给来源相隔绝？看情形确是如此。再把其余几样想一想，他觉得它们同这个想法也不相悖。《福尔赛世家》同大多数小说不同，那不同之点别具一格，而且耐人寻味：它包含好几部小说，部部都是足本，甚至比足本还长，而妙就妙在全集合印在这一卷里，用的是薄纸。同样一卷书，哪里还能装下更多的阅读材料？换句话说，远征随身携带的小说，哪里还能找到更合适的？

===Original===
The sou’wester also fitted from this point of view into the scheme of things, but it added a distinctive suggestion all its own: that of the sea. French’s thoughts turned towards a voyage. But it could not be an ordinary voyage in a well-appointed liner, where peaches and matches and novels would be as plentiful as in the heart of London. Nor did it seem likely that it could be a trip in the *Enid*. Such a craft could not remain out of touch with land for so long a period as these stores seemed to postulate. French could not think of anything that seemed exactly to meet the case, though he registered the idea of an expedition as one to be kept in view.

===Chinese===
从这一点看，油布雨帽也同这盘打算扣合得上，只是它另添了一层独有的暗示：海。弗伦奇的思路转向了航海。但这不会是设备齐全的邮轮上的寻常航行——在那种船上，桃子、火柴、小说会同在伦敦市中心一样充足。看样子也不像乘*伊尼德*号出游：这样一艘小船，不可能如这批储备所要求的那样，长时期同陆地断绝音讯。有什么正好对得上号的，弗伦奇想不出来，不过他把“远征”这个念头记在心里，当作值得留意的方向之一。

===Original===
Leaving the point for the time being, he turned over the paper and began to examine its other side.

===Chinese===
这个问题暂且放下，他把纸片翻过来，开始查看另一面。

===Original===
It formed the middle portion of an old hotel bill, the top and bottom having been torn off. The items indicated a stay of one night only being merely for bed and breakfast. The name of the hotel had been torn off with the bill head, and also all but a few letters of the green rubber receipt stamp at the bottom. French felt that if he could only ascertain the identity of the hotel it might afford him a valuable clue, and he settled down to study it in as close detail as possible.

===Chinese===
这是一张旧旅馆账单的中段，上头下尾都撕掉了。从账目看，只宿了一夜，不过是房钱加早餐。旅馆的名称随账单抬头一同撕去，底下那枚绿色橡皮收讫戳，也只剩下几个字母。弗伦奇觉得，只要能查明这家旅馆，也许就能得到一条宝贵的线索，于是他沉下心来，尽可能细地研究起来。

===Original===
He recalled two statements that Speedwell had made about Dangle. First, the melancholy detective had said that commencing about a fortnight after the acquisition by the gang of Price’s letter and the tracing, Dangle had begun paying frequent visits to the Continent or Ireland, and secondly, that in a tube lift he had overheard Dangle say that he was crossing on a given night, but would be back the next. French thought he might take it for granted that this bill had been incurred on one of these trips. He wondered if Dangle had always visited the same place, as, if so, the bill would refer to an hotel near enough to England to be visited in one day. Of none of this was there any evidence, but French believed that it was sufficiently probable to be taken as a working hypothesis. If it led nowhere, he could try something else.

===Chinese===
他想起斯皮德韦尔讲过的关于丹格尔的两句话。第一，那位郁郁寡欢的侦探说过，自团伙弄到普赖斯的信和那张描图之后约莫两个星期起，丹格尔便频频前往欧陆或爱尔兰；第二，他曾在地铁的升降梯里偶然听见丹格尔说，某晚要渡海过去，第二天便回。弗伦奇觉得不妨认定，这张账单就是那一趟趟旅行中某一趟留下的。他疑心丹格尔回回是否总去同一处；倘若如此，账单所指的旅馆必是近得一天可以打来回的地方。这些固然都无凭据，但弗伦奇相信，作为一条工作假说，可能性足够大了。倘若钻不通，再换路子不迟。

===Original===
Assuming then that one could cross to the place in one night and return the next, it was obvious that it must be comparatively close to England, and, the language on the bill being French, it must be in France or Belgium. He took an atlas and a *Continental Bradshaw*, and began to look out the area over which this condition obtained. Soon he saw that while the whole of Belgium and the northwest of France, bounded by a rough line drawn through Chalons, Nancy, Dijon, Angoulême, Chartres, and Brest, were within the *possible* limit, giving a reasonable time in which to transact business, it was more than likely the place did not lie east of Brussels and Paris.

===Chinese===
那么，姑且假定那地方一夜可渡、次日可返，它显然离英格兰相当近；又因账单上用的是法文，必在法国或比利时。他取出一册地图集和一本《欧陆布拉德肖列车时刻表》（*Continental Bradshaw*），开始查找符合这一条件的地域范围。不久他便看出：比利时全境，加上法国西北部——以大致穿过沙隆（Chalons）、南锡（Nancy）、第戎（Dijon）、昂古莱姆（Angoulême）、沙特尔（Chartres）与布雷斯特（Brest）的一条粗线为界——都在*可能*的限度之内，办正经事的时间绰绰有余；只是那地方多半不会在布鲁塞尔和巴黎以东。

===Original===
He turned back to the torn bill. Could he learn nothing from it?

===Chinese===
他回到那张撕剩的账单上。难道从它身上就再榨不出一点消息吗？

===Original===
First, as to the charges. With the franc standing at eighty, twenty four francs seemed plenty for a single room, though it was by no means exorbitant. It and the 4.50 fr. for *petit déjeuner* suggested a fairly good hotel﻿—probably what might be termed good second-class﻿—not one of the great hotels de luxe like the Savoy in London or the Crillon or Claridge’s in Paris, but one that ordinary people patronized, and which would be well known in its own town.

===Chinese===
先看收费。法郎行市八十的时候，一间单人房二十四法郎，价钱似嫌不小，却决算不上离谱。这个数目，连同 4.50 法郎的 *petit déjeuner*（法式早餐），说明这家旅馆相当不错——大概称得上上等二流——不是伦敦的萨伏伊（Savoy）、巴黎的克里永（Crillon）或克拉里奇（Claridge’s）那一路的大饭店，而是普通人光顾的那一种，在本城之内颇有名气。

===Original===
Of all the information available, the most promising line of research seemed that of the rubber stamp, and to that French now turned his attention. The three lines read:

===Chinese===
在现有的全部线索里，最有指望的研究对象似乎就是那枚橡皮戳，弗伦奇此刻便把注意力转到这上面。那三行是：

===Original===
> … uit 

===Chinese===
> … uit 

===Original===
 … lon, 

===Chinese===
 … lon, 

===Original===
 … S.

===Chinese===
 … S.

===Original===
French thought he had something that might help here. He rose, crossed the room, and after searching in his letter file, produced three or four papers. These were hotel bills he had incurred in France and Switzerland when he visited those countries in search of the murderer of Charles Gething of the firm of Duke & Peabody, and he had brought them home with him in the hope that some day he might return as a holidaymaker to these same hotels. Now perhaps they would be of use in another way.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇想，这一回他手里可有帮得上忙的东西了。他站起身，穿过房间，在信件夹里翻寻了一阵，取出三四张纸来。那是他当年为侦缉杜克-皮博迪商行（Duke & Peabody）查尔斯·格辛（Charles Gething）凶杀案、前往法国和瑞士时住店留下的旅馆账单；他把它们带回家来，原指望有朝一日以度假客的身份重访那几家旅馆。如今，它们或许能派上另一种用场了。

===Original===
He spread them out and examined their receipt stamps. From their analogy the “… uit” on his fragment obviously stood for the words “*Pour acquit*,” anglice: “paid.” The middle line ending in “… lon” was unquestionably the name of the hotel, and the third, ending in *S*, that of its town. And here again was a suggestion as to the size of the establishment. A street was not included in the address. It must therefore be well known in its town.

===Chinese===
他把它们摊开，逐一查看上面的收讫戳。两相比照，他那片纸上的“… uit”显然代表“*Pour acquit*”二字——译成英文就是“付讫”。中间一行以“… lon”收尾，毫无疑问是旅馆的名称；第三行以 *S* 收尾，是所在的城镇。而这里又透露出一点关于旅馆规模的消息：地址里不含街名。可见这家旅馆在本城必定人人皆知。

===Original===
It seemed to him moreover that this fact also conveyed a suggestion as to the size of the town. If the latter were Paris or Brussels﻿—as he had thought not unlikely as both these names ended in *s*﻿—a street address would almost certainly have been given. The names of the hotel and town alone pointed to a town of the same standing as the hotel itself﻿—a large town to have so important an hotel, but not a capital city. In other words, there was a certain probability the hotel was situated in a large town comparatively near the English Channel, Paris and Brussels being excepted.

===Chinese===
再者，他觉得这一事实对城镇的规模也有所暗示。倘若那城镇是巴黎或布鲁塞尔——他本觉得并非无此可能，因为这两个名字都以 *s* 收尾——那么地址上几乎一定会写明街名。单凭旅馆名和城镇名，便指向一座同旅馆身份相称的城镇：城镇不小，才容得下这样一家要紧的旅馆，却不是国都。换句话说，颇有几分可能：这家旅馆坐落在离英吉利海峡相当近的一座大城里——巴黎与布鲁塞尔除外。

===Original===
As French sat pondering over the affair, he saw suddenly that further information was obtainable from the fact that the lettering on a rubber stamp is always done symmetrically. Once more rising, he found a small piece of tracing paper, and placing this over the mutilated receipt stamp, he began to print in the missing letters of the first line. His printing was not very good, but he did not mind that. All he wanted was to get the spacing of the letters correct, and to this end he took a lot of trouble. He searched through the advertisements in several papers until he found some type of the same kind as that of the “… uit,” and by carefully measuring the other letters he at last satisfied himself as to just where the *P* of *Pour acquit* would stand. This, he hoped, would give him the number of letters in the names of both the hotel and the town. Drawing a line down at right angles to the *t* of acquit, he found that the *n* of “… lon” projected slightly over a quarter inch farther along, while the *S* of the town was almost directly beneath. By drawing another line down from the *P* of *Pour*, and measuring these same distances from it, he found the lengths of the names of hotel and town, and by further careful examination and spacing of type, he reached definite conclusions. The name of the hotel, including the word hotel, contained from eighteen to twenty letters and that of the town six, more or less according to whether letters like *I* or *W* predominated.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇坐在那里琢磨这件事，忽然又看出一层：橡皮戳上的刻字总是左右对称排布的，由此还能再挤出些消息。他又站起身，找出一小张描图纸，覆在那枚残缺的收讫戳上，动手补排第一行缺掉的字母。他写的字不大高明，好在他并不在乎。他要的只是字母间距排得准确，为此他下了一番苦功。他翻遍几份报纸的广告栏，直到找出一副同“… uit”同类的铅字，再仔细量过其余字母，才终于拿准了 *Pour acquit* 的 *P* 该落在哪里。他指望由此得出旅馆名和城镇名各自的字母数目。他从 acquit 的 *t* 上引一条垂直线下来，量得“… lon”的 *n* 略微探出四分之一英寸挂零，而城镇的 *S* 几乎正在正下方。再从 *Pour* 的 *P* 引下一条线，从它量出同样的距离，他便得出了旅馆名和城镇名的长度；又经一番仔细审看、斟酌字距，他得出了确定的结论：旅馆的名称——连“旅馆”（hotel）一词在内——含十八到二十个字母；城镇名含六个字母上下，视 *I* 或 *W* 这类字母的多寡而略有出入。

===Original===
He was pleased with his progress. Starting from nothing he had evolved the conception of an important hotel﻿—the something-lon, in a large town situated in France or Belgium, and comparatively near the English Channel, the name of the town consisting of five, six, or seven letters of which the last one was *S*. Surely, he thought, such an hotel would not be hard to find.

===Chinese===
他对自己的进展颇为满意。从一无所有出发，他已勾勒出这样一家旅馆的概念：一家要紧的旅馆——“什么什么隆”，坐落在一座法国或比利时的大城里，离英吉利海峡相当近；城镇名由五、六或七个字母组成，末一个字母是 *S*。他想，这样一家旅馆，总归不难找到吧。

===Original===
If he was correct as to the size of the town, it was one which would be marked on a fairly small scale map, and taking his atlas, he began to make a list of all those which seemed to meet the case. He soon saw there were a number﻿—Calais, Amiens, Beauvais, Étaples, Arras, Soissons, Troyes, Ypres, Bruges, Roulers, and Malines.

===Chinese===
倘若他对城镇规模的判断不错，这样的城镇在比例尺相当小的地图上也该标得出来。他取出地图集，开始把似乎合乎条件的城镇一一开列成单。不多时他便看出有一批：加来（Calais）、亚眠（Amiens）、博韦（Beauvais）、埃塔普勒（Étaples）、阿拉斯（Arras）、苏瓦松（Soissons）、特鲁瓦（Troyes）、伊珀尔（Ypres）、布鲁日（Bruges）、鲁瑟拉勒（Roulers）和马林（Malines）。

===Original===
He had by this time become so excited over his quest that in spite of the hour﻿—it was long past his bedtime﻿—he telephoned to the Yard to send him Baedeker’s Guides to Northern France and Belgium, and when these came he began eagerly looking up the hotels in each of the towns on his list. For a considerable time he worked on without result, then suddenly he laughed from sheer delight.

===Chinese===
此时他对这桩追寻已兴奋到这地步：虽然时间不早——早过了他睡觉的钟点——他还是打电话到苏格兰场，让人把贝德克尔《北法与比利时旅行指南》（Baedeker’s Guides）送来。书一到，他便迫不及待地逐一查访单上各城镇的旅馆。查了老半天毫无结果，忽然间，他出于纯粹的欢喜笑出声来。

===Original===
He had reached Bruges, and there, third on the list, was “Grand Hôtel du Sablon!” Moreover, this name exactly filled the required space.

===Chinese===
他查到了布鲁日，那里单子上第三家赫然是“Grand Hôtel du Sablon”——萨布隆大旅馆！况且这名字不多不少，恰好填满所需的空当。

===Original===
“Got it in one,” he chuckled, feeling immensely pleased with himself.

===Chinese===
“一枪中的。”他轻声笑道，心里得意极了。

===Original===
But French, if sometimes an enthusiastic optimist and again a down and out pessimist, was at all times thorough. He did not stop at Bruges. He worked all the way through the list, and it was not until he had satisfied himself that no other hotel fulfilling the conditions existed in any of the other towns, that he felt himself satisfied. It was true there was an Hotel du Carillon in Malines, but this name was obviously too short for the space.

===Chinese===
不过弗伦奇这个人，有时是热忱百倍的乐天派，忽而又是一蹶不振的悲观论者，唯独在周密二字上从不打折扣。他没有止步于布鲁日。他把单子从头到尾查了个遍，直到确证其余城镇再无一家合乎条件的旅馆，这才安下心来。马林确有一家卡里永旅馆（Hôtel du Carillon），只是这名字对那空当来说，显然太短了。

===Original===
As he went jubilantly to bed, the vision of a trip to the historic city of Bruges bulked large in his imagination.

===Chinese===
他欢天喜地上床去睡，想象里满满当当，尽是那趟历史名城布鲁日之行的图景。

## XVI A Tale of Two Cities / 十六 双城记

===Original===
Next morning French had an interview with his chief at the Yard at which he produced the torn hotel bill, and having demonstrated the methods by which he had come to identify it with the Grand Hôtel du Sablon in Bruges, suggested that a visit there might be desirable. To his secret relief Chief Inspector Mitchell took the same view, and it was arranged that he should cross as soon as he could get away.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，弗伦奇在苏格兰场与上司面谈，拿出了那张撕碎的旅馆账单，说明了自己凭什么一步步认定它出自布鲁日的萨布隆大旅馆（Grand Hôtel du Sablon），并建议：似有必要亲自去跑一趟。使他暗暗宽心的是，米切尔总探长（Chief Inspector Mitchell）所见略同，于是便这样定下来：他一脱开身，即刻渡海前往。

===Original===
On his return to his room he found Cheyne waiting for him. The young man seemed to have aged by years since his frenzied appeal to the Yard, and his anxious face and distrait manner bore testimony to the mental stress through which he was passing. Eagerly he inquired for news.

===Chinese===
回到自己房间，他发现切恩正等着他。自从那回疯了一般跑到苏格兰场求告之后，这年轻人仿佛老了好几岁；一张焦虑的脸、一副心不在焉的神气，都印证着他此刻正承受着怎样的精神煎熬。他急切地打听消息。

===Original===
“None so far, I’m sorry to say,” French answered, “except that we have found that Miss Merrill did return to her rooms that night,” and he told what he had learned of Joan’s movements, as well as of his visit to Hackworth’s garage, and of Blessington’s exchange of cars. But of Bruges and the hotel bill he said nothing. Cheyne, he felt sure, would have begged to be allowed to accompany him to Belgium, and this he did not want. But in his kindly way he talked sympathetically to the young man reiterating his promise to let him know directly anything of importance was learned.

===Chinese===
“很抱歉，至今还没有，”弗伦奇答道，“只查实了一件事：梅里尔小姐那晚确实回过自己的房间。”于是他把打听到的琼的种种行止、他去哈克沃思车行的经过、以及布莱辛顿换车的情况，一一说了。唯独布鲁日和旅馆账单，他只字未提。他料定切恩必定会苦苦央求，要跟他同去比利时，而这正是他不愿意的。不过他还是好言好语，对这年轻人宽慰了一番，重申一有要紧消息便即刻相告的承诺。

===Original===
Cheyne having reluctantly taken his leave, French turned to routine business, which had got sadly behind during the last few days. At this he worked all the morning, but on his return from lunch he found that further news had come in.

===Chinese===
切恩老大不情愿地告辞去了，弗伦奇便回头料理日常公务——这几天下来，积压得实在不成样子。他一上午都埋头于此，谁知吃过午饭回来，发现又有一条新消息送到。

===Original===
Sergeant Burnett, the man he had put on the Waterloo Station job, was waiting for him, and reported success in his mission. He had, he said, spent the whole of the day from early morning at the station, and at last he had obtained what he wanted. A taximan on a nearby stand had been called to the footpath at the arrival side of the station at about 2:00 p.m. He had drawn up behind an old black car, which he had thought was a Napier. His own fare, a lady, kept him waiting for a few seconds while she took a somewhat leisurely farewell of the gentleman who was seeing her off, and during this time he had idly watched the vehicle in front. He had seen an invalid lady in a sable colored fur coat being helped in. There was a second lady with her, and a tall man. The three got in, and the car moved off at the same time as his own. Sergeant Burnett had questioned the man on the appearance of the travelers, and was pretty certain that they were Joan, Susan, and Sime. Dangle, so far as he could learn, was not with them.

===Chinese===
他派去办滑铁卢车站差事的伯内特警士正候着他，报告差事办成了。据他说，他从一大早起就在车站泡了一整天，终于问出了想要的东西。附近候客点上的一个出租汽车司机，那天下午 2:00 前后被叫到车站到达一侧的人行道旁。他把车停在一辆黑色旧车后头，心里估摸着那是辆纳皮尔（Napier）。他自己的乘客是位太太，同送行的先生慢条斯理地道别，害他多等了几秒钟；就在这几秒钟里，他闲着无事，把前头那辆车瞧了个大概。他看见一位身穿黑貂色裘皮大衣、病恹恹模样的太太被人搀扶上车，随行的还有另一位太太和一个高个子男人。三人上了车，那车便同他自己的车同时开动。伯内特警士盘问过那人几位旅客的相貌，颇有把握地认定：正是琼、苏珊和赛姆。至于丹格尔，据他所能查知的，没有同行。

===Original===
French felt the sudden thrill of the artist who has just caught the elusive effect of light which he wanted, as he reflected how sound had been his deduction. He had considered it likely that these people would use Waterloo Station to effect the change of cars, and now it seemed that they had done so. Nothing like a bit of imagination, he thought, as he good-naturedly complimented the sergeant on his powers, and dismissed him.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇顿觉一阵艺术家式的兴奋——好比恰好捉住了那道寻觅已久、倏忽难测的光影效果。他暗自回味，自己的推断何等站得住脚。他本来就料定这伙人多半会借滑铁卢车站完成换车，如今看来果不其然。他想，什么都比不上一点想象力。他厚道地夸奖了警士一番本事，便打发他去了。

===Original===
Having too much to see to at the Yard to catch the 2:00 p.m. from Victoria for Ostend, he rang up and engaged a berth on the Harwich-Zeebrugge boat, and that night at 8:40 p.m. he left Liverpool Street for Belgium.

===Chinese===
苏格兰场里待办的事太多，他赶不上下午 2:00 从维多利亚车站（Victoria）开往奥斯坦德（Ostend）的车，便打电话在哈里奇—泽布吕赫（Harwich–Zeebrugge）航线的船上订了个铺位，当晚 8:40 从利物浦街车站（Liverpool Street）动身去比利时。

===Original===
Apart from his actual business, he was looking forward with considerable keenness to the trip. Foreign travel had become perhaps his greatest pleasure, and he had never yet been in Belgium. Moreover he had always heard Bruges mentioned as the paradise of artists, and in a rather shamefaced way he admitted an interest in and appreciation of art. He had determined that if at all possible he would snatch enough time to see at least the more interesting parts of the old town.

===Chinese===
且不说正事，他对这趟旅行本来就怀着相当热切的期待。出洋旅行如今大概要算他最大的乐趣了，而比利时他还从没到过。况且他素来听人把布鲁日称作艺术家的天堂，他自己也多少有点不好意思地承认，他对艺术既有兴趣，也能鉴赏。他打定主意，只要腾得开，就挤出点时间，至少把这座老城比较有意思的部分看上一看。

===Original===
They left the Parkeston Quay at 10:30, and by 6 next morning French was on deck. He was anxious to miss no possible sight of the approach of Zeebrugge. He had read with a thrilled and breathless interest the story of what was perhaps the greatest naval exploit of all time﻿—as, indeed, who has not?﻿—and as the long, low line of the famous mole loomed up rather starboard of straight ahead, his heart beat faster and a lump came in his throat. There, away to the right, round the curve of the long pier, must have been where *Vindictive* boarded, where in an inferno of fire her crew reached with their scaling ladders the top of the great sea wall, and climbing down on the inside, joined a hand-to-hand fight with the German defenders. And here, at the left hand end of the huge semicircle, was the lighthouse, which he was now rounding as *Thetis*, *Intrepid*, and *Iphigenia* rounded it on that historic night. He tried to picture the scene. The screen of smoke to sea, which baffled the searchlights of the defenders and from which mysterious and unexpected craft emerged at intervals, the flashing lights as guns were fired and shells burst over the mole, the sea, and the low-lying sand dunes of the coast behind. The din of hell in the air, fire, smoke, explosion, and death﻿—and those three ships passing on; *Thetis* a wreck, struck and fiercely burning, forced aside by the destruction of her gear, but lighting her fellows straight to their goal﻿—the mouth of the canal which led to the submarine base at Bruges. French crossed the deck and gazed at the spot with its swing bridge and stone side walls, as he thought how, had the desperate venture failed, history might have been changed and at that touch and go period of the war the Central Powers might have triumphed. It was with renewed pride and wonder in the men who conceived and carried out the wonderful enterprise that he crossed back over the deck and set himself to the business of landing.

===Chinese===
船 10:30 离开帕克斯顿码头（Parkeston Quay），第二天早晨 6 点，弗伦奇已经站在甲板上了。他生怕错过泽布吕赫渐渐驶近时可能望见的任何一景。那桩也许是有史以来最伟大的海上壮举，他早怀着屏息动魄的心情读过它的始末——说实在的，谁又没读过呢？——当那道著名的防波堤又长又低的轮廓，在正前方偏右一点的方向上隐隐浮现时，他的心跳快了，喉咙里也哽了一下。那边，靠右远处，绕着长堤的弧线，想必就是“温迪克蒂夫”号（Vindictive）靠帮登岸之处，就是在一片烈火地狱之中，她的船员用攀墙梯够上大防波堤顶、再翻到里面下去、同德国守军短兵相接之处。而这边，在巨大半圆的左端，是那座灯塔——此刻他正绕着它转弯，一如在那个历史性的夜晚“忒提斯”号（Thetis）、“因特雷皮德”号（Intrepid）和“伊菲革涅亚”号（Iphigenia）绕过它一样。他竭力想象当时的景象：海面上那道烟幕，叫守军的探照灯无从捉摸，又不时有神秘的、出人意料的舰艇从烟里钻出来；炮口火光闪烁，炮弹在防波堤上、海面上、还有身后海岸低低的沙丘上炸开；半空中地狱般的喧嚣，火、烟、爆炸和死亡——而那三艘船继续前行；“忒提斯”号已成残骸，中弹后燃起熊熊大火，操纵装置被毁，被挤偏到一旁，却仍为她的伙伴们引路，直指目标——那便是通往布鲁日潜艇基地的运河口。弗伦奇横过甲板，凝望那处地方——带着活动桥和石砌的堤壁，心想：倘若那场孤注一掷的行动失利，历史或许已然改写；在大战那个千钧一发的关头，同盟国（Central Powers）说不定已告得手。他重新怀着对那些构想并完成这项壮举的人们的骄傲与惊叹走回甲板另一侧，着手办登陆的事。

===Original===
A short run past the sandhills at the coast and across the flat Belgian fields brought the spires of Bruges into view, and slowly rounding a sharp curve through the gardens of the houses in the suburbs, they joined the main line from Ostend, and a few minutes later entered the station. Emerging on to the wide boulevard in front, French’s eyes fell on a bus bearing the legend “Grand Hôtel du Sablon,” and getting in, he was driven across the boulevard and a short way up a long, rather narrow and winding street, between houses some of which seemed to have stood unaltered﻿—and doubtless had﻿—for six hundred years, when Bruges, three times its present size, was the chief trading city of the Hanseatic League. As he turned into the hotel, chimes rang out﻿—from the famous belfry, the porter told him﻿—tinkling, high-pitched bells and silvery, if a trifle thin in the clear morning air.

===Chinese===
列车驶过海岸的沙丘，穿过比利时平坦的田野，跑不多时，布鲁日的塔尖便映入眼帘；缓缓拐过一道急弯，穿过城郊一座座宅子的花园，列车并入奥斯坦德开来的干线，几分钟后进了站。走出站来，到了前面宽阔的林荫大道上，弗伦奇一眼瞥见一辆公共汽车，车身上标着“Grand Hôtel du Sablon”的字样；他上了车，被载着穿过林荫大道，又沿一条狭长而颇多曲折的街道走了一小段，两旁的房屋，有几座看去仿佛六百年一直原封未动——多半也确实如此——那时布鲁日的规模是如今的三倍，是汉萨同盟（Hanseatic League）首屈一指的商城。他转身走进旅馆时，一阵钟乐荡漾开来——据搬运工告诉他，出自那座著名的钟楼——叮叮咚咚，高亢而带银铃之色，在清朗的晨空中只略嫌单薄。

===Original===
He called for some breakfast, and as he was consuming it the anticipated delights of sightseeing receded, and interest in the movements of James Dangle became once more paramount. He was proud of his solution of the problem of the torn hotel bill, and not for a moment had a doubt of the correctness of that solution entered his head.

===Chinese===
他叫了早点，吃着吃着，料想中的观光乐趣便退了下去，对詹姆斯·丹格尔行踪的关切重新占了上风。他对那张撕碎的旅馆账单之谜的解答很是得意，脑子里一刻也不曾闪过这解答可能有误的念头。

===Original===
It came upon him therefore as a devastating shock when the courteous manager of the hotel, with whom he had asked an interview, assured him not only that no such person as the original of the photograph he had presented had ever visited his establishment, but that the fragment of the bill was not his.

===Chinese===
因此，当他求见的那位彬彬有礼的旅馆经理向他保证——不但他出示的照片上的那个人从未光顾过本店，连那片账单残纸也不是本店的——这对他无异晴天霹雳。

===Original===
To French it seemed as if the bottom had fallen out of his world. He had been so sure of his ground; all his reasoning about the stamp, the size of the hotel and town and lengths of their names had seemed so convincing and unassailable. And the names Grand Hôtel du Sablon and Bruges had worked in so well! More important still, no other hotel seemed to fill the bill. French felt cast down to the lowest depths of despair, and for a time he could only stare speechlessly at the manager.

===Chinese===
在弗伦奇，只觉得天塌地陷。他原是那样有把握；什么印章啦，旅馆与城镇的规模、名字的长短啦，这一套推理曾显得那样令人信服、无懈可击。何况“Grand Hôtel du Sablon”与布鲁日这两个名字又扣得那样严丝合缝！更要紧的是，除它之外，再没有一家旅馆对得上号。弗伦奇一败涂地，跌到了绝望的谷底，有一阵子只能一言不发地干瞪着经理。

===Original===
At last he smiled rather ruefully.

===Chinese===
末了，他苦笑了一下。

===Original===
“That’s rather a blow,” he confessed. “I was pretty sure of my ground. Indeed, so sure was I, that if I might without offense, I should like to ask you again if there is no possibility that the man might have been here, say, during your absence.”

===Chinese===
“这可真是当头一棒，”他坦白道。“我原是相当有把握的。实在有把握得很，所以恕我冒昧，想再问您一遍：有没有这种可能——比如在您不在店里的日子里，那人到过这里？”

===Original===
The manager was sympathetic. He brought French a sample of his bill, stamped with his rubber receipt stamp, and French saw at once their dissimilarity with those he had been studying. Moreover, the manager assured him that neither had been altered for several years.

===Chinese===
经理很表同情。他拿了一张本店账单的样式给弗伦奇看，上面盖着那枚橡皮收款章，弗伦奇一眼便看出，它同自己反复研究过的那两个印记毫不相似。经理还向他保证，账单样式和印章都已好几年没有改动过了。

===Original===
So he was no further on! French lit a cigar, and retiring to a deserted corner of the salon, sat down to think the thing out.

===Chinese===
这么说他毫无进展！弗伦奇点上一支雪茄，退到大厅一个无人的角落里坐下，把这桩事从头想个透。

===Original===
What was he to do next? Was he to return to London by the next boat, giving up the search and admitting defeat, or was there any possible alternative? He set his teeth as he swore great oaths that nothing short of the direct need would lead him to abandon his efforts until he had found the hotel, and learned Dangle’s secret.

===Chinese===
下一步怎么办？是搭下一班船回伦敦，就此撒手、认输服输，还是另有什么路可走？他咬紧牙关，狠狠起誓：非到万不得已，绝不善罢甘休，定要找到那家旅馆、探出丹格尔的秘密不可。

===Original===
But heroics were all very well: what, in point of fact was he to do? He sat considering the problem for an hour, and at the end of that time he had decided to go to Brussels, borrow or buy a Belgian hotel guide, and go through it page by page until he found what he wanted. If none of the hotels given suited, he would go on to Paris and try a similar experiment.

===Chinese===
可是豪言壮语归豪言壮语：实际上到底该干什么？他坐着把这问题盘算了一个钟头，最后打定主意：去布鲁塞尔，借或买一本比利时旅馆指南来，一页一页翻下去，直到翻出所要的东西为止。倘若所列旅馆没有一家对得上，就再赴巴黎，如法炮制一遍。

===Original===
This decision he reached only after long consideration, not because it was not obvious﻿—it had instantly occurred to him﻿—but because he was convinced that the methods he had already tried had completely covered the ground. He had proved that there was no hotel whose name ended in “… lon” in a fair-sized town whose name ended in “… s” in all the district in question, other than the Grand Hôtel du Sablon at Bruges. There still remained, however, the chance that it might be a southern French or Swiss hotel, and he saw that he would have to make sure of this before returning to London.

===Chinese===
这个决定他斟酌了许久才拿下，倒不是因为它不够现成——他起初便想到了——而是因为他认定，已经试过的那些法子早把地面搜了个遍。他证明了：在整个相关地区，凡名字以“s”收尾的像样城镇里，除布鲁日的萨布隆大旅馆之外，再无一家旅馆名字以“lon”收尾。不过，那账单出自法国南部或瑞士某家旅馆的可能依然存在，他明白，回伦敦之前，非得把这一点弄确实不可。

===Original===
Still buried in thought, he walked slowly back to the station to look up trains to Brussels. The fact that he was in the most interesting town in Belgium no longer stirred his pulse. His disappointment and anxiety about his case drove all irrelevant matters from his mind, and he felt that all he wanted now was to be at work again to retrieve his error.

===Chinese===
他仍旧埋在思绪里，慢慢踱回车站去查往布鲁塞尔的车次。人在比利时最有趣味的城里，这一层已不再使他的心跳加速。案子带给他的失望与焦灼，把一切不相干的事都逐出了脑海；他只觉得，如今一心想要的，就是重新干起来，挽回自己的失误。

===Original===
He reached the station, and began searching the huge timetable boards for the train he wanted. He was interested to notice that the tables were published in two languages, French and what he thought at first was Dutch, but concluded later must be Flemish. Idly he compared the different spelling of the names of the towns. Brugge and Bruges, Gent and Gand, Brussel and Bruxelles, Oostende and Ostende, and then suddenly he came up as it were all standing, and a sudden wave of excitement passed over him as he stood regarding another pair of names. Antwerpen and Anvers! Anvers! A six lettered town ending in *s*! He cursed himself for his stupidity. He had always thought of the place as Antwerp, but he ought to have known its French name. Anvers! Once more he was alert and full of eager optimism. Had he got it at last?

===Chinese===
到了车站，他开始在巨大的时刻表牌上寻找所要的车次。他饶有兴味地发现，这些表用两种文字刊行：法文，还有一种他起初当作荷兰文、后来断定必是弗拉芒文的文字。他闲着无事，便比较起城镇名字的不同拼法来：Brugge 与 Bruges，Gent 与 Gand，Brussel 与 Bruxelles，Oostende 与 Ostende——蓦地，他仿佛猛然惊醒，整个人钉在原地，望着另一对名字，一阵兴奋涌遍全身。Antwerpen 与 Anvers！Anvers（昂韦尔）！一个以“s”结尾的六字母城镇！他把自己骂了个狗血淋头：他一直管那地方叫 Antwerp（安特卫普），可他本该知道它的法语名字。Anvers！他重新抖擞起来，满心热切的乐观。这一回总该是它了吧？

===Original===
He passed through on to the platform, and making for a door headed “Chef de Gare,” asked for the stationmaster. There, after a moment’s delay, he was shown into the presence of an imposing individual in gold lace, who, however, was not too important to listen to him carefully and reply courteously in somewhat halting English. Monsieur wished to know if there was an hotel whose name ended in “… lon” in Antwerp? He could not recall one off hand, but he would look up the advertisements in his guides and tourist programs. Ah, what was this? The Grand Hôtel du Carillon. Was that what monsieur required?

===Chinese===
他穿进站台，朝一扇写着“Chef de Gare”（站长室）的门走去，求见站长。稍待片刻，他便被引到一位满身金绦、气派堂堂的人物面前；不过此人架子虽大，倒还肯仔细听他把话讲完，并用相当结巴的英语客气作答。这位先生是想打听，安特卫普有没有一家旅馆，名字以“lon”结尾？他一时想不起来，但他可以查查指南和旅游册子上的广告。啊，这是什么？Grand Hôtel du Carillon——卡里永大旅馆。这是不是先生要找的？

===Original===
A name of twenty letters﻿—which would exactly fill the space on the receipt stamp! It certainly was what monsieur required! The very idea raised monsieur to an exalted pitch of delighted enthusiasm. The stationmaster was gratified at the reception of his information.

===Chinese===
二十个字母的名字——不多不少，恰好填满收款章上的空当！这正是先生要找的！单这一个念头，就把这位先生拔到了欢欣鼓舞、意气飞扬的九霄之上。站长见自己报出的消息如此受欢迎，心里也很受用。

===Original===
“I haf been at the ’otel myself,” he volunteered. “It is small, but vair’ goot. It is in the Place Verte, near to the Cathedral. Does monsieur know Antwerp?”

===Chinese===
“那家旅馆我自家就去过，”他主动搭话。“店不大，可是好得很、好得很。就在绿色广场（Place Verte）上，紧挨着大教堂。先生到过安特卫普没有？”

===Original===
Monsieur did not, but he expressed the pleasure it would give him to make its acquaintance, and thanking the polite official he returned to the timetables to look up the trains thither.

===Chinese===
先生不曾到过，但他表示，得有机会结识此城，实为乐事。他谢过这位客气的官员，回到时刻表前，去查开往那边的火车。

===Original===
His most direct way, it appeared, was through Ghent and Termonde, but on working out the services he found he could get quicker trains via Brussels. He therefore booked by that route, and at 11:51 he climbed into a great through express from Ostend to Brussels, Aix-la-Chapelle, Strasbourg, and, it seemed to him, the whole of the rest of Europe. An hour and a half’s run brought him into Brussels-Nord, and from there he wandered out into the Place Rogier for lunch. Then returning to the station he took an express for Antwerp, arriving in the central terminus of that city a few minutes after three o’clock.

===Chinese===
一算下来，最直捷的路该取道根特和泰尔蒙德（Termonde），可把班次一核，他发现取道布鲁塞尔车更快。于是他便按这条线买了票，11:51，他登上一列由奥斯坦德开来的大型直达快车——直通布鲁塞尔、亚琛（Aix-la-Chapelle）、斯特拉斯堡（Strasbourg），而且在他看来，简直直通欧洲其余的全部。一个半小时后，车抵布鲁塞尔北站（Brussels-Nord），他信步走到站外的罗日耶广场（Place Rogier）吃午饭。然后回站，搭上一班开往安特卫普的快车，3 点过几分，到了该市的中央总站。

===Original===
He had bought a map of Antwerp at a bookstall in Brussels, from which he had learned that the Place Verte was nearly a mile away in the direction of the river. His traveling impedimenta consisting of a handbag only, he determined to walk, and emerging from the great marble hall of the station, he passed down the busy Avenue de Keyser, and along the Place de Meir into the older part of the town. As he walked he was immensely impressed by the fine wide streets, the ornate buildings, and the excellence of the shops. Everywhere were evidences of wealth and prosperity, and as he turned into the Place Verte, and looked across at the huge bulk of the Cathedral with its soaring spire, he felt that here was an artistic treasure of which any city might well be proud.

===Chinese===
在布鲁塞尔的书报摊上，他买了一张安特卫普地图，从图上得知绿色广场在朝大河的方向、将近一英里之外。他的行装不过一只手提包，便决意步行。出了车站宏大的大理石厅堂，他沿着热闹的凯塞尔大道（Avenue de Keyser），再经梅尔大街（Place de Meir），走进老城。一路行去，那宽阔漂亮的街道、华美的楼宇、讲究的铺面，都令他印象至深。处处显露着富足兴旺；待他转进绿色广场，望见对面大教堂巍峨的身量和直插云霄的尖塔，他只觉得：这里珍藏的艺术之宝，随便哪座城市都尽可引以为荣。

===Original===
The Grand Hôtel du Carillon was an old, quaint looking building looking out over the Place Verte. French, entering, called for a bock in the restaurant, and after he had finished, asked to see the manager. A moment later a small, stout man with a humorous eye appeared, bowed low, and said that he was M. Marquet, the proprietor.

===Chinese===
卡里永大旅馆是座古雅别致的老房子，面向绿色广场。弗伦奇进门，在餐厅里叫了一杯博克（bock）啤酒，喝完之后求见经理。不一刻，出来一个矮矮胖胖、眼睛带着诙谐神气的男人，深深鞠了一躬，自称是店主人马尔凯先生（M. Marquet）。

===Original===
“A word with you in private, M. Marquet,” French requested, when they had exchanged confidences on the weather. “Won’t you take something with me?”

===Chinese===
两人先就天气寒暄了一阵，随后弗伦奇才开口：“马尔凯先生，想同您私下讲句话。赏光陪我喝点什么好不好？”

===Original===
The proprietor signified his willingness in excellent English, and when further drinks had been brought, and French had satisfied himself that they were alone, he went on:

===Chinese===
店主人用一口极好的英语表示遵命。酒又端上来，弗伦奇也确知四下无人，这才接着说：

===Original===
“I am a detective officer from the London police, and I am trying to trace an Englishman called Dangle. I have reason to suppose he stayed at this hotel recently. There is his photograph. Can you help me at all?”

===Chinese===
“我是伦敦警察厅的侦探警官，正在追查一个名叫丹格尔的英国人。我有理由认为，他近来到过贵旅馆。这是他的照片。您能帮上点忙吗？”

===Original===
At the name Dangle, M. Marquet had nodded, and when he saw the photograph he beamed and his whole body became affirmation personified. But certainly, he knew M. Dangle. For several weeks﻿—he could not say how many, but he could ascertain from his records﻿—for several weeks M. Dangle had been his guest at intervals. Sometimes he had stayed one night, sometimes two, sometimes three. Yes, he was usually alone, but not always. On three or four occasions he had been accompanied by another gentleman﻿—a tall, well-built, clean-shaven man, and once a third man had come, a short man with a fair mustache. Yes, that was the photograph of the short man, M.﻿—? Yes; Blessington. The other man’s name he could not remember, but it would appear in the register: Sile, Site﻿—something like that. Yes, Sime: that was it. No, he was afraid he knew nothing about these gentlemen or their business, but he would be glad to do everything in his power to assist monsieur.

===Chinese===
一听丹格尔这名字，马尔凯先生便点头；待看到照片，他笑逐颜开，整个人简直成了“千真万确”的化身。当然，他认得丹格尔先生。好几个星期——说不上究竟几个星期，不过他可以从簿册上查实——好几个星期以来，丹格尔先生隔三差五便是他的客人。有时住一夜，有时两夜，有时三夜。对，他通常独自一人，但不总是。有三四次，有另一位先生陪他同来——高个子，体格结实，脸刮得精光；还有一回来了第三个人，矮个子，留一撇浅色胡子。对，这正是那位矮个子的照片，他叫——？对了：布莱辛顿。另一位先生的名字他记不起来了，不过登记簿上查得到：Sile、Site——诸如此类的拼法。啊，是了，赛姆：就是这个。至于这些先生的来历营生，他抱歉实在一无所知，但凡他力所能及，无不愿意为先生效劳。

===Original===
French, his enthusiasm and delight remaining at fever heat, was suitably grateful. He wished just to ask M. Marquet a few more questions. He would like to know the last occasion on which M. Dangle had stayed.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇的热忱与快意仍沸腾未减，便得体地道了谢。他只想再问马尔凯先生几个问题。他想了解，丹格尔先生最后一次住店是在什么时候。

===Original===
“Why,” M. Marquet exclaimed, “he just left yesterday. He came here, let me see, on Tuesday night quite late, indeed it was nearly one on Wednesday morning when he arrived. He came, he said, off the English boat train which arrives here about midnight. He stayed here two days﻿—till yesterday, Thursday. He left yesterday shortly after *déjeuner*.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，”马尔凯先生叫起来，“他昨天刚走！他来的那天，让我想想，是星期二夜里，来得极晚——其实到店已是星期三凌晨快 1 点了。他说，他是坐英国船班联运车来的，那车大约午夜到站。他在这儿住了两天——住到昨天，星期四。昨天*déjeuner*（法语：午餐）之后不久，他就动身走了。”

===Original===
“He was alone?”

===Chinese===
“他一个人？”

===Original===
“Yes, monsieur. This time he was alone.”

===Chinese===
“是的，先生。这一回他是一个人。”

===Original===
French, metaphorically speaking, hugged himself on hearing this news. Through his brilliant work with the torn bill, he had added one more fine achievement to the long list of his successes. He could not but believe that the most doubtful and difficult step of the investigation had now been accomplished. With a trail only twenty four hours old, he should surely be able to put his hands on Dangle with but little delay. Moreover, from the fact that so many visits had been paid to Antwerp it looked as if the secret of the gang was hidden in the city. Greatly reassured, he proceeded to acquire details.

===Chinese===
打个比方说，弗伦奇听了这消息，简直想搂住自己美一番。凭着那张撕碎账单上的一番漂亮功夫，他又在自己长长的功劳簿上添了漂亮的一笔。他不能不相信，本案最没把握、最棘手的一步，如今已经迈了过去。线索距今不过二十四小时，他要擒获丹格尔，想来不必再费多少周折。再者，他们到安特卫普来了这么许多趟，可见团伙的秘密就藏在这座城里。他大为宽心，便着手收集细节。

===Original===
He began by obtaining from M. Marquet’s records lists of the visits of the three men, and that gentleman’s identification of the torn bill. Also he pressed him as to whether he could not remember any questions or conversations of the trio which might give him a hint as to their business, but without success. He saw and made a detailed search of the room Dangle had occupied during his last visit, but here again with no result. Dangle, M. Marquet said, had been out all day on the Wednesday, the day after his arrival, but on Thursday he had remained in the hotel until his departure about 2:00 p.m. M. Marquet had not seen him leave, but he had sent the waiter for his bill after *déjeuner*, and the proprietor believed he had gone a little later. Possibly the porter could give more information on the point.

===Chinese===
他先从马尔凯先生的簿册里抄下三人历次来店的日期清单，又请这位先生对那片撕碎的账单作了指认。他还追问：那三个人可曾有过什么言谈问答，能让店主对他们的事务窥得一点端倪——终归徒劳。丹格尔末次住宿的房间，他亲自去看，细细搜了一遍，也是一无所获。据马尔凯先生说，丹格尔星期三——到店的次日——一整天都在外面；星期四则一直待在旅馆里，到下午 2:00 左右才离店。马尔凯先生没亲眼看见他走，但*déjeuner*之后他已派侍者去结账，店主人想，他走的时候稍晚一点。这一点，搬运工或许能提供更多情况。

===Original===
The porter was sent for and questioned. He knew M. Dangle well and recognized his photograph. He had been present in the hall when the gentleman left on the previous day, shortly before two o’clock. M. Dangle had walked out of the hotel with his suitcase in his hand, declining the porter’s offer to carry it for him or call a taxi. The trams, however, passed the door, and the porter had assumed M. Dangle intended to travel by that means. No, he had not noticed the direction he took. There was a “stillstand” or tramway halt close by. Dangle had not talked to the porter further than to wish him good day when he met him. He had not asked questions, or given any hint of his business in the town.

===Chinese===
搬运工被叫来问过了。他同丹格尔先生很熟，一眼认出照片。前一天 2 点差几分那位先生离店时，他正在门厅里。丹格尔先生手提旅行箱，自己走出旅馆，谢绝了搬运工替他提箱或叫车的殷勤。不过电车就打门前经过，搬运工猜想丹格尔先生打算乘车代步。方向呢，他可没留意。近旁有一处“stillstand”（弗拉芒语：电车站）。丹格尔同搬运工再没说过什么，遇见时道声日安而已。他没有打听什么，也没有露出半点他在城里营生的口风。

===Original===
Following his usual procedure under such circumstances, French next asked for interviews with all those of the staff who had come in any way in contract with his quarry, but in spite of his most persistent efforts he could not extract a single item of information as to the man’s business or movements.

===Chinese===
照他处在这种情况下的一贯做法，弗伦奇接着要求逐一约见店员中凡同他追寻的猎物有过任何接触的人，可任凭他锲而不舍，也未能从这些人嘴里抠出关于此人来历行踪的一星半点。

===Original===
Baffled and weary from his journey, French took his hat and went out in the hope that a walk through the streets of the fine old city would clear his brain and bring him the inspiration he needed. Crossing beneath the trees of the Place Verte, he passed round the cathedral to the small square from which he could look up at the huge bulk of the west front, with its two unequal towers, one a climbing marvel of decoration, “lace in stone,” the other unfinished, and topped with a small and evidently temporary spire. Then, promising himself a look round the interior before leaving the town, he regained the tramline from the Place Verte, and following it westwards, in two or three minutes came out on the great terraces lining the banks of the river.

===Chinese===
碰了壁，又添了旅途的劳顿，弗伦奇取了帽子走出去，指望在这座美妙老城的街道上走一走，能清醒清醒头脑，带来他所需要的灵感。他从绿色广场的树下穿过，绕过大教堂，走到一个小广场上，从那里仰望大教堂西立面巍然的躯体和两座高低不等的塔：一座满身装饰、层层而上，堪称奇观，是所谓“石上花边”；另一座尚未完工，顶上安着一个小小的、一望而知是临时凑合的尖顶。他暗自许诺，离城之前要到里面转一圈看看；随后回到绿色广场的电车线旁，沿着它向西走，两三分钟后便来到了沿河两岸的高大平台上。

===Original===
The first sight of the Scheldt was one which French felt he would not soon forget. Well on to half a mile wide, it bore away in both directions like a great highway leading from this little Belgium to the uttermost parts of the earth. Large ships lay at anchor in it, as well as clustering along the wharves to the south. This river frontage of wharves and sheds and cranes and great steamers extended as far as the eye could reach; he had read that it was three and a half miles long. And that excluded the huge docks for which the town was famous. As he strolled along he became profoundly impressed, not only with the size of the place, but more particularly with the attention which had been given to its artistic side. In spite of all this commercial activity the city did not look sordid. Thought had been given to its design; one might almost say loving care. Why, these very terraces on which he was walking, with their cafés and their splendid view of the river, were formed on neither more nor less than the vast roofs of the dock sheds. French, who knew most of the English ports, felt his amazement grow at every step.

===Chinese===
初见斯海尔德河（Scheldt），弗伦奇便觉得此景终身难忘。河面宽阔得将近半英里，向两侧铺展开去，宛如一条大干道，从这个小小的比利时通往天涯海角。河中泊着大船，一艘挨一艘，还有许多簇拥在南边的码头旁。这带由码头、货栈、吊车和大轮船组成的河岸线，一直延伸到目力尽头；他读到过，它长三英里半。这还不算那令此城闻名的巨大船坞群。他一路闲逛，印象愈来愈深：不单是这地方的规模，更其是人们对它艺术一面的用心。尽管商业活动如此繁盛，这座城市却不见半点鄙俗气。它的规划是动过脑筋的，几乎可以说倾注了痴爱。喏，他此刻走过的这些平台，连同那些咖啡馆和望河的绝佳景致，恰恰就筑在码头货栈的巨大屋顶上，不多也不少。弗伦奇认得英国的大多数港口，他只觉得步步都有新的惊叹。

===Original===
He followed the quays right across the town till he came to the Gare du Sud, then turning away from the river, he found himself in the Avenue du Sud. From this he worked back along the line of great avenues which had replaced the earlier fortifications, until eventually, nearly three hours after he had started, he once again turned into the Place Verte, and reached the Carillon.

===Chinese===
他沿着码头穿过全城，一直走到南站（Gare du Sud），然后背河而行，来到南大道（Avenue du Sud）。由此他沿着一条条大街折返回去——这些大街取代了早年的城防工事——走了将近三个钟头，终于又转进绿色广场，回到了卡里永旅馆。

===Original===
He ordered a room for the night, and some strong tea, after which he sat on in his secluded corner of the comfortable restaurant, and smoked a meditative cigar. His walk had done him good. His brain had cleared, and the weariness of the journey, and the chagrin of his deadlock had vanished. His thoughts returned to his problem, which he began to attack in the new.

===Chinese===
他订下过夜的房间，又要了些浓茶，随后在舒适餐厅里他那僻静的角落坐着不动，抽起一支沉思的雪茄。这一趟走下来对他大有好处。脑子清爽了，旅途的困顿、僵局带来的懊丧，都消散了。他的思绪回到那个难题上，从头开始重新进攻。

===Original===
He puzzled over it for the best part of an hour, without making the slightest progress, and then he began to consider how far the ideas he had already arrived at fitted in with what he had since learned of Dangle’s movements.

===Chinese===
他苦思了将近一个钟头，没有丝毫进展，这才转而掂量：他先前已形成的那些想法，同他后来获悉的丹格尔行踪，到底合得上几成。

===Original===
He had thought that the nature of the articles on Dangle’s list suggested a sea expedition. He remembered the delight with which, many years earlier, he had read *The Riddle of the Sands*, and he thought that had Dangle contemplated just such another cruise as that of the heroes of that fascinating book, he might well have got together the articles in question. But since these ideas had passed through his mind, French had learned the following fresh facts:

===Chinese===
他原本认为，丹格尔单子上那些物件的性质，指向的是一次海上远行。他记得多年以前读《沙洲之谜》（The Riddle of the Sands）时那份津津有味，心想：倘若丹格尔筹划的正是那本迷人的书里主人公们那一路的巡航，那么他把这几样东西凑到一处，就再顺理成章不过了。不过，自打这些念头掠过脑海，弗伦奇又获悉了以下几桩新事实：

===Original===
1. From a fortnight after obtaining the tracing, Dangle had been paying frequent visits to Antwerp.

===Chinese===
1. 自获得描图两个星期之后起，丹格尔频频到访安特卫普。

===Original===
2. He had on these occasions put up at the Carillon.

===Chinese===
2. 他这些趟都下榻卡里永旅馆。

===Original===
3. His last visit had followed immediately on the failure to murder Cheyne, with its almost certain result of the calling in of Scotland Yard.

===Chinese===
3. 他这最后一次来，紧接在谋杀切恩未能得手之后——那一着几乎注定要招来苏格兰场过问。

===Original===
4. He had on this last visit remained at the Carillon for two days, leaving about 2:00 p.m. on the Thursday, the previous day.

===Chinese===
4. 末次来访，他在卡里永旅馆盘桓两天，星期四——也就是昨天——下午 2:00 前后离店。

===Original===
5. He had carried his handbag from the hotel, without calling for a taxi.

===Chinese===
5. 他手提旅行袋步行离开旅馆，没有叫车。

===Original===
At first French could not see that these additional facts had any bearing on his theory, but as he continued turning them over in his mind, he realized that all but one might be interpreted as tending in the same direction.

===Chinese===
起初弗伦奇看不出这些补充事实同他的推想有什么相干，但在脑子里反复掂量之后，他意识到：除一条之外，其余各条都不妨解作朝着同一个方向。

===Original===
1. Dangle’s visits to Antwerp. Supposing Dangle had been planning some secret marine expedition, where, French asked himself, could he have found a more suitable base from which to make his arrangements? Antwerp was a seaport: moreover, it was a great seaport, large enough for a secret expedition to set sail from without attracting notice. It was a foreign port, away from the inquisitive notice of the British police, but, on the other hand, it was the nearest great port to London. If these considerations did not back up his theory, they at least did not conflict with it.

===Chinese===
1. 丹格尔之屡访安特卫普。设若丹格尔筹划的正是某次秘密出海远征，弗伦奇问自己：哪里还能找到比这更合适的根据地来作布置？安特卫普是海港，而且是大海港，大到一支秘密远征队从这儿启航也不至于惹眼。它是外国港口，躲开了英国警察爱打听的耳目；而另一方面，它又是离伦敦最近的大港。这些考虑纵然算不上给他推想添了佐证，至少也不与之相悖。

===Original===
2. Why had Dangle put up at the Carillon? The hotels near the station were the obvious ones for English visitors. Could it be because the Place Verte was close to the river and the shipping? This, French admitted to himself, sounded farfetched, and yet it might be the truth.

===Chinese===
2. 丹格尔为何偏偏下榻卡里永旅馆？英国客人照理该住车站附近的旅馆。会不会就因为绿色广场挨着大河、挨着船舶？这一点，弗伦奇自己也承认听着牵强，然而说不定正是实情。

===Original===
3. The dispersal and disappearance of the gang immediately on the probability of its activities becoming known to the police looked suspiciously like a flight.

===Chinese===
3. 团伙在其行迹很可能败露给警方之后，随即星散匿迹，那模样可疑得很，活脱脱是一场逃亡。

===Original===
4. Could it be that Dangle’s arrival in Antwerp was ahead of schedule, that is, the flight brought him there two days before the expedition was to start? Or could it be that on his arrival he immediately set to work to organize the departure, but was unable to complete his arrangements for two days? At least, it might be so.

===Chinese===
4. 会不会是丹格尔到安特卫普到得比原计划早，也就是说，这场逃亡提前两天把他送到，而远征尚未启航？又或者，他一到就立刻着手安排启程，只是花了两天工夫才把各项布置办妥？至少，有此可能。

===Original===
Lastly, had he carried his bag from the hotel for the same reason as he might have chosen the hotel: that he was going, not to the station, but the few hundred yards to the quays, thence to start on this maritime expedition? Again, it might be so.

===Chinese===
末了，他提着提袋走出旅馆，会不会同他挑中这家旅馆是同一个缘故：他要去的地方不是车站，而是几百码外的码头，从那儿踏上这次海上远征？同样，有此可能。

===Original===
French was fully aware that the whole of these elaborate considerations had the actual stability of a house of cards. Each and every one of his deductions might be erroneous and the facts might be capable of an entirely different construction. Still, there was at least a suggestion that Dangle might have left Antwerp by water shortly after two o’clock on the previous day. It was the one constructive idea French could evolve, and he decided that in the absence of anything better he would try to follow it up.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇心里明白，这一整套煞费苦心的推敲，根基之稳当，实则同纸牌搭的屋子相差无几。他的每一条推论都未必靠得住，那些事实也完全可以作另一种全然不同的解释。不过，至少有这么一点苗头：丹格尔可能是前一天 2 点刚过，便由水路离开了安特卫普。这是弗伦奇所能想出的唯一一条建设性主意；他打定主意，在没有更好的办法之前，就循着这条线追下去。

===Original===
It was too late to do anything that night. After dinner, therefore, he had another walk, spent an hour in a cinema, and then went early to bed, so as to be fresh for his labors of the following day.

===Chinese===
这天夜里再干什么都太迟了。于是晚饭后他又散步一阵，在电影院里消磨了一个钟头，便早早上了床，好养足精神，应付明天的操劳。

## XVII On the Flood Tide / 十七 涨潮

===Original===
French was astir betimes next morning, and over his coffee and rolls and honey he laid his plans for the day. As to the next step of his investigation he had no doubt. He must begin by finding out what vessels had left the city after 2:00 p.m. on the previous Thursday. That done, he could go into the question of the passengers each carried, in the hope of learning that Dangle was among them.

===Chinese===
第二天一早弗伦奇就起身了，就着咖啡、面包卷和蜂蜜，把这一天的计划定下来。调查下一步该怎么走，他心里毫无疑虑：先得查清上星期四下午 2:00 之后有哪些船只离了港。这一步办完，再去查每条船各载了哪些乘客，指望能查出丹格尔名列其中。

===Original===
At the outset he was faced by the handicap of being a stranger in a strange land. If Antwerp had been an English port he would have known just where to get his information, but here he was unfamiliar with the ropes. He did not know if all sailings were published in any paper or available to the public at any office; moreover, his ignorance of both French and Flemish precluded his mixing with clerks or dock loafers from whom he might pick up information. Of course there were the Belgian police, but he did not wish to apply to them if he could carry out his job by himself.

===Chinese===
一开头他就遇上一重障碍：人地生疏。安特卫普若是座英国港口，他准知道该上哪儿去打听；可在这里，个中门道他一概不熟。他不知道船期是否都登在什么报纸上，也不知道有什么办公处可供公众查询；再说，他法语、佛兰芒语一窍不通，也就没法混到职员或码头闲汉中间去捡消息。当然还有比利时警方，可只要自己办得成这差事，他并不想去求他们。

===Original===
However, this part of his problem proved easier of solution than he had expected. Inquiries at the post office revealed the fact that there was a shipping agency in the Rue des Tanneurs, and soon he had reached the place, found a clerk who spoke English, and put his question.

===Chinese===
不过，问题的这一部分解起来倒比他预想的容易。在邮局一打听，得知制革匠街（Rue des Tanneurs）有一家航运代理行，不多时他便寻到了那里，找到一位会说英语的职员，把问题提了出来。

===Original===
When French wished to be suave, as he usually did, he could, so to speak, have wheedled his best bone from a bulldog. Now, explaining in a friendly and confidential manner who he was and why he wanted the information, he begged the other’s good offices. The clerk, flattered at being thus courteously approached, showed a willingness to assist, with the result that in ten minutes French had the particulars he needed.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇素来待人和气，真要施展柔功的时候，可以说连斗牛犬嘴边的骨头都哄得下来。此刻他亲亲热热、推心置腹地说明了自己是谁、为什么要打听这些消息，恳请对方从中帮忙。那职员受此礼遇，心里受用，欣然愿助一臂之力，结果不出十分钟，弗伦奇便把所需的详情都弄到了手。

===Original===
He turned into a café, and calling for a bock, sat down to consider what he had learned. And of this the very first fact filled him with delight, as it seemed to fit in with the theory he had evolved.

===Chinese===
他拐进一家咖啡馆，要了一杯博克啤酒（bock），坐下来琢磨刚才听来的情况。其中头一件事实就叫他满心欢喜，因为它似乎正合他推演出来的那套理论的路子。

===Original===
On Thursday it had been high water at 2:30 p.m. By 2:30 the dock gates had been opened, and it appeared that, taking advantage of this, several steamers had left shortly after that hour.

===Chinese===
星期四那天下午 2:30 满潮。到 2:30 船闸已然开启，看样子有几艘轮船正是趁这个时机，在那之后不久离的港。

===Original===
This was distinctly encouraging, and French turned to the list of ships with a growing hope that the end of his investigation might be coming into sight. In all, eleven steamers had left the port on the day in question, between the hours of 2:00 and 6:00 p.m., the period he had included in his inquiry.

===Chinese===
这情形着实鼓舞人心。弗伦奇转而研究那份船舶清单，心里越来越存着指望：这桩调查的尽头，也许已经遥遥在望。在他查询的时段——那天下午 2:00 到 6:00 之间——离港的轮船共计十一艘。

===Original===
There was first of all a Canadian Pacific liner, which had sailed from the quays at 3:00 p.m., and at 3:30 a small passenger boat had left for Oslo and Bergen. The remaining boats were tramps. There were four coasters, two for Newcastle, one for Goole, and one for Belfast, a 6,000 tonner for Singapore and the Dutch Islands, another slightly smaller ship for Genoa and Spezia, and another for Boston, U.S.A. Then there was a big five-masted sailing ship, bound with a general cargo for Buenos Aires and the River Platte, and finally there was a small freighter in ballast for Casablanca.

===Chinese===
头一艘是加拿大太平洋轮船公司（Canadian Pacific）的班轮，下午 3:00 从码头起航；3:30 又有一艘小客船开往奥斯陆（Oslo）和卑尔根（Bergen）。余下的都是不定期货船：四艘跑沿海航线，两艘去纽卡斯尔（Newcastle），一艘去古尔（Goole），一艘去贝尔法斯特（Belfast）；一艘六千吨的去新加坡（Singapore）和荷属群岛；一艘略小些的去热那亚（Genoa）和斯佩齐亚（Spezia）；还有一艘去美国波士顿（Boston）。再往下是一艘五桅大帆船，装着杂货开往布宜诺斯艾利斯（Buenos Aires）和拉普拉塔河（River Platte）；末了一艘小货轮，空载压舱，开往卡萨布兰卡（Casablanca）。

===Original===
Of these eleven ships, the windjammer at once attracted French’s attention. Here was a vessel on which, if you took a passage, you might easily require three dozen tins of peaches before you reached your journey’s end. He determined to begin with this, taking the other ships in order according to the position of their offices. Fortunately in each case the clerk had given him the name of the owners or agents.

===Chinese===
这十一艘船里，那艘大帆船一下就勾住了弗伦奇的注意。乘这样一条船搭客远行，只怕还没到旅程的尽头，轻轻松松就得吃掉三打桃子罐头。他决意先从它查起，其余各船，再按各自船东事务所的位置顺路挨家去过。幸而每艘船的船东或代理行的名号，那位职员都给了他。

===Original===
His first call, therefore, was at an old-fashioned office in a small street close to the Steen Museum. There he saw M. Leblanc, the owner of the windjammer, and explained his business. But M. Leblanc could not help him. The old gentleman had never heard of Dangle nor had anyone resembling his visitor’s photograph called or done any business with his firm. Moreover, no passengers had shipped on the windjammer, and the crew that had sailed was unchanged since the previous voyage.

===Chinese===
于是他头一处拜访的，是斯滕博物馆（Steen Museum）近旁小巷里一家老式事务所。他在那里见到了大帆船的船东勒布朗先生（M. Leblanc），说明来意。可是勒布朗先生帮不上忙。这位老先生从没听说过丹格尔其人，也没有任何相貌同来访者那张照片相像的人来过、同这家商行打过交道。再者，大帆船上没有搭载乘客，出海的船员同上一航次一样，原班未换。

===Original===
This was not encouraging, and French went on to the next item on his program, the headquarters of the small freighter which had sailed in ballast for Casablanca. She was owned by Messrs. Merkel & Lowenthal, whose office was farther down the Rue des Tanneurs, and five minutes later he had pushed open the door and was inquiring for the principal.

===Chinese===
这结果不算鼓舞人心。弗伦奇便接着办计划上的下一项：那艘空载开往卡萨布兰卡的小货轮的行址。这船属于默克尔-洛温塔尔商行（Messrs. Merkel & Lowenthal），事务所就在制革匠街更下去一点的地方。五分钟后，他已推开门，求见负责人。

===Original===
This was a more modern establishment than that of M. Leblanc. Though small, the office ran to plate glass windows, teak furniture, polished brass fittings, and encaustic tiles, while the two typists he could envisage through the small inquiry window seemed unduly gorgeous as to raiment and pert as to demeanor.

===Chinese===
这一家的气派比勒布朗先生的事务所新式。铺面虽小，却很舍得下本钱：平板大玻璃窗、柚木家具、擦得锃亮的黄铜配件、彩纹瓷砖；从那扇小小的问询窗望进去，能看见两名打字员，衣着一味艳丽，神气一味轻佻。

===Original===
He was kept waiting for some minutes, then told that M. Merkel, the head of the business, was away, but that M. Lowenthal, the junior partner, would see him.

===Chinese===
他被晾了好几分钟，然后被告知：商行掌班默克尔先生外出了，不过小合伙人洛温塔尔先生可以见他。

===Original===
His first glance told French that M. Lowenthal was a man to be watched. Seldom had he seen so many of the telltale signs of roguery concentrated in the features of one person. The junior partner had a mean, sly look, close-set, shifty eyes which would not meet French’s, and a large mouth with loose, fleshy lips. His manner was in accord with his appearance, now blustering, now almost fulsomely ingratiating. French took an instant dislike to him, and though he remained courteous as ever, he determined not to lay his cards on the table.

===Chinese===
头一眼弗伦奇就看出，这位洛温塔尔先生是个须得提防的人物。他很少见过这么多奸猾的征候集中体现在一张脸上。这位小合伙人一副刻薄相、狡猾相，两眼挤得很近，眼神闪烁游移，不敢同弗伦奇对视，一张大嘴，嘴唇松肥。他的做派也同这副长相相称：忽而咋咋呼呼，忽而近乎谄媚得叫人起腻。弗伦奇顿生厌恶，但表面上照旧客气周到，心里却打定了主意：不亮底牌。

===Original===
“My name,” he began, “as you will have seen from my card, is French, and I carry out the business of a general agent in London. I am trying to obtain an interview with a friend, who has been staying here, off and on, for some time. I came on here from Brussels in the hope of seeing him, but he had just left. I was told that he had sailed with your ship, the *L’Escaut*, on Thursday afternoon, and if so I called to ask at which port I should be likely to get in touch with him. His name is Dangle.”

===Chinese===
“我的姓名，”他开口道，“您从名片上想必已经看到，是弗伦奇，在伦敦做一般代理商的营生。我想见一位朋友一面，他断断续续在此地盘桓了些时日。我从布鲁塞尔赶来指望见他，不巧他刚走。听说他星期四下午搭贵行的船——莱斯考号（*L’Escaut*）——出海了；若是这样，我登门是想请问：到哪个港口最有希望同他取得联系？他的名字是丹格尔。”

===Original===
While French spoke he watched the other narrowly, on his favorite theory that the involuntary replies to unexpected remarks﻿—starts, changes of expression, sudden pallors﻿—were more valuable than spoken answers.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇说话的时候，眼睛紧紧盯着对方。按他那套心爱的理论：人对意外之言不自觉的反应——一惊、变脸、霎时面无血色——远比口头的回答更有价值。

===Original===
But M. Lowenthal betrayed no emotion other than a mild surprise.

===Chinese===
可是洛温塔尔先生除了微微露出一点惊讶之外，什么也没有流露。

===Original===
“That iss a very egstraordinary statement, sir,” he said in heavy guttural tones. “I do not really know who could haf given you such misleading information. Your friend’s name is quite unknown to me, and in any case we do not take passengers on our ships.”

===Chinese===
“这可是句非同寻常的话，先生，”他用浓重的喉音说道。“我实在想不出，是谁给了您这样误导人的消息。您那位朋友的大名，我压根没有听说过；再说，我行的船本来就一概不载客。”

===Original===
This seemed an entirely reasonable and proper reply, and yet to French’s highly developed instincts it did not ring true. However, he could do nothing more, and after a little further conversation containing not a few veiled inquiries, all of which, he noted, were skillfully parried by the other, he apologized for his mistake and withdrew.

===Chinese===
这回答听上去入情入理、无可挑剔，可在弗伦奇那副千锤百炼的直觉听来，总归有点不对味。不过他一时也无可再为。又稍谈了一会儿，话里夹了不少藏头露尾的探问——他注意到，这些全教对方四两拨千斤地挡了回去——于是他为自己的弄错道歉一声，告退出来。

===Original===
Though he was dissatisfied with the interview, he could only continue his program. He recognized that the secret might be located in Canada or the States, and that Dangle might have booked on the C.P.R. liner. Or he might have gone to Norway﻿—indeed, for the matter of that, he might have signed on on any of the ships for any part of the world.

===Chinese===
这一场会面虽叫他不满，他也只能照原计划继续查。他承认，秘密没准儿就在加拿大或美国，丹格尔或许订了加拿大太平洋班轮的铺位；要么就是去了挪威（Norway）——说实在的，退一步讲，他也完全可能随便在哪条船上签了约，去往世界上任何一个角落。

===Original===
But after a tedious morning of calls and interviews, French had to confess defeat. He could get no farther. At none of the offices at which he applied had he obtained the slightest helpful hint. It began to look as if he had been mistaken as to Dangle’s sea expedition, and if so, as he reminded himself with exasperation, he had no alternative theory to follow up.

===Chinese===
可是一个上午跑下来，拜访一处又一处、会面一场又一场，乏味之极，弗伦奇只得承认失败。他再也查不下去了。所叩过的事务所，没有一家给过他一丝一毫有用的暗示。事情渐渐像是：他把丹格尔海上远征这一层想岔了；倘若当真如此——他一面恼火一面提醒自己——手头可再没有别的理论可供追索了。

===Original===
He strolled slowly along the pleasant, sunlit streets, as he reviewed his morning’s work. He was satisfied with all his interviews but the one. Everywhere save in M. Lowenthal’s office he felt he had been told the truth. But instinctively he distrusted the junior partner. That the man had lied to him he had no reason to suspect, but he had no doubt that he would do so if it suited his book.

===Chinese===
他沿着阳光和煦、景致宜人的街道缓步走着，回味一上午的功课。除那一场之外，其余的会面他都满意。除了洛温塔尔先生的事务所，走到哪儿他都觉得听到的是实话。可是对这位小合伙人，他本能地信不过。说他已经对自己撒了谎，倒还没有什么理由可以指摘；可他毫不怀疑：只要合乎此人的盘算，他是撒得出来的。

===Original===
French felt that it was unsatisfactory to leave the matter in this state, and he presently thought of a simple subterfuge whereby it might be cleared up. It was almost the lunch hour, a suitable time for putting his project into operation. He hurried back to the Rue des Tanneurs, and turning into a café nearly opposite Messrs. Merkel & Lowenthal’s premises, ordered a bock and selected a seat from which he could observe the office door.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇觉得，事情撂在这个状态未免不甘，不多时便想出一条简单的小计策，或许能把它弄个水落石出。此刻将近午饭时分，正宜行事。他赶紧回到制革匠街，拐进一家几乎正对默克尔-洛温塔尔商行门面的咖啡馆，要了一杯博克啤酒，拣了个看得住那事务所大门的座位。

===Original===
He was only just in time. He had not taken his place five minutes when he saw M. Lowenthal emerge and walk off towards the center of the town. Three men clerks and the two rapid-looking typists followed, and lastly there appeared the person for whom he was waiting﻿—the sharp-looking office boy who had attended to him earlier in the day.

===Chinese===
他来得恰是时候。落座还不到五分钟，就见洛温塔尔先生出了门，朝市中心方向去了。三名男职员和那两名模样轻佻的打字员鱼贯而出，末了现身的，正是他守候的那位——白天接待过他的那个机灵相的办公室小伙计。

===Original===
The boy turned off in the opposite direction to his principal﻿—towards a quarter inhabited by laborers and artisans, and French, getting up from his table, slipped quietly out of the café and followed him.

===Chinese===
那小伙计同他东家背道而驰，拐向工人和手艺人聚居的一片街区。弗伦奇从座位上起身，悄悄溜出咖啡馆，跟了上去。

===Original===
The chase continued for some ten minutes, when the quarry disappeared into a small house in a back street. French strolled up and down until some half an hour later the young fellow reappeared. As he approached French allowed a look of recognition and slight surprise to appear on his features.

===Chinese===
跟踪持续了约莫十分钟，目标便钻进了后街一座小屋。弗伦奇踱来踱去地守着，约莫半个钟头后，小伙子重新露面。待他走近，弗伦奇让脸上浮出一副认出对方、略带惊讶的神情。

===Original===
“Ah,” he said, pausing with a friendly smile, “you are the clerk who attended to me this morning in Messrs. Merkel & Lowenthal’s office, are you not? A piece of luck meeting you! I wonder if you could give me a piece of information? I forgot to ask it of M. Lowenthal this morning, and as I am in a hurry, it would be worth five francs to me not to have to go back to your office.”

===Chinese===
“啊，”他停住脚，含着友好的笑容说道，“你就是今早在默克尔-洛温塔尔商行事务所里接待过我的那位职员，对不对？碰见你可真巧！不知你能不能给我指点一件事？今早我忘了问洛温塔尔先生，眼下又赶时间，若能省得回贵行跑一趟，五个法郎我是乐意的。”

===Original===
The youth’s eyes had brightened at the suggestion of financial dealings, and French felt he would learn all the other could tell him. He therefore continued without waiting for a reply.

===Chinese===
一听有金钱交易，小伙计的眼睛顿时亮了。弗伦奇觉得，对方能说的，自己全能听得到，于是不等答话，径直往下说。

===Original===
“The thing is this: I am joining my friend, M. Dangle, aboard the *L’Escaut* at the first opportunity. It was arranged between us that one of us should take with him a couple of dozen of champagne. I want to know whether he took the stuff, or whether I am to. Can you help me at all?”

===Chinese===
“事情是这样：一有机会，我就要上莱斯考号去会我的朋友丹格尔先生。我们俩讲定了，由一个人带上两打香槟。我想知道，那批货究竟是他带了，还是该由我来带。你能不能帮个忙？”

===Original===
The clerk’s English, though fairly good, was not quite equal to such a strain, and French had to repeat himself less idiomatically. But the boy grasped his meaning at last, and then at once dashed his hopes by saying he had never heard of any M. Dangle.

===Chinese===
小伙计的英语虽说还不错，应付这样的话却有点吃力，弗伦奇只得换一套少些惯用语的说法重讲一遍。那小伙计总算弄懂了他的意思，可紧接着一句话就教他的指望落了空：他从没听说过有什么丹格尔先生。

===Original===
“There he is,” French went on, producing his photograph. “You must have seen him scores of times.”

===Chinese===
“喏，就是他，”弗伦奇说着，掏出照片。“你准见过他几十回了。”

===Original===
And then French got the reward of his pertinacity. A look of recognition passed over the clerk’s features, and he made a gesture of comprehension.

===Chinese===
这一下，弗伦奇的锲而不舍得了报偿。小伙计脸上掠过一丝认出来的神色，做了一个会意的手势。

===Original===
“*Mais oui, m’sieur*; yes, sir,” he answered quickly, “but that is not M. Danggalle. I know him: it is M. Charles.”

===Chinese===
“*Mais oui, m’sieur*（是的，先生）；对，先生，”他飞快地答道，“可那不是当加勒先生。这个人我认得：他是夏尔先生（M. Charles）。”

===Original===
“That’s right,” French returned, trying to keep the triumph out of his voice. “His name is Dangle Charles. I know him as M. Dangle, because he is one of four brothers at our works. But of course he would give his name here as M. Charles. But now, can you tell me anything about the champagne?”

===Chinese===
“这就对了，”弗伦奇应道，竭力不让话音里透出得意。“他名叫丹格尔·夏尔。我平日叫他丹格尔先生，因为在我们厂里，他是丹格尔家四兄弟之一。不过到了这边，他自然只报夏尔先生的名字。那么现在——香槟的事，你能告诉我些什么吗？”

===Original===
The clerk shook his head. He had not known upon what business M. Charles had called at the office.

===Chinese===
小伙计摇了摇头。夏尔先生为着什么事到事务所来，他并不知道。

===Original===
“Oh, well, it can’t be helped,” French declared. “I thought that perhaps when he was in with you last Wednesday you might have heard something about it. You don’t know what luggage he took aboard the *L’Escaut*?”

===Chinese===
“唉，那也没法子，”弗伦奇说道。“我原想着，上星期三他在你们里间的当口，你们兴许听到过一句半句呢。他带上莱斯考号的行李是些什么，你也不知道么？”

===Original===
The clerk had not been aware that M. Charles had embarked on the freighter, still less did he know of what his luggage had consisted. But as French talked on in his pleasant way, the following facts became apparent; first, that Dangle for some weeks past had been an occasional visitor at the shipping office; second, that on the previous Wednesday he had been closeted with the partners for the greater part of the day; third, that the *L’Escaut* had evidently sailed on an expedition of considerable importance and length, for a vast deal of stores had gone aboard her, about which both partners had shown very keen anxiety; fourthly, that not only had M. Merkel, the senior partner, himself sailed on her, but it was likely that he intended to be away some time as M. Lowenthal had moved into his room, and lastly, that the *L’Escaut* had come up from the firm’s yard during the Wednesday night and had anchored in the river off the Steen until she left about 3:00 p.m. on the Thursday.

===Chinese===
夏尔先生登上了那艘货轮，小伙计先前并不知道；他带了些什么行李，就更无从知晓了。可是经弗伦奇一路亲切地闲谈，下面几桩事实渐渐显豁出来：第一，几个星期以来，丹格尔时不时到这航运事务所来走动；第二，上星期三那一整天，大半工夫他都同两位合伙人关在里间密谈；第三，莱斯考号这回显然是执行一趟相当紧要、也相当长远的远航，因为船上装了大量物料储备，两位合伙人对这些物料都挂心得紧；第四，不单是大合伙人默克尔先生本人随船出海了，而且看来他打算离家相当时日，因为洛温塔尔先生已经搬进了他的房间；末了，莱斯考号是星期三夜里从商行的船坞里开上来的，一直在斯滕堡外的河面上下锚，直到星期四下午 3:00 前后才离港。

===Original===
These admissions made it abundantly clear that French was once more on the right track, and he handed over his five francs with the feeling that he had made the cheapest bargain of his life.

===Chinese===
这些口供充分说明，弗伦奇又摸上正路了。他交出那五个法郎时，只觉得这是他此生做得最便宜的一桩买卖。

===Original===
He had no doubt that Dangle had sailed with the senior partner on the tramp, but he felt he must make sure, and he walked slowly back towards the quays, turning over in his mind possible methods for settling the point. One inquiry seemed promising. If the ship had lain at anchor out in the river, and if Dangle had gone aboard her, he must have had a boat to do so. French wondered could he find that boat.

===Chinese===
丹格尔是随大合伙人搭那艘不定期货船出的海，这一点他已没有疑问；可他觉得还须坐实，便缓步朝码头走回去，一路在心里盘算坐实这一点的种种可行法子。有一条查法似乎有希望：那船既锚泊在河心，丹格尔若上了船，势必得坐小艇摆渡。弗伦奇寻思，那条小艇还能不能找到。

===Original===
He felt himself held up by the language difficulty. Up to the present he had had extraordinary luck in this respect, but then up to the present he had been interviewing educated persons whose business brought them in contact with foreigners. He doubted if he could make boatmen and loafers about the quays understand what he wanted.

===Chinese===
他觉得自己又被语言这一关卡住了。至今为止，他在这方面运气好得出奇；可话说回来，至今为止他会晤的都是些因营生之便惯同外国人打交道的读书人。码头上的船夫闲汉能不能听懂他要什么，他心里没底。

===Original===
A trial convinced him that his fears were well founded, and he lost a solid hour in finding the Berlitz School and engaging a young linguist with a reputation for discretion. Then, accompanied by M. Jules Renard, he returned to the quays and set systematically to work. He began by inquiring where boats might be hired, and where there were steps at which ships’ boats might come alongside. Taking these in turn he asked had the boatmen taken a passenger out to the *L’Escaut* between 2:00 and 3:00 p.m. on the previous Thursday? Or had the loafer, stevedore, shunter, or constable, as the case might be, noticed if a boat had come ashore from the same vessel on the same date and at the same time?

===Chinese===
试了那么一回，他便确信所虑不虚，于是足足耗掉一个钟头，寻到伯利茨语言学校（Berlitz School），雇下一位素有口风谨严之誉的年轻语言学者。此后，由朱尔·勒纳尔先生（M. Jules Renard）作陪，他回到码头，有条不紊地干了起来。他先打听哪里可以雇到小艇，哪里有供船板靠岸的台阶；然后挨处问过去：上星期四下午 2:00 到 3:00 之间，众位船夫可曾送一位乘客去莱斯考号？或者——视对方是闲汉、装卸工、调车工还是警察而定——可曾有人留意到，同一日期、同一时刻，有一条小艇从那艘船上回靠过岸？

===Original===
Though the work was easy it bade fair to be tedious, and therefore for more than one reason French felt a glow of satisfaction when at his fourth inquiry his question received an affirmative answer. A wizened old man, one of a small knot of longshoremen whom M. Renard addressed, separated himself from his companions and came forward. He said that he was a boatman, and that he had been hailed by a man﻿—an Englishman, he believed﻿—at the time stated, and had rowed him out to the ship.

===Chinese===
这活儿虽不难，却眼见得单调冗长，因此——也不单为这一个缘故——问到第四处便得了肯定答复时，弗伦奇只觉一阵暖洋洋的快意。勒纳尔先生同一小簇码头工人搭上了话，其中一位干瘦的老头儿从同伴中站出来，走上前。他说自己是个船夫，说是在前面说过的那个时刻，被一个人喊住——他估摸是个英国人——把他划到那艘船上去了。

===Original===
“Ask him if that’s the man,” French directed, producing Dangle’s photograph, though he felt there could be no doubt as to the reply.

===Chinese===
“问问他，是不是这个人，”弗伦奇吩咐道，一面掏出丹格尔的照片，尽管他觉得答复必无可疑。

===Original===
He was therefore immensely dashed when the boatman shook his head. This was not the man at all. The traveler was a short, rather stout man with a small fair mustache.

===Chinese===
因此，那船夫摇头时，他大感泄气。根本不是这个人。搭船的是个矮个子、颇富态的男人，留着两撇浅色小胡子。

===Original===
French gasped. The description sounded familiar. Taking out Blessington’s photograph he passed it over.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇倒吸了一口气。这番描述听着耳熟。他掏出布莱辛顿的照片，递了过去。

===Original===
This time the boatman nodded. Yes, that was the man he had rowed out. He had no doubt of him whatever.

===Chinese===
这一回船夫点头了。对，他划出去的正是这个人。对此他毫无疑问。

===Original===
This was unexpected but most welcome news, though as French thought over it, he saw that it was not so surprising after all. If Dangle was in it, why not Blessington, and for the matter of that, why not Sime also? In this case he wondered where Susan could be, and more acutely, what had been the fate of Joan Merrill. Possibly, he thought, his inquiries about Dangle would solve these questions also.

===Chinese===
这消息出人意料，却也求之不得。不过弗伦奇细细一想，又觉得其实不算太意外：丹格尔既已入伙，为什么布莱辛顿不能入伙？再退一步说，赛姆为什么不能？这么一来他就纳闷了：苏珊会在哪儿？而更叫他揪心的是，琼·梅里尔落了个什么下场。他想，说不定顺着丹格尔这条线查下去，这些问题也会一并解开。

===Original===
Half an hour later he struck oil for the second time. Another boatman, a little further along the quays, had also rowed a passenger out to the *L’Escaut*, and this one, it appeared, was Dangle. But though French kept working steadily away, he could hear nothing of Sime.

===Chinese===
半个钟头后，他第二回凿出了油。码头再往前去些，另一个船夫也曾送一位乘客去莱斯考号，而这一位，据说正是丹格尔。可是任凭弗伦奇稳扎稳打一路查下去，赛姆却杳无音信。

===Original===
In the end it was a suggestion of Renard’s that put him once more on the trail. The interpreter proved an intelligent youth, and when he had grasped the point at issue, he stopped and pointed to the river.

===Chinese===
到头来，倒是勒纳尔的一句提议，教他重又摸上了线索。这位译员果然是个伶俐青年；等他抓住了问题的症结，便站住脚，朝河面一指。

===Original===
“You say, monsieur, that the sheep, she lie there, opposite the Musée Steen, is it not so? *Bon!* We haf walked along all the quays near to that. Your friends would not haf hired boat from farther on﻿—it is too far. You say, too, they come from England secretly, is it not? *Bon!* They would come to the other side.”

===Chinese===
“先生不是说，那条……那条‘绵羊’——船——就停在那儿，斯滕博物馆（Musée Steen）对面，对不对？*Bon*（好）！那一带的码头，咱们全走遍了。您的朋友们决不会再往远处去雇船——太远了。先生还说过，他们是打英国悄悄过来的，对不对？*Bon*！那他们就会到那一边去。”

===Original===
French did not understand.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇没有听懂。

===Original===
“The other side?” he repeated questioningly.

===Chinese===
“那一边？”他带着疑问重复了一遍。

===Original===
“But yes, monsieur, the other side.” The young fellow’s eyes flashed in his eagerness. “Over there, La Gare de Waes.” He pointed out across the great stream to its west bank.

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛，先生，那一边。”小伙子急得两眼放光。“在那边，韦斯车站（La Gare de Waes）。”他抬手朝大河对岸、西面一岸指去。

===Original===
“I didn’t know there was a station across there,” French admitted. “Where does the line go to?”

===Chinese===
“我还不知道河那边有一座车站呢，”弗伦奇承认道。“这条线通到哪儿？”

===Original===
“Direct to Ghent. Your friends change trains at Ghent. It is a quiet railway. They come unseen.”

===Chinese===
“直达根特。您的朋友们在根特换车。那是一条清静的铁路。他们来时不叫人看见。”

===Original===
“Good man,” said French heartily. “We’ll go and find out. How do you get to the blessed place?”

===Chinese===
“好伙计，”弗伦奇由衷赞道。“咱们这就去查个明白。这要命的地方怎么个去法？”

===Original===
M. Renard smiled delightedly.

===Chinese===
勒纳尔先生喜滋滋地笑了。

===Original===
“Ah yes, monsieur. You weesh to cross? Is it not?” he cried. “This way. We take ferry from the Quai Van Dyck. It is near.”

===Chinese===
“啊，对了，先生。您是想渡过去，是不是？”他叫道。“这边走。咱们从范戴克码头（Quai Van Dyck）搭渡船。就在近处。”

===Original===
Half an hour later they had reached the Tête de Flandre﻿—the low-lying western bank of the Scheldt. It bore a small but not unpicturesque cluster of old-fashioned houses, nestling about one of the historic Antwerp forts. Renard, now apparently quite as interested in the chase as French, led the way along the river bank from boatman to boatman, with the result that before very many minutes had passed French had obtained the information he wanted.

===Chinese===
半个钟头后，他们到了佛兰德角（Tête de Flandre）——斯海尔德河（the Scheldt）低平的西岸。岸边簇拥着一小片老式房屋，倒也不乏画意，偎依在安特卫普一处历史要塞旁。勒纳尔此刻对这场追踪的兴致，显然已同弗伦奇一般无二，领头沿着河岸挨个船夫问过去；不多几分钟，弗伦奇便把想要的消息弄到了手。

===Original===
It appeared that about 1:00 p.m. on the day in question, a strapping young boatman had noticed three strangers approaching from the direction of the Waes Station, a hundred yards or more distant. They consisted of a tall, clean-shaven man of something under middle age and two women, both young. One was tall and strongly made and dark as to hair and eyes, the other was slighter and with red gold hair. The smaller one seemed to be ill, and was stumbling along between the other two, each of whom supported her by an arm. None of the trio could speak French or Flemish, but they managed by signs to convey the information that they wanted to be put on board the *L’Escaut*, which was lying out in midstream. The man had rowed them out, and they had been received on board by an elderly gentleman with a dark beard.

===Chinese===
据说在前面说的那个日子下午 1:00 前后，一个魁梧的年轻船夫注意到三个陌生人，打百码开外的韦斯车站方向走来。三人是一个高个子、脸刮得干干净净、年纪还没到中年的男人，和两个年轻女子：一个高挑结实，头发眼睛都黑；另一个纤瘦些，一头红金色头发。瘦小的那位像是病了，踉踉跄跄走在两人中间，一边一个架着她的胳膊。三人谁也不会说法语或佛兰芒语，只靠连比带划表明：要人送他们上停在河心的莱斯考号。船夫把他们划了出去，船上是一位黑胡子的上了年纪的绅士把他们接上去的。

===Original===
Further questions produced the information that the fair lady appeared to be seriously ill, though whether it was her mind or body that was affected, the boatman couldn’t be sure. She was able to walk, but would not do so unless urged on by the others. She had not spoken or taken any interest in the journey. She had not appeared even to look round her, but had sat gazing listlessly at nothing, with a vacant expression in her eyes. Her companions had had real difficulty in getting her up the short ladder on to the *L’Escaut*’s deck.

===Chinese===
再三追问之下又问出：那位浅色头发的太太病得像是不轻，只是病在心上还是身上，船夫拿不准。她走得动，可若没人催促，便不肯挪步。一路上一言不发，对这趟行程全无兴致，甚至像是连四下里都不曾看一看，只呆坐着恹恹地望着虚空，眼里一片空茫。她的同伴们扶她攀上那段短短的舷梯、登上莱斯考号的甲板，着实费了一番手脚。

===Original===
The news was rather unexpected to French. About Joan Merrill it was both disconcerting and reassuring; the former because he could not see that the gang had anything but a sinister reason for inveigling the young girl aboard the ship﻿—probably she will fall overboard at night, he thought; the latter because she was at least still alive, or had been two days ago. It was quite evident that she was drugged, probably with morphine or something similar. It might, however, mean that while wishing Joan no harm, they were taking her with them on their expedition to insure her silence as to their movements.

===Chinese===
这消息对弗伦奇来说相当意外。关于琼·梅里尔，它既叫人不安，又叫人宽心：不安的是，他想不出这伙人诱这位年轻姑娘上船还能有什么缘由，除了不怀好意——多半哪天夜里她就会“失足落水”，他想；宽心的是，她至少还活着，或者说两天前还活着。显而易见，她是被下了药，多半是吗啡之类。不过，这也可能意味着：他们虽无意加害琼，却要把她带在远征队里，好确保她对他们的行踪守口如瓶。

===Original===
As French returned across the ferry, he kept on puzzling as to Lowenthal’s position. Could Lowenthal be arrested? Was he in league with the gang? If so, could he be held responsible for the abduction of Joan Merrill? French didn’t think the evidence would justify drastic measures. He had, as a matter of fact, no actual evidence against Lowenthal. Of his complicity he was satisfied, but he doubted if he could prove it.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇搭渡船折返时，一路反复琢磨洛温塔尔的处境。洛温塔尔可以逮捕么？他同团伙串通一气么？若是，琼·梅里尔遭劫持一事，能否要他担责？弗伦奇觉得，眼下的证据还不够采取断然措施的资格。说实在的，他手里没有一件针对洛温塔尔的实据。此人的干系，他确信无疑；可要证实，他没把握。

===Original===
He got rid of the young interpreter, and strolling slowly along the quays, thought the matter out. No, he had not a enough case with which to go to the Belgian police. But he could do the next best thing. He could call on M. Lowenthal for the second time, and try to bluff an admission out of him.

===Chinese===
他打发走了那位年轻译员，沿码头缓步而行，把这件事从头想了个透。不行，案子还不够分量，不足以端到比利时警方面前。可退而求其次的办法还是有的：他可以二访洛温塔尔先生，试着唬出一句招认来。

===Original===
