As he walked to the Rue des Tanneurs, he felt his prospects were not rosy. But at least he had no difficulty in obtaining his interview. M. Lowenthal seemed surprised to see him so soon again, but received him politely, and asked what he could do for him.

===Chinese===
往制革匠街去的路上，他自觉前景并不美妙。但至少，这次会面他毫不费力就求到了。洛温塔尔先生见他这么快又来，似乎吃了一惊，但还是客气地接待了他，问有何贵干。

===Original===
“I want to ask you another question, M. Lowenthal, if you please,” French answered in his pleasantest manner, “and first I must tell you that the agency I hold is that of Detective Inspector at New Scotland Yard in London. My question is this: When you and M. Merkel entered into relations with Blessington, Sime, and the Dangles, did you know that they were dangerous criminals wanted by the English police?”

===Chinese===
“要是您允许，洛温塔尔先生，我还想再问您一个问题，”弗伦奇用他最讨喜的态度答道，“不过首先我得奉告：在下一身所任的差使，是伦敦新苏格兰场的探长。我的问题是：您和默克尔先生同布莱辛顿、赛姆和丹格尔家兄妹打交道的时候，可知道他们都是英国警方通缉的危险罪犯？”

===Original===
In spite of the most evident efforts for self-control, Lowenthal was so much taken aback that he could not for some moments speak. His swarthy face turned a greenish hue and little drops of sweat showed on his forehead. To the other pleasant characteristics with which French had mentally endowed him, he now added that of coward, and his hopes of his bluff succeeding grew brighter. He sat waiting in silence for the other to recover himself, then said suavely:

===Chinese===
尽管明显竭力自制，洛温塔尔还是惊得有好一阵子说不出话。他那张黝黑的脸膛泛出一层青色，额头上沁出细小的汗珠。弗伦奇在心里给他记下的种种可爱品性中，如今又添了一样：懦夫。他这一场唬诈得手的指望，随之更亮了几分。他默坐静候，等对方回过神来，才温和地说：

===Original===
“After that, M. Lowenthal, you will see for yourself that you cannot plead ignorance of the affair. Let me advise you for your own sake to be open with me.”

===Chinese===
“话已至此，洛温塔尔先生，您自己也看得出，这件事您再不能推说不知情了。恕我奉劝一句：为您自己着想，还是对我坦白些好。”

===Original===
The man pulled himself together. He wiped his brow as he replied earnestly, but in somewhat shaky accents:

===Chinese===
那人重新振作起来。他抹了把额头，一面恳切作答，只是语声有些发颤：

===Original===
“That I haf met Blessington, Sime, and Dangle I do not deny, though they were Merkel’s friends﻿—not mine. But I do not know that they are criminal. Dangle, he called here and asked Merkel to take him on the next”﻿—he hesitated for a word﻿—“next work, next sail of the sheep. Merkel said that Dangle iss a writer﻿—he writes books. He weeshed to see the sail to Casablanca to deescribe it in hiss book. Merkel said he would haf to pay fare, the firm could not afford it unless. Dangle agreed. Merkel was going himself, and Dangle suggested Sime and Blessington go also to make party﻿—to play cards. Of a second Dangle I know nothing. They went secretly﻿—I admit it﻿—because the law forbids to take passengers for sail without a certificate. That is all of the affair.”

===Chinese===
“我见过布莱辛顿、赛姆和丹格尔，这我不否认，不过他们是默克尔的朋友——不是我的。可我并不知道他们是罪犯。丹格尔，他来过这儿，求默克尔带上他搭下一趟”——他一时找不着词——“下一趟活计，下一班‘绵羊’——船。默克尔说，丹格尔是个作家——写书的。他想看看开往卡萨布兰卡的这一趟，好写进他的书里去。默克尔说，他得付船钱，不然商行负担不起。丹格尔答应了。默克尔自己本来要去，丹格尔便提议，让赛姆和布莱辛顿也一起去凑一伙人——打打牌。至于还有什么第二个丹格尔，我一无所知。他们是瞒着人走的——这我承认——因为法律禁止无证载客出海。事情的全部，就是这些。”

===Original===
Not a single word of this statement did French believe, but he saw that unless he could get some further information, or surprise this Lowenthal into some more damaging admission, he could not have him arrested. After all, the story hung together. Merkel might conceivably be playing his own game, and have pitched the yarn of the author out for copy to his partner. The contravention of the shipping laws would undoubtedly account for the secrecy with which the start was made. Certainly there was no evidence to bring before a jury.

===Chinese===
这番话，弗伦奇一个字也不信；但他明白，除非再取得些进一步的消息，或者出其不意地诈出这个洛温塔尔更见罪状的口供，否则没法逮捕他。毕竟，这套说法自成一体、说得圆。默克尔没准儿真在打自己的算盘，把“作家出海找素材”这套瞎话先编给了自己的合伙人听。违反航运法规一条，也确凿能解释启程为何如此隐秘。拿到陪审团面前，眼下肯定还没有证据。

===Original===
French proceeded to question the junior partner with considerable thoroughness, but he could not shake his statement. The only additional facts he learned were that the *L’Escaut* was going to Casablanca on the order of the Moroccan Government to load up a cargo of agricultural samples for the Italian market, and that M. Merkel was accompanying it simply as a holiday trip.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇接着又相当彻底地盘问了这位小合伙人一番，却始终没能动摇他的供词。他额外问出来的只有两件事：其一，莱斯考号是奉摩洛哥政府之命前往卡萨布兰卡，装运一船供应意大利市场的农产品样品；其二，默克尔先生随船同行，纯粹是当作一趟假日消游。

===Original===
With this French had to be content, and he went to the post office, and got through on the long distance telephone to his chief at the Yard. To him he repeated the essentials of the tale, asking him to inquire from the Moroccan authorities as to the truth of their portion of it, as well as to endeavor to trace the *L’Escaut*.

===Chinese===
事已至此，弗伦奇也只好满足。他去邮局接通了打给苏格兰场上司的长途电话，把这件事的要点向他复述了一遍，请他向摩洛哥当局查证其中摩方说法的真伪，同时设法追查莱斯考号的下落。

===Original===
On leaving the post office, it occurred to him that communication with the *L’Escaut* should be possible by wireless, and he returned to the Rue des Tanneurs to ascertain this point. There he was told that just after he had left M. Lowenthal had received a telephone call, requiring his immediate presence in Holland, and he had with a great rush caught the afternoon express for the Dutch capital.

===Chinese===
出了邮局，他忽然想到，同莱斯考号联系应该可以走无线电这条路，便又折回制革匠街来问个明白。到那里却听说，他前脚刚走，洛温塔尔先生后脚就接了一个电话，有事要他即刻赶赴荷兰，他便火急火燎地搭上午后开往荷兰首都的快车走了。

===Original===
“Skedaddled, by Jove!” said French to himself. “Guess that lets in the Belgian police.”

===Chinese===
“好家伙，溜之大吉了！”弗伦奇自言自语。“看来这一来，比利时警方非请出来不可了。”

===Original===
He called at headquarters, and saw the officer in charge, and before he left to catch the connection for London, it had been arranged that the movements of the junior partner should be gone into, and a watch kept for the return of that enterprising weaver of fairy tales.

===Chinese===
他到警察总局拜会了主管官员。等他去赶开往伦敦的联程车之前，一切已安排停当：这位小合伙人的种种行踪要细细查究，还要布下眼线，专候那位别出心裁、专会编神话的先生归来。

## XVIII A Visitor from India / 十八 印度来的访客

===Original===
When French reached Victoria, the first person he saw on the platform was Maxwell Cheyne.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇到达维多利亚车站时，在月台上看见的第一个人就是麦克斯韦·切恩。

===Original===
“They told me at the Yard that you might be on this train,” the young man said excitedly as he elbowed his way forward. “Any news? Anything about Miss Merrill?”

===Chinese===
“苏格兰场的人告诉我，您可能就在这班车上。”年轻人一边往前挤，一边激动地说。“有消息吗？有梅里尔小姐的音信吗？”

===Original===
He looked old and worn, and it was evident that his anxiety was telling on him. In his eagerness he could scarcely wait for the Inspector to dismount from his carriage, and his loud tones were attracting curious looks from the bystanders.

===Chinese===
他显得苍老憔悴，焦虑分明正折磨着他。他心急火燎，几乎等不及探长走下车厢，大嗓门引得旁边的人纷纷侧目。

===Original===
“Get a taxi,” French answered quietly. “We can talk there.”

===Chinese===
“去叫一辆出租汽车。”弗伦奇轻声答道。“咱们在车上谈。”

===Original===
A few seconds later they found a vehicle, and Cheyne, gripping the other by the arm, went on earnestly:

===Chinese===
几秒钟后两人便找到了车，切恩一把攥住对方的胳膊，恳切地接着说：

===Original===
“Tell me. I can see you have learned something. Is she﻿—all right?”

===Chinese===
“快告诉我。我看得出您打听到消息了。她——她还好吗？”

===Original===
“I got news of her on Thursday last. She was all right then, though still under the influence of a drug. The whole party has gone to sea.”

===Chinese===
“上星期四我得到了她的消息。那时她安然无恙，只是还受着药物的影响。那一伙人全都出海去了。”

===Original===
“To sea?”

===Chinese===
“出海？”

===Original===
“Yes, to sea in a small tramp. I don’t know what they are up to, but there is no reason to suppose Miss Merrill is otherwise than well. Probably they took her with them to prevent her giving them away. They would drug her to get her to go along, but would cease it as soon as she was on board. I wired for inquiries to be made at the different signal stations, and news may be waiting for us at the Yard.”

===Chinese===
“是的，乘一条小货轮出海。他们想干什么勾当，我不知道，可也没有理由认为梅里尔小姐有什么不妥。多半是怕她说出去坏了他们的事，才把她一起带走的。起初要她跟着走，会给她下药；等她一上船，药就会停了。我已经拍电报叫各个信号站去查访，说不定此刻苏格兰场里正有消息等着我们。”

===Original===
A few seconds sufficed to put Cheyne in possession of the salient facts which French had learned, and the latter in his turn asked for news.

===Chinese===
不消几秒钟，切恩便掌握了弗伦奇查明的那些要点，接着就轮到他来讲这边的消息了。

===Original===
“By Jove, yes!” Cheyne cried, “there is news. You remember that Arnold Price had disappeared? Well, yesterday I had a letter from him!”

===Chinese===
“天哪，可不是！”切恩喊道，“真有消息。您还记得阿诺德·普赖斯失踪的事吧？嘿，昨天我收到他一封信！”

===Original===
“You don’t say so?” French rejoined in surprise. “Where did he write from?”

===Chinese===
“竟有这事？”弗伦奇吃惊地应道。“他从哪儿写来的？”

===Original===
“Bombay. He was shortly leaving for home. He expects to be here in about a month.”

===Chinese===
“孟买。他不久就要动身回国，预计一个月左右到这儿。”

===Original===
“And what about his disappearance?”

===Chinese===
“那他失踪是怎么回事？”

===Original===
“He was ill in hospital. He had gone up to Agra on some private business and met with an accident﻿—was knocked down in the street and was insensible for ages. He couldn’t say who he was, and the hospital people in Agra couldn’t find out, and he hadn’t told the Bombay people where he was going to spend his leave.”

===Chinese===
“他病倒进了医院。他为一点私事上阿格拉（Agra）去，出了意外——在街上被撞倒，人事不省了老半天。他说不清自己是谁，阿格拉医院里的人查不出来，他又没告诉孟买的人自己要去哪里休假。”

===Original===
“Did he mention the letter?”

===Chinese===
“他提到那封信了吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, he thanked me for taking charge of it and said that when he reached home he would relieve me of further trouble about it. He little knows!”

===Chinese===
“提了。他谢我代为保管，还说一到家，就把我从这桩麻烦里解脱出去。他还蒙在鼓里呢！”

===Original===
“That’s so,” French assented.

===Chinese===
“可不是。”弗伦奇深以为然。

===Original===
Their taxi had been held up by a block at the end of Westminster Bridge, but now the mass cleared and in a few seconds they reached the Yard.

===Chinese===
他们的车在威斯敏斯特桥桥头被车流堵住，这时前面疏通了，不多几秒钟便到了苏格兰场。

===Original===
French’s first care was to get rid of Cheyne. He repeated what he had learned about Joan Merrill, then, assuring him that the key of the matter lay in the cipher, he advised him to go home and try it once more. Directly any more news came in he would let him know.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇头一件事是把切恩打发走。他把关于琼·梅里尔的消息重述了一遍，然后向他担保，全案的钥匙就在那份密码里，劝他回家再去试它一试。一有新的消息，他就会通知他。

===Original===
Cheyne having reluctantly taken his departure, French made inquiries as to what had been done in reference to his telephone from Antwerp. It appeared that the Yard had not been idle. In the first place an application had been made to the Moroccan Government, who had replied that no ship had been chartered by them for freight at Casablanca, nor was anything known of agricultural samples for the Italian market. Lowenthal’s story must therefore have been an absolute fabrication. He had, however, told it so readily that French suspected it had been made up beforehand, so as to be ready to serve up to any inquisitive policeman or detective who might come along.

===Chinese===
切恩一步三回头地走了。弗伦奇便着手查问：他从安特卫普打来电话之后，这边都办了些什么。看来苏格兰场没有闲着。头一件，已经向摩洛哥政府提出查询，对方答复说，他们并未在卡萨布兰卡租船运货，也不知道有什么运往意大利市场的农业样品。可见洛温塔尔那套说辞纯属捏造。不过他讲得那么现成，弗伦奇倒疑心那套话是事先编好的，专等随便哪个爱刨根问底的警察或侦探找上门来，就端出来应付。

===Original===
Next Lloyd’s had been approached, as to the direction the *L’Escaut* had taken, and a reply had shortly before come in from them. It stated that up to noon on that day, the vessel had not been reported from any of their stations. But this, French realized, might not mean so much. If she had gone south down the English Channel it would have been well on to dark before she reached the Straits of Dover. In any case, had she wished to slip through unseen, she had only to keep out to the middle of the passage, when in ordinary weather she would have been invisible from either coast. On the other hand, had she gone north, she would almost naturally have kept out of sight of land. It was true that in either case she would have been likely to pass some other vessel which would have spoken her, and the fact that no news of such a recognition had come to hand seemed to indicate that she was taking some unusual course out of the track of regular shipping.

===Chinese===
第二件，已经向劳合社（Lloyd’s）查询莱斯考号驶往哪个方向，对方不久前刚作了答复：截至当天正午，他们的各处分站都没有报告见到这艘船。不过弗伦奇明白，这也未必说明什么。她若是沿英吉利海峡南下，驶到多佛海峡时天已擦黑。况且，她真想悄悄溜过去，只消走航道正中，天气平常的日子里，两岸都望不见她。反过来，她若是北上，那也自然会离岸远远的，不叫陆地上望见。诚然，无论南下北上，她都可能遇上别的船，同对方互通名号；而这种相遇的消息一条也没传来，看来倒表明她走的是某条不寻常的路线，避开了正规航线。

===Original===
French wired this information to the Antwerp police, and then, his chief being disengaged, went in and gave him a detailed account of his adventures in Belgium.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇把这些情况电告安特卫普警方，随后趁上司得空，进去把自己在比利时的经历详细报告了一遍。

===Original===
Chief Inspector Mitchell was impressed by the story. He sat back in his chair and treated French to a prolonged stare as the latter talked. At the end of the recital he remained sitting motionless for some moments, whistling gently below his breath.

===Chinese===
米切尔总探长听了这个故事，颇为动容。弗伦奇讲述的当口，他仰靠在椅子里，久久盯着他看。听罢，他又一动不动地坐了片刻，压着嗓子轻轻吹口哨。

===Original===
“Any theories?” he said at last.

===Chinese===
“有什么高见？”他终于开口。

===Original===
French shook his head.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Well, no, sir,” he answered slowly. “It’s not easy to see what they’re after. And it’s not easy to see, either, why the whole gang wanted to go. It looked at first as if they were just clearing out because of Cheyne’s coming to the Yard, but it’s more than that. The arrangements were made too long ago. They have been dealing with that Antwerp firm for several weeks.”

===Chinese===
“唔，没有，先生。”他慢吞吞地答道。“他们图的是什么，不容易看清。还有，为什么全伙人都要走，也不容易看清。起初像是切恩上了苏格兰场，他们索性一走了之，可事情不止如此。那些安排动手得太早了。他们同那家安特卫普商行打交道，已经打了好几个星期。”

===Original===
“The hard copper was all a story?”

===Chinese===
“硬铜那一套全是编的？”

===Original===
“Looks like it, sir. As a matter of fact every single statement those men made that could be tested has been proved false. Even when there didn’t seem any great object in a yarn they pitched it. Lies seemed to come easier to them.”

===Chinese===
“看样子是的，先生。事实上，那伙人说的话，凡是可以查证的，一查全是假的。有时编个瞎话看不出有多大的用处，他们也照样编。撒谎对他们来说，张口就来。”

===Original===
“Well, I’ve known a good few cases of that, and so have you, French. It’s a habit that grows. Now, what’s your next move?”

===Chinese===
“唔，这种情形我见过不少，你也见过，弗伦奇。撒谎这习惯是越养越深的。说吧，下一步你打算怎么办？”

===Original===
French hesitated.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇迟疑了一下。

===Original===
“For the moment the outlook’s not very cheery,” he said at last. “All the same I can’t believe that boat can go away out of the Scheldt and disappear. In my judgment she’s bound to be reported before long, and I’m looking forward to getting word of her within the next day or so. Then I have no doubt that the tracing is some kind of cipher, and if we could read it we should probably get light on the whole affair.”

===Chinese===
“眼下的前景不算乐观。”他终于说道。“可我不信那船能驶出斯海尔德河，就此无影无踪。依我判断，早晚会有人报告她的行踪，我指望这一两天就能得到消息。再者，我毫不怀疑那份描图是某种密码，只要能读通它，全案多半就能见到亮了。”

===Original===
“Why shouldn’t you read it? Try it again.”

===Chinese===
“你怎么就读不通呢？再试试。”

===Original===
“I intend to, sir. But I don’t hope for much result, because I don’t believe we’ve got the genuine document. I don’t believe they would have handed it, nor a copy of it either, to a man they intended to murder, lest it should be found on his body. I’d state long odds they gave him a fake.”

===Chinese===
“我打算再试，先生。可我不抱多大指望，因为我们手里这份，我不信是真件。他们存心要害死一个人，就不会把原件交给他，连抄件也不会——就怕在他尸身上搜出来。要说他们给了他一件假货，我肯押重注。”

===Original===
“I think you’re probably right,” the chief admitted. “Try at all events. You never know your luck.”

===Chinese===
“我想你多半说得对。”上司承认道。“无论如何试一试。运气这东西，谁也说不准。”

===Original===
He bent over his desk, and French, realizing that the interview had come to an end, quietly left the room. Then, seeing there was nothing requiring his attention urgently, and tired after his journey, he went home.

===Chinese===
上司俯身回到案头。弗伦奇看出谈话已经到头，便悄悄退了出去。他见没有急事等着自己办，加之旅途劳顿，就回家去了。

===Original===
But contrary to his expectations, the next day passed without any news of the *L’Escaut*, and the next, and many days after that. Nor could all his efforts with the tracing throw any light on that mysterious document. As time passed he began to grow more and more despondent, and the fear that he was going to make a mess of the case grew steadily stronger. In vain he laid his difficulties before his wife. For once that final source of inspiration failed him. Mrs. French did not take even one illuminating notion. When the third week had gone by, something akin to despair seized upon the Inspector. The only possibility of hope now seemed to lie in the return of Arnold Price, and French began counting the days until his arrival.

===Chinese===
可是与他的期望相反，第二天过去了，莱斯考号毫无消息；第三天又过去，此后一连许多天也是如此。他在描图上使尽浑身解数，也没能让那份神秘文书透出半点亮光。日子一天天过去，他越来越消沉，生怕这桩案子要砸在自己手里，这恐惧也一天强似一天。他把难处摊给妻子看，也是枉然。这最后一处灵感的源头，破天荒头一回没了用场——弗伦奇太太连半点有启发的念头也没能想出来。第三个星期过完，一种近乎绝望的情绪攫住了探长。如今唯一的一点指望，似乎就落在阿诺德·普赖斯回国这件事上，弗伦奇掰着指头数他到来的日子。

===Original===
One night about three weeks after his return from Belgium he settled down with a cigar after dinner, his thoughts running in their familiar groove: What were these people engaged on? Was there any way in which he could find out? Had he overlooked any evidence or any inquiries? Had he neglected any possible line of research?

===Chinese===
从比利时回来约莫三个星期后的一个晚上，他饭后点上一支雪茄坐定下来，思绪又淌进那道熟辙：这伙人究竟在干什么？有什么法子查出来？有没有漏掉什么证据、什么查访？有没有忽略什么可行的研究路子？

===Original===
The more he considered the affair in all its bearings, the more conscious he became of the soundness of the advice he had given to Cheyne, and which in his turn he had received from his chief. Unquestionably in the tracing lay the solution he required, and once again he racked his brains to see if he could not by any means devise a way to read its message.

===Chinese===
这件事的方方面面，他越琢磨，越觉得自己给切恩的那条建议——也就是他得自上司的那条——确有道理。毫无疑问，他要的答案就藏在描图里，于是他再一次绞尽脑汁，看能不能想出什么法子，把其中的信息读出来。

===Original===
On this point he concentrated, going over and over again everything he had learned about it. For perhaps an hour he remained motionless in his chair, while the smoke from his cigar curled up and slowly dissolved into the blue haze with which the room was becoming obscured. And then suddenly he sat up and with a dawning, tremulous eagerness considered an idea which had just leaped into his mind.

===Chinese===
他把心思全集中在这上头，把已知的一切翻来覆去过了又过。约莫一个钟头，他在椅子里一动不动，雪茄的烟袅袅升起，缓缓融进渐渐蒙住全屋的蓝色雾霭里。蓦地，他坐直身子，带着初生的、微微发颤的兴奋，捉住刚刚跳进脑海的一个念头。

===Original===
He had suddenly remembered a statement made by Cheyne when he was giving his first rather incoherent account of his adventures. The young man said that it had been arranged between himself and Joan Merrill that if either were lucky enough to get the tracing into his or her possession, the first thing he or she would do would be to photograph it. Now, in juxtaposition with that statement, French recalled the facts, first, that Joan must have reached her flat on the night of her abduction at least several minutes before Blessington and Sime arrived with their car; and secondly, that during those minutes she had the tracing with her﻿—the genuine tracing, as there was every reason to believe. *Had Joan photographed it?*

===Chinese===
他猛然记起，切恩头一回语无伦次地讲述自己历险时说过的一句话。年轻人说，他和琼·梅里尔事先讲定：两人不管哪一个，只要有幸把描图弄到手，头一件事就是把它拍下来。把这句话同几件事实并到一处，弗伦奇想起来了：第一，琼被劫走的那天夜里，她回到寓所，必定比布莱辛顿和赛姆乘车赶到早至少好几分钟；第二，就在那几分钟里，描图正在她身上——而且正如种种理由所示，那是真描图。*琼把它拍下来了吗？*

===Original===
French was overwhelmed with amazement and chagrin at his failure to think of this point before, nor could he acquit Cheyne of a like astounding stupidity. For himself he felt there was no excuse whatever. He had even specially noticed the girl’s camera and the flashlight apparatus which she used for her architectural details when he was searching her rooms, but he had then, and since then up till this moment, entirely and completely forgotten the arrangement made between the partners.

===Chinese===
竟一直没有想到这一层，弗伦奇又是惊愕又是懊恼；切恩同样糊涂得惊人，他想不出该怎样替他开脱。至于他自己，更没有任何借口。他搜查那姑娘房间的时候，还特别留意过她的照相机，以及她拍建筑细部用的闪光灯设备，可当时——此后直到此刻——他把两位合伙人之间的这个约定忘得干干净净。

===Original===
Late as it was, French decided to go then and there to ascertain the point. The key of Joan’s flat was at the Yard, and twenty minutes later he had obtained it and was in a taxi bowling towards Horne Terrace.

===Chinese===
夜虽然深了，弗伦奇还是决定当即就去查明这一点。琼那套寓所的钥匙存在苏格兰场，二十分钟后，他已领到钥匙，坐上出租汽车，向霍恩台一路疾驰而去。

===Original===
He kept the vehicle while he ran up the ten flights to No. 12 and secured the camera. Then hastening down, he was driven back to the Yard.

===Chinese===
他让车等着，自己一口气跑上十段楼梯到 12 室，取到那架照相机，随即匆匆奔下楼，坐车回到苏格兰场。

===Original===
By a piece of good luck he found a photographer who had been delayed by other important work, and him he pressed into the service forthwith. With some grumbling the man returned to his dark room. French, too eager to await his report, accompanying him.

===Chinese===
也是运气好，他找到一位被别的重要活计耽搁住的摄影师，立刻把这人拉来当差。那人嘟嘟囔囔地回了暗房，弗伦奇等不及听回话，也跟了进去。

===Original===
A few moments sufficed to settle the question. The camera contained a roll of films of which the first seven had been exposed, and a short immersion in the developer showed that numbers 5, 6, and 7 bore the hoped for impress.

===Chinese===
不消片刻，问题就见了分晓。相机里有一卷胶卷，头七张已经曝光，在显影液里略一浸，便看出第 5、6、7 三张正带着盼望中的影像。

===Original===
Gone was French’s despondency and the weariness caused by his heavy day, and instead he was once more the embodiment of enthusiasm and cheery optimism. He had it now! At last the secret was within his grasp! Of his ability to read the message, now that he was sure he had the genuine one, he had no doubt. He had always liked working out ciphers, and since he had succeeded in extracting the hidden meaning from the stock and share list which had been sent to the elusive Mrs. X in the Gething murder case, his belief in his own powers had become almost an obsession. He could hardly restrain his eagerness to get to grips with this new problem until the negatives should be dry and prints made.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇的沮丧不见了，一整天的劳累也烟消云散，他重新变回了热情与快活乐观的化身。到手了！秘密终于攥在他掌心里了！既然确信拿到的是真件，他对自己读通信息的能力毫不怀疑。他素来喜欢破解密码，自从在格辛凶案里从寄给那位遍寻不获的X太太的股票行情表中破出隐藏含义之后，他对自身本领的信心几乎成了一种执念。底片要晾干、照片要印出才能动手，他好不容易才按捺住扑向这个新难题的急切。

===Original===
The photographer was able to promise these for the following day, and till then French had to possess his soul in patience. But on his return from lunch he found on his desk three excellent prints of the document.

===Chinese===
摄影师答应第二天把这些交出来，在那之前，弗伦奇只好按捺着性子苦等。可他吃过午饭回来，就见桌上摆着三张印得极好的文书照片。

===Original===
They were only half-plate size, or about one-third that of the tracing which had been given to Cheyne. He therefore instructed the photographer to prepare enlargements which would bring the document up to more nearly the size of the original. These were ready before it was time for him to leave for home, and he sat down with ill-controlled excitement to compare them with the document at which he had already spent so much time.

===Chinese===
照片只有半版（half-plate）大，约合交给切恩的那份描图的三分之一。于是他吩咐摄影师加印放大的，把这份文书放大到接近原件的尺寸。还没到他该回家的时候，放大件已经备齐，他怀着按捺不住的激动坐下来，把它同那份他已耗去许多工夫的文书细加比对。

===Original===
And then he suddenly experienced one of the most bitter disappointments of his life. To all intents and purposes the two were the same! There were the same circles, the same numbers, letters, and signs enclosed therein, the same phrase, “England expects every man to do his duty,” spaced round in the same way! The tracing had not been very accurately done, as some of the circles seemed slightly out of place, but the discrepancies were trifling, and seemed obviously due to careless copying. He gave vent to a single bitter oath, then sat motionless, wrapped in the most profound gloom.

===Chinese===
紧接着，他就尝到了此生最苦涩的一次失望。两件东西实质上毫无二致！同样的圆圈，圈内同样的数字、字母和符号，同样那句“英格兰期望人人恪尽职守”，同样绕成一圈排开！描图摹得不算很准，有些圆圈位置仿佛略略偏了，但出入微不足道，显然只是摹写马虎所致。他狠狠地咒骂了一声，便坐在那里一动不动，陷进最深重的阴郁里。

===Original===
He took tracing and photographs home with him, and spent the greater part of the evening making a minute comparison between the two. The enlargement unfortunately was not exactly the same size as the tracing, and he therefore began his work by covering the surfaces of both with proportionate squares.

===Chinese===
他把描图和照片带回家去，花了晚上大部分时间，将两件东西细细比对。可惜放大件和描图的尺寸不完全一样，于是他动手头一件事，就是在两件东西的表面各打上成比例的方格。

===Original===
Taking the tracing first he drew parallel lines one inch apart both up and down it and across, thus covering its whole surface with inch squares. Then he divided the prints into the same number of equal parts both vertically and horizontally and ruled them up in squares also. These squares were slightly smaller than the others﻿—about seven-eighths of an inch only﻿—but relatively the lines fell on each in the same positions. A comparison according to the squares thus showed at a glance similarity or otherwise between the two documents.

===Chinese===
先拿描图来，他沿纵横两个方向，每隔一英寸画一条平行线，把整个纸面划成一英寸见方的格子。然后他把照片也按纵横两个方向分成同等数目的等份，同样打出方格。这些方格比描图上的略小——每边只有大约八分之七英寸——但相对而言，线条落在各自纸面上的位置是一样的。照着方格一比，两份文书何处相同、何处不同，一眼便见。

===Original===
As he examined them in detail certain interesting facts began to emerge. The general appearance, the words “England expects every man to do his duty,” and the circles with their attendant letters and numbers were identical on both sheets. But there were striking variations. The position of certain of the circles was different. Those containing numbers and crooked lines were all slightly out of place, while those containing letters remained unmoved. Moreover, the little crooked lines, while preserving a rough resemblance to the originals, were altered in shape. The more he considered the matter the more evident it became to French that these divergences were intentional. The tracing which had been given to Cheyne was intended to resemble the other superficially﻿—and did so resemble it, but it had clearly been faked to make it valueless.

===Chinese===
他细细查看之下，几桩有趣的事实渐渐显豁出来。两纸的大体面貌、“英格兰期望人人恪尽职守”的字样、连同圆圈及其旁附的字母数字，全都一模一样。可差异也触目惊心。某些圆圈的位置不同。含数字和折线的圆圈都略略挪了位，含字母的圆圈却原封未动。再者，那些小折线虽然大体仍像原件，形状却改过了。弗伦奇越琢磨越明白：这些歧异是存心弄出来的。交给切恩的那份描图，就是要在外表上同另一份相像——它也确实像——可它分明被做了手脚，叫它一文不值。

===Original===
If French were right so far, and he had but little doubt of it, it followed that the essential feature of the circles and crooked lines was position. This, he felt, should be a useful hint, but as yet he could not see where it led.

===Chinese===
倘若弗伦奇到这里为止都想对了——他对此几乎没有怀疑——那么圆圈和折线的要害便是位置。他觉得这该是一条有用的线索，只是眼下还看不出它通向哪里。

===Original===
He pondered fruitlessly over the problem till the small hours, and next morning he took the documents back to the Yard to continue his studies. But he did not have an opportunity to do so. Other work was waiting for him. To his delight he found that Arnold Price had reached home, and that he and Cheyne were waiting to see him.

===Chinese===
这个问题他徒劳地琢磨到后半夜。第二天早晨，他把文书带回苏格兰场，想接着研究，却没有得到机会——别的工作正等着他。令他大喜的是，阿诺德·普赖斯已经到家，正同切恩一道等着见他。

===Original===
Price proved to be a lanky and rather despondent-looking individual with a skin burned to the color of copper and a pair of exceedingly shrewd blue eyes. He dropped into the chair French indicated, and instantly pulled out and lit a well-blackened cutty pipe.

===Chinese===
普赖斯原来是个瘦长个子、神情颇为委靡的人，皮肤晒成了铜色，一双蓝眼睛精明透顶。他一屁股坐进弗伦奇指给他的椅子，立刻掏出一支熏得乌黑的短柄陶土烟斗，点着了。

===Original===
“Got in yesterday morning,” he announced laconically, “and wired Torquay I was going down. By the merest luck I got a reply before I started that Cheyne was in town. I looked him up and here I am.”

===Chinese===
“昨天上午到的。”他言简意赅地说。“拍了电报给托基，说要回去。动身之前纯属碰巧得了回电，说切恩正在城里。我寻到他，这就来了。”

===Original===
French smiled pleasantly. Though interested in the man, he could not help noting with some amusement at once the restraint and the completeness of his statement. How refreshing, he thought, and how rare, to meet someone who will give you the pith of a story without frills!

===Chinese===
弗伦奇愉快地微微一笑。他虽然对这个人感兴趣，却不免带着几分好笑，同时注意到这话说得多么有分寸、又多么周全。他想，遇上一个能把一件事的要点和盘托出、不添枝加叶的人，多么提神，又多么难得！

===Original===
“I’m glad to meet you, Mr. Price,” he said cordially. “I suppose Mr. Cheyne has told you the effect that your letter has had on us all?”

===Chinese===
“很高兴见到您，普赖斯先生。”他热诚地说。“您那封信在我们大家身上起了什么作用，切恩先生想必已经告诉您了吧？”

===Original===
The other nodded.

===Chinese===
对方点了点头。

===Original===
“Not altogether surprising,” he declared. “There’s money in the thing﻿—or so I always believed, and this other crowd must believe it too; though how they got on to the affair licks me.”

===Chinese===
“这倒不全出乎意料。”他声明道。“这件事里头有钱——至少我一向这么相信，那边那伙人也必定这么信；只是他们怎么盯上这件事的，我就想不明白了。”

===Original===
“We shall be very much interested to hear what you can tell us about it,” French prompted. “Will you smoke, Mr. Cheyne?” He held out his cigar case.

===Chinese===
“您能讲些什么，我们极愿洗耳恭听。”弗伦奇引他往下说。“抽烟吗，切恩先生？”他递出雪茄烟盒。

===Original===
“I can’t tell you much,” Price returned, “and nothing that will clear up this blessed mystery that seems to have started up. But this is my story for what it’s worth. Before the war I was on one of the Hudson and Spence boats and I had the luck to get into the R.N.R. when hostilities broke out. I stayed on in my old ship till she was torpedoed a couple of years later, then I was appointed third officer on the *Maurania*. We were on a trip from South Africa to Brest with army stores, when one day, just as we came into the English Channel, we were attacked by a U-boat. We had an 18-pounder forward, and by a stroke of luck we gave old Fritz one on the knob that did him in. The boat went down and a dozen of the crew were left swimming. We put out a boat and picked one or two of them up. The skipper was clinging on to a lifebelt, but just as we came up he let go and began to sink. I was in charge of the boat, and some fool notion came over me﻿—I think in the hurry I forgot he was a U-boat skipper﻿—but anyhow like a fool I got overboard and got hold of him. It was nothing like a dramatic rescue﻿—there was no danger to me﻿—and we were back on board inside fifteen minutes.”

===Chinese===
“我讲不出多少东西。”普赖斯答道，“也讲不出什么能解开这个凭空冒出来的鬼谜团的东西。不过我的经历就在这里，有用没用随它去。战前我在哈德逊-斯宾斯（Hudson and Spence）商行的船上做事，开战时有幸进了皇家海军后备队（R.N.R.）。我在原来那条船上接着干，直到两年后她被鱼雷击沉，随后我被派到毛拉尼亚号上任三副。我们载着军需品从南非开往布雷斯特，有一天刚进英吉利海峡，一艘U型潜艇袭击了我们。我们舰首有一门 18 磅炮，运气好，一炮正敲在老弗里茨的天灵盖上，结果了它。潜艇沉了，十来个艇员在水里挣扎。我们放下一只小艇，捞起来一两个。那个艇长抓着一只救生圈，可我们刚划到跟前，他一撒手就往下沉。当时我管那条小艇，也不知哪来的糊涂念头——我想是情急之下忘了他是U型潜艇的艇长——总之我像个傻瓜似的翻出船去，抓住了他。这算不上什么戏剧性的营救——我毫无危险——不到一刻钟，我们就回到了大船上。”

===Original===
French and Cheyne were listening intently to this familiar story. So far it was almost word for word that told by Dangle. Apparently, then, there was at least one point on which the latter had told the truth.

===Chinese===
这个故事弗伦奇和切恩都听过，此刻仍听得聚精会神。到这里为止，它同丹格尔讲的几乎一字不差。可见丹格尔至少在一点上说了实话。

===Original===
“We weren’t out of trouble,” Price resumed, “and next day we came up against another submarine. We exchanged a few shots and then a British destroyer came up and drove him off. But I had the luck to stop a splinter of shell, and when we got to Brest I was sent to hospital. The U-boat skipper had got a crack on the head when his boat went down, and he was sent in too. By a chance we got side by side beds in the same ward, and used to talk a bit, though he was a rotter, even for a Boche.”

===Chinese===
“我们的麻烦还没有完。”普赖斯接着讲。“第二天我们又撞上一艘潜艇。互相开了几炮，随后一艘英国驱逐舰赶到，把它赶跑了。可我有幸挨了一块弹片，船到布雷斯特后，我被送进了医院。那个U型潜艇艇长的艇沉时头上磕了一下，也给送了进来。碰巧我们的病床在同一间病房里挨着，不时聊上几句——虽然即便按德国佬的标准衡量，他也是个人渣。”

===Original===
Price paused to draw on his cutty pipe, expelling great clouds of smoke of a peculiarly acrid and penetrating quality. Then, the others not speaking, he went on:

===Chinese===
普赖斯停下来吸他的短柄烟斗，吐出一团团味道特别辛辣刺鼻的浓烟。见另外两人不言语，他接着说：

===Original===
“It turned out that the wound on Schulz’s head﻿—his name was Schulz﻿—was serious, and he grew steadily worse. Then one night when the ward was quiet, he woke me and said he knew his number was up and that he had a secret to tell me. We listened, but all the other fellows seemed asleep, and then he told me he could put me in the way of a fortune﻿—that he had hoped to get it himself after the war, but now that it would be a job for someone else. He said he would tell me the whole thing, and that I might make what I could out of it, if only I would pledge myself to give one-eighth of what I got to his wife. He gave me the address﻿—somewhere in Breslau. He asked me to swear this and I did, and then he took a packet from under his pillow and handed it to me. ‘There,’ he said, ‘the whole thing’s there. I put it in cipher for safety, but I’ll tell you how to read it.’ Well, he began to do so, but just then a sister came in, and he shut up till she would leave. But the excitement of talking about the thing must have been too much for him. He got a weak turn and never spoke again.”

===Chinese===
“原来舒尔茨头上那处伤——他姓舒尔茨——伤得不轻，人一天不如一天。一天夜里，病房里静悄悄的，他把我叫醒，说他知道自己大限将至，有一桩秘密要告诉我。我们侧耳听了听，别的人似乎都睡着了，他便对我说，他能指给我一条发财的路子——他本指望战后自己去取，如今只好留给别人去干了。他说愿把整件事原原本本告诉我，我能从中捞到多少算多少，只要我立誓把所得的八分之一给他的妻子。他把地址给了我——在布雷斯劳（Breslau）什么地方。他要我起誓，我起了，随后他从枕头底下摸出一个小包，递到我手里。‘喏，’他说，‘全部东西都在这里头。为保险起见，我把它写成了密码，回头我教你怎样读。’就这样，他开始讲解，可恰在那时一位护士进来，他便住口等她走。想是谈这件事太激动了，他一阵衰弱，从此再没能开口。”

===Original===
“But,” Cheyne interposed, “what about the hard copper? Dangle told us about Schulz’s discovery.”

===Chinese===
“可是，”切恩插话道，“硬铜是怎么回事？丹格尔给我们讲过舒尔茨的发现。”

===Original===
Price gazed at him vacantly for some moments and then suddenly smote the table.

===Chinese===
普赖斯茫然地瞪了他片刻，猛地一巴掌拍在桌上。

===Original===
“I’ve got it!” he cried with an oath. “Dangle! I remember that chap now! He was in the next bed on the other side of Schulz. That’s right! I couldn’t call him to mind when you mentioned him before. Of course! He heard the whole tale, and that’s what started him on this do.”

===Chinese===
“想起来了！”他骂了一句，喊道。“丹格尔！我现在记起那家伙了！他就在舒尔茨另一边的邻床。没错！方才你们提起他，我怎么也想不起来。原来如此！他听去了全部的话，这就是他干这档子事的起头！”

===Original===
“I know,” Cheyne returned. “He admitted that all right. But he told us about the hard copper. You haven’t mentioned that.”

===Chinese===
“这我知道。”切恩答道。“这一点他也痛快承认了。可他还给我们讲了硬铜的事。您没提到这个。”

===Original===
Price shook his head.

===Chinese===
普赖斯摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Don’t know what you’re talking about,” he declared. “What do you mean by hard copper?”

===Chinese===
“不知道你们在说什么。”他声明道。“你们说的硬铜是什么意思？”

===Original===
“Dangle mentioned it. He was listening to the conversation. He told us all that about Schulz’s story of the fortune, and about his wife and all that, just as you have, but he said Schulz went on to explain what the fortune was: that he had hit on a way of treating copper that made it as hard as steel. The cipher contained the formula.”

===Chinese===
“丹格尔提到过。他偷听了那番谈话。舒尔茨讲横财、讲他妻子等等那些话，他一五一十都告诉了我们，同您讲的分毫不差，只是他说舒尔茨还接着讲了那笔财是什么：说他碰上了一种炼铜的法子，能把铜炼得同钢一样硬。密码里装的就是那个配方。”

===Original===
Again Price shook his head.

===Chinese===
普赖斯又摇了摇头。

===Original===
“All spoof,” he observed. “Not a word of truth in it. Schulz never mentioned copper or said anything more than I’ve told you.”

===Chinese===
“纯属瞎编。”他说道。“没有半个字是真的。舒尔茨根本没提过铜，除了我告诉你们的，什么也没多说。”

===Original===
French spoke for the first time.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇第一次开口。

===Original===
“We found this Dangle a man of imagination, all through, and it is easy to see why he invented that particular yarn. By that time he had undoubtedly read the cipher, and he wanted something to mislead Mr. Cheyne as to its contents. The story of the hard copper would start a bias in Mr. Cheyne’s mind which would tend to keep him off the real scent.” He paused, but his companions not speaking, continued: “Now we have that bias cleared away, at least one interesting fact emerges. The whole business starts with the sea﻿—the U-boat commander, Schulz, and it looks as if it was going to end up with the sea, the tramp, the *L’Escaut*.”

===Chinese===
“我们发现这个丹格尔自始至终都是个富于想象的人，他为什么要编那套瞎话，也不难明白。到那时他无疑已经读通了密码，需要点东西，好把切恩先生对密码内容的想法引上岔路。硬铜的故事会在切恩先生心里种下一个先入之见，把他引离真正的线索。”他顿了顿，见两位同伴没有言语，又接着说：“如今这个先入之见扫除了，至少有一桩有趣的事实显露出来。整件事从海上起头——U型潜艇艇长舒尔茨；而看样子，它还要在海上收场——那条货轮，莱斯考号。”

===Original===
As French said these words an idea flashed into his mind, and he went on deliberately, but with growing excitement:

===Chinese===
这些话出口的同时，弗伦奇脑海里电光一闪，他放慢语速，却带着越来越浓的兴奋接着说：

===Original===
“And when we connect the idea of a U-boat commander giving a message which ends with a sea expedition, with the fact, which I have just discovered, that the essence of his cipher is the *position* of the markings on it, we seem to be getting somewhere.”

===Chinese===
“一位U型潜艇艇长传下一份信息，而这信息的结局是一次出海远征；把这个想法，同我刚发现的事实——他的密码的要害在于记号的*位置*——联系到一起，我们似乎摸到门道了。”

===Original===
Price smote his thigh.

===Chinese===
普赖斯一巴掌拍在大腿上。

===Original===
“By Jemima!” he cried. “I’ve got you. That blessed tracing is a map!”

===Chinese===
“我的老天！”他喊道。“我明白了。那张要命的描图是一幅地图！”

===Original===
“A map, yes. That’s what I think,” French answered eagerly, and then as suddenly he saw the possible significance of Nelson’s exhortation, he went on dramatically: “A map of England!”

===Chinese===
“一幅地图，正是。我也这么想。”弗伦奇急切地应道，随即又猛然悟出纳尔逊那句训勉可能的深意，便加重语气接着说：“一幅英格兰的地图！”

===Original===
Cheyne swore softly.

===Chinese===
切恩低低地咒骂了一声。

===Original===
“My word, if we aren’t a set of blithering idiots!” he exclaimed. “Of course! ‘England’ is the title. That’s as clear as day! The other words are added as a blind. Let’s have the thing out, Inspector, and see if we can’t make something of it now.”

===Chinese===
“我的天，咱们这一伙可真是十足的蠢货！”他叫道。“可不是嘛！‘英格兰’就是图名。这明明白白！其余的字是添上去障眼的。探长，把东西拿出来吧，看看这回咱们能不能琢磨出个名堂。”

===Original===
As French produced his enlarged photographs not one of the three men doubted that they were at last well on the way towards wresting the secret from the document which had so long baffled them.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇取出放大照片，三个人没有一个怀疑：那份久久难倒他们的文书，其中的秘密，他们终于就要稳稳到手了。

## XIX The Message of the Tracing / 十九 描图的讯息

===Original===
Inspector French spread the photograph on his desk, and Cheyne and Price having drawn up chairs, all three gazed at it as if expecting that in the light of their great idea its message would have become obvious.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇探长把照片摊开在办公桌上，切恩和普赖斯拉过椅子凑上前，三个人一同凝视着它，仿佛指望经他们那个高明主意一照，照片的讯息便会豁然显明。

===Original===
But in this they were disappointed. The suggestion did not seem in any way to help either French or Cheyne, and Price, who of course had not seen the document before, was satisfactorily mystified. Granted that the thing was a map, granted even that it was a map of England, its meaning remained just as provokingly hidden as ever.

===Chinese===
但这一回他们失望了。那个设想似乎无论如何也帮不上弗伦奇和切恩的忙，而普赖斯——他先前当然从未见过这份文书——则被它弄得满头雾水，这效果倒也“令人满意”。就算这东西是幅地图，就算它甚至是幅英国地图，它的含义依旧同先前一样，藏得叫人干着急。

===Original===
Presently Price gave vent to an exclamation. “Hang it all!” he cried irritably, and then: “I suppose those numbers couldn’t be soundings? Could they give depths at the circles?”

===Chinese===
过了一会儿，普赖斯发出一声惊叹。“见鬼！”他烦躁地喊道，接着又说：“我想，那些数字总不会是测深读数吧？会不会是标出各个圆圈处的水深？”

===Original===
“That’s an idea,” Cheyne cried, but French shook his head.

===Chinese===
“这倒是个思路。”切恩喊道，但弗伦奇摇了摇头。

===Original===
“I think there’s more in it than that,” he observed. “If you examine those numbers you’ll find that they’re consecutive, they run from one to thirty-six. Soundings wouldn’t lend themselves to such an arrangement. You may be right, Mr. Price, and we must keep your idea in view, but I don’t see it working out for the moment.”

===Chinese===
“我看这里头的名堂不止于此。”他说道。“细看那些数字您就会发现，它们是连续的，从一排到三十六。测深读数可排不成这种阵势。您也许说得对，普赖斯先生，这个想法我们得记在心上，不过眼下我看它行不通。”

===Original===
Silence reigned for a few moments, then Price sat back from the table and spoke again.

===Chinese===
一时无人作声，随后普赖斯从桌上直起身子，往椅背上一靠，又开口了。

===Original===
“Look here, Inspector,” he said, knocking the ashes out of his pipe and beginning to fill it with his strong, black mixture, “you said something just now I didn’t quite follow. Let’s get your notion clear. You talked of this thing beginning with the sea﻿—at Schulz, and ending with the sea﻿—at *L’Escaut*, and Schulz’s message being a map. Just what was in your mind?”

===Chinese===
“听着，探长，”他说着，在烟斗上磕掉烟灰，动手往里装他那浓烈的黑色板烟，“您刚才说的话我没能完全听懂。咱们把您的想法摆摆明白。您说这件事以海起头——在舒尔茨那里，又以海收尾——在莱斯考号上，还说舒尔茨的遗言是一幅地图。您心里究竟是怎么想的？”

===Original===
“Only the obvious suggestion that if you leave a message which provokes an expedition, you must also convey in your message the destination of that expedition, and a map seems the simplest way of doing it. But on second thoughts I question my first conclusion. There must be an explanation of the secret as well as a direction of how to profit by it, and it would seem to me doubtful that such an explanation could be covered by a map.”

===Chinese===
“无非是那个浅显的念头：假如你留下的一份讯息会招来一趟远征，你就必须在讯息里交代那趟远征的目的地，而地图似乎是办这件事最省事的法子。不过再一细想，我又怀疑起自己最初的结论了。这秘密既须有个说明，又须有一条如何从中取利的指引；这样一份说明能否被一幅地图涵盖，我看靠不住。”

===Original===
“Sounds all right, that,” Price admitted. “Have you any idea what the secret might be? Sounds like treasure or salvage or something of that kind.”

===Chinese===
“这话在理。”普赖斯承认道。“这秘密可能是什么，您心里有数吗？听上去像是宝藏啦、打捞啦一类的名堂。”

===Original===
“I scarcely think salvage,” French answered. “The *L’Escaut* is not a salvage boat, and a boat not specially fitted for the purpose would be of little use. But I thought of treasure all right. This Schulz might have robbed his ships﻿—there would always be money aboard, and even during the war many women traveled with jewelry. The man might easily have made a cache of valuables somewhere round the coast.”

===Chinese===
“打捞我看不像。”弗伦奇答道。“莱斯考号不是打捞船，一条不是专门为这行装备起来的船派不上多大用场。不过宝藏嘛，我确实想到了。这个舒尔茨没准劫掠过他经手的船——船上总归有钱，大战年月里连许多妇女都随身带着珠宝。这家伙满可以沿岸找个地方，窖藏下一批值钱之物。”

===Original===
“Easily,” Cheyne intervened, “or he might have learned of some valuable deposit in some out of the way cove round the coast, like those chaps in that clinking tale of Maurice Drake’s, *WO2*.”

===Chinese===
“容易得很，”切恩插话道，“再不然，他也可能是打听到了沿岸哪个偏僻小湾里藏着一宗值钱的东西，就像莫里斯·德雷克（Maurice Drake）那篇顶呱呱的小说《WO2》里那帮家伙一样。”

===Original===
“As at Terneuzen?” said French. “I read that book﻿—one of the best I ever came across. It’s a possibility, of course.”

===Chinese===
“就像泰尔讷曾（Terneuzen）那种地方？”弗伦奇说。“那本书我读过——是我读过的最好读物之一。这当然是一种可能。”

===Original===
The talk here became somewhat rambling, Price not having read *WO2* and wanting to know what it was about, but French soon reverted to his photograph. He reminded his hearers that they were all interested in its elucidation. Miss Merrill’s safety, his own professional credit, Cheyne’s peace of mind, and Price’s fortune, all were at stake.

===Chinese===
谈话到这里有些跑了野马——普赖斯没读过《WO2》，想知道它讲的是什么——但弗伦奇很快又回到他的照片上。他提醒两位听客：把这张照片琢磨透，与他们个个利害攸关。梅里尔小姐的安危、他自己的职业体面、切恩的心神安宁、普赖斯的身家富贵，全都押在上面了。

===Original===
“We have,” he went on, “evolved the idea that perhaps this tracing may be a map of England. On further thought that suggestion does not seem promising, but as we have no other let us work on it. Assume it is a map of England, and let us see if it leads us anywhere.” There were murmurs of assent from his hearers, and he continued: “Now it seems to me the first thing to do is to try if we can fit these circles and lines into the map of England. Is there anything corresponding to them in English geography?”

===Chinese===
“我们刚才，”他接着说，“琢磨出了这样一个想法：这张描图也许是幅英国地图。再想想，这设想不见得有多大指望，可咱们手头别无线索，不妨就顺着它做。姑且假定它是幅英国地图，看看能不能把咱们引到哪里。”两位听客低声应和，他便继续说道：“眼下我看，头一件事是试试能不能把这些圆圈和线条套进英国地图。英国地理里有没有跟它们对得上的东西？”

===Original===
No one being able to answer this query, French went on:

===Chinese===
这个问题谁也答不上来，弗伦奇便接着往下说：

===Original===
“I think we must distinguish between the letter circles on the one hand and those of the numbers and lines on the other. The position of the former was not altered in the faked copy; that of the latter was. From this may we not assume that the message lies in the numbers and lines only? Possibly the letters were added as a blind, as we have already assumed the words ‘expects every man to do his duty’ were added as a blind to ‘England.’ Suppose at all events that we eliminate the letter circles and concentrate on the others for our first effort?”

===Chinese===
“我看咱们得把字母圆圈跟数字、线条圆圈区分开来。前者的位置在伪造件里没有变动；后者的位置动过了。由此咱们何不推断：讯息只在数字和线条里？那些字母也许是外加的障眼法，就像我们已经认定‘期望人人恪尽职守’那几个词是加在‘英格兰’上的障眼法一样。无论如何，第一步先把字母圆圈撇开，专攻其余那些，怎么样？”

===Original===
“That sounds all right.”

===Chinese===
“听着没毛病。”

===Original===
“Good. Then let us go a step further. Have you noticed the distribution of the numbers, letters and lines? The numbers are bunched, roughly speaking, towards the center, the letters round the edge, and the irregular lines between the two. Does this central mass give us anything?”

===Chinese===
“好。那就再进一步。数字、字母和线条的分布，你们留意过没有？大体说来，数字抱团在中部，字母环在边缘，不规则线条夹在两者之间。居中这一大团，能给我们点什么吗？”

===Original===
“I get you,” Price replied. He had risen and begun to pace the room, but now he returned to the table and stood looking down at the photograph. “You know, as a matter of fact,” he went on slowly, “if, as you say, you take that central part which contains numbers only, the shape of the thing is not so very unlike England after all. Suppose the numbers represent land and the letters sea. Then this patch of letters in the top left-hand corner might be the Irish Sea, and this larger patch to the right the North Sea. And look, the letter circles form a band across the bottom. What price that for the English Channel?”

===Chinese===
“我明白您的意思。”普赖斯答道。他本已起身在屋里踱步，这时回到桌旁，站着俯视那张照片。“您知道，说实在的，”他缓缓地接着说，“照您说的，单取当中那个只含数字的部分，这东西的形状其实并不那么不像英国。假定数字代表陆地、字母代表海。那么左上角这一片字母就是爱尔兰海，右边这片大些的就是北海。再看，字母圆圈在底部连成一带。拿这一带当英吉利海峡，诸位说值不值？”

===Original===
French crossed the room, and taking a small atlas from a shelf, opened it at the map of England and laid it down beside the photograph. With a rising excitement all three compared them. Then Cheyne burst out irritably:

===Chinese===
弗伦奇走到房间另一头，从架子上取下一本小地图册，翻到英国那页地图，摊在照片旁边。三个人怀着节节升高的兴奋把它们比来比去。随后切恩烦躁地嚷了起来：

===Original===
“Confound the thing! It’s like it and it’s not like it. Let’s draw a line round those number circles and see if it makes anything like the shape.” He seized the photograph and took out a pencil.

===Chinese===
“这劳什子！说像又不像。咱们沿那些数字圆圈画条线，看看画出来像不像那个形状。”他抓过照片，掏出一支铅笔。

===Original===
But just as in the scientific and industrial worlds discoveries and inventions seldom come singly, so among these three men the begetting of ideas begot more ideas. Scarcely had Cheyne spoken when French made a little gesture of comprehension.

===Chinese===
不过，正如在科学界和实业界，发现与发明很少单独降临，在这三个人中间，念头的孕育也会催生更多念头。切恩话音未落，弗伦奇已做了一个恍然的小手势。

===Original===
“I believe I have it at last,” he said quietly but with ill-concealed eagerness in his tones. “Those irregular lines in certain of the circles are broken bits of the coast line. See here, those two between 8 and *U* are surely the Wash, and that below *H* is Flamborough Head. Let’s see if we can locate correspondingly shaped outlines on the atlas, and fill in between those on the photograph with pencil.”

===Chinese===
“我相信我终于把它抓住了。”他语气平静，声调里却掩不住急切。“某些圆圈里那些不规则线条，是海岸线的碎段。瞧这儿，8 和 U 之间那两条准是沃什湾（the Wash），H 下面那道是弗兰伯勒角（Flamborough Head）。咱们看看能不能在地图册上找到形状相应的轮廓，再用铅笔把照片上这些地方之间的海岸线补上。”

===Original===
A few seconds’ examination only were needed. Opposite, but slightly above the projection which French suggested as Flamborough Head was an angled line between *GU* and 31 which all three simultaneously pronounced St. Bee’s Head. Short double lines on each side of 24 showed two parts of the estuary of the Severn, and projections along the bottom near *X* and 27 were evidently St. Alban’s Head and Selsey Bill.

===Chinese===
只消几秒钟的查验。在弗伦奇认作弗兰伯勒角的那道突出部的对面、略微偏上处，GU 和 31 之间有一道折线，三个人不约而同断定是圣比斯角（St. Bee’s Head）。24 两侧的短短双线显示出塞文河（the Severn）河口的两个部分，底部靠近 X 和 27 的几道突出部，则显然是圣奥尔本斯角（St. Alban’s Head）和塞尔西角（Selsey Bill）。

===Original===
That they were on the right track there could now no longer be any doubt, and they set themselves with renewed energy to the problem still remaining﻿—the meaning of the circles and the numbers they contained.

===Chinese===
走对了路子，这一点如今再无可疑。于是他们抖擞精神，扑向尚未解决的难题——圆圈及圈中数字的含义。

===Original===
“We can’t locate the blessed things this way,” French pointed out. “We’ll have to rule squares on the atlas to correspond. Then we can pencil in the coast line accurately, and see just where the circles lie.”

===Chinese===
“这么着没法把这些劳什子定准。”弗伦奇指出。“得在地图册上相应地打出方格。然后就能用铅笔把海岸线描准，看清圆圈究竟落在哪里。”

===Original===
For a time measuring and the drawing of lines were the order of the day. And then at last the positions of the circles were located. They were all drawn round towns.

===Chinese===
有一阵子，量尺寸、画线条成了正经活计。最后，各个圆圈的位置终于定了下来。它们全都是圈着城镇画的。

===Original===
“Towns!” Price exclaimed. “Guess we’re getting on.”

===Chinese===
“城镇！”普赖斯叫出声来。“依我看，咱们有进展了。”

===Original===
“Towns!” Cheyne echoed in his turn. “Then you must have been right, Inspector, about those letters being merely a blind.”

===Chinese===
“城镇！”切恩也跟着应了一声。“那么探长，您说那些字母纯属障眼法，必定是说对了。”

===Original===
“I think so,” French admitted. “Look at it in this way. If only the towns and coast were marked, the shape of England would show too clearly. But adding those letter circles disguises the thing﻿—prevents the shape becoming apparent. Now, I may be wrong, but I am beginning to question very much if this map has anything to do with indicating a position﻿—I mean directly. I am beginning to think it is merely a cipher. Let us test this at all events. Let us write down the names of the towns in the order of the numbers and see if that gives us anything.”

===Chinese===
“我也这么想。”弗伦奇承认道。“这么看吧。假如标出来的只有城镇和海岸，英国的形状就未免太清楚了。可加上那些字母圆圈，就把这东西伪装起来——叫形状露不出形迹。当然，我或许错了，可我已开始十分怀疑，这幅地图究竟同标示位置有没有关系——我是说直接标示。我渐渐觉得它只是一份密码。无论如何咱们来验证一番。把城镇名称按数字顺序写下来，看看能得出什么。”

===Original===
He took a sheet of paper, while Price found No. 1 on the photograph and Cheyne identified its position with that of a town on the atlas map.

===Chinese===
他取过一张纸；普赖斯在照片上找出 1 号，切恩把它的位置同地图册上的某座城镇一一对应。

===Original===
“No. 1,” said Cheyne, “is Salisbury.”

===Chinese===
“1 号，”切恩说，“是索尔兹伯里（Salisbury）。”

===Original===
French wrote down: “1, Salisbury.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇写下：“1，索尔兹伯里。”

===Original===
“No. 2,” went on Cheyne, “is Immingham.”

===Chinese===
“2 号，”切恩接着说，“是伊明厄姆（Immingham）。”

===Original===
“2, Immingham,” wrote French, as he remarked, “Salisbury﻿—Immingham: *S*﻿—*I*. That goes all right so far.”

===Chinese===
“2，伊明厄姆。”弗伦奇一面写一面说，“索尔兹伯里——伊明厄姆：S——I。到这一步还顺。”

===Original===
The next three towns were Liverpool, Uttoxeter, and Reading, and though none of the men could see where **silur** was leading, it was at least pronounceable.

===Chinese===
接下来三座城是利物浦、厄托克塞特（Uttoxeter）和雷丁（Reading）。三个人虽然都看不出 **silur** 会引向何方，但至少拼得出口。

===Original===
But when the next three letters were added French gave a mighty shout of victory. No. 6 was Ipswich, No. 7 Andover, and No. 8 Nottingham. **ian** added to **silur** made Silurian.

===Chinese===
可等接下来三个字母一添上，弗伦奇便迸出一声胜利的大吼。6 号是伊普斯威奇（Ipswich），7 号是安德沃（Andover），8 号是诺丁汉（Nottingham）。**ian** 加在 **silur** 之后，拼出了 Silurian（西卢里亚号）。

===Original===
“*Silurian!*” French cried, striking the table a mighty blow with his clenched fist. “*Silurian!* That begins to show a light!”

===Chinese===
“西卢里亚号！”弗伦奇喊道，攥起的拳头在桌上重重一击。“西卢里亚号！这下开始透出光亮了！”

===Original===
The others stared.

===Chinese===
另外两人瞪着他。

===Original===
“Don’t you recognize the name?” went on French. “The *Silurian* was a big Anchor liner, and she was torpedoed on her way to the States with two and a half millions in gold bars aboard!”

===Chinese===
“你们不认得这个名字吗？”弗伦奇接着说。“西卢里亚号是安卡班轮公司（Anchor Line）的一条大邮轮，她载着 250 万英镑的金条在开往美国途中被鱼雷击沉了！”

===Original===
The others held their breath and their eyes grew round.

===Chinese===
另外两人屏住呼吸，眼睛都瞪圆了。

===Original===
“Any of it recovered?”

===Chinese===
“金子打捞上来过吗？”

===Original===
“None: it was in mid-Atlantic.”

===Chinese===
“没有：那是在大西洋中部。”

===Original===
“But,” stammered Cheyne at last, “I don’t follow﻿—”

===Chinese===
“可是，”切恩终于结结巴巴地说，“我还是跟不上——”

===Original===
“I don’t follow myself,” French returned briskly, “but when the cipher which leads to a maritime expedition begins with a wreck with two and a half millions aboard, well then, I say it is suggestive. Come along, let’s read the rest of the thing. We’ll know more then.”

===Chinese===
“我自己也还没想通。”弗伦奇干脆利落地答道，“不过，凡是引向一次海上远征的密码，开篇便是一条载着 250 万英镑的沉船——那么我说，这就大有意味。来，把余下的读完。到时候咱们就知道得更多了。”

===Original===
With breathless eagerness the other towns were looked up, and at last French’s list read as follows:

===Chinese===
其余城镇也一个个屏着气急急查了出来，最后弗伦奇的名单如下：

===Original===
1. Salisbury

===Chinese===
1. 索尔兹伯里

===Original===
2. Immingham

===Chinese===
2. 伊明厄姆

===Original===
3. Liverpool

===Chinese===
3. 利物浦

===Original===
4. Uttoxeter

===Chinese===
4. 厄托克塞特

===Original===
5. Reading

===Chinese===
5. 雷丁

===Original===
6. Ipswich

===Chinese===
6. 伊普斯威奇

===Original===
7. Andover

===Chinese===
7. 安德沃

===Original===
8. Nottingham

===Chinese===
8. 诺丁汉

===Original===
9. Oxford

===Chinese===
9. 牛津

===Original===
10. Northampton

===Chinese===
10. 北安普顿

===Original===
11. Evesham

===Chinese===
11. 伊夫沙姆

===Original===
12. Doncaster

===Chinese===
12. 唐克斯特

===Original===
13. Exeter

===Chinese===
13. 埃克塞特

===Original===
14. Gloucester

===Chinese===
14. 格洛斯特

===Original===
15. Ripon

===Chinese===
15. 里彭

===Original===
16. Ely

===Chinese===
16. 伊利

===Original===
17. Eastbourne

===Chinese===
17. 伊斯特本

===Original===
18. Wigan

===Chinese===
18. 威根

===Original===
19. Exmouth

===Chinese===
19. 埃克斯茅斯

===Original===
20. Swansea

===Chinese===
20. 斯旺西

===Original===
21. Tonbridge

===Chinese===
21. 汤布里奇

===Original===
22. Nuneaton

===Chinese===
22. 纳尼顿

===Original===
23. Ilfracombe

===Chinese===
23. 伊尔弗勒科姆

===Original===
24. Newport

===Chinese===
24. 纽波特

===Original===
25. Eaglescliff

===Chinese===
25. 伊格尔斯克利夫

===Original===
26. Taunton

===Chinese===
26. 汤顿

===Original===
27. Eastleigh

===Chinese===
27. 伊斯特利

===Original===
28. Ebbw Vale

===Chinese===
28. 埃布韦尔

===Original===
29. Northallerton

===Chinese===
29. 诺思阿勒顿

===Original===
30. Folkestone

===Chinese===
30. 福克斯通

===Original===
31. Appleby

===Chinese===
31. 阿普尔比

===Original===
32. Tamworth

===Chinese===
32. 塔姆沃思

===Original===
33. Huntingdon

===Chinese===
33. 亨廷登

===Original===
34. Oldham

===Chinese===
34. 奥尔德姆

===Original===
35. Middlesborough

===Chinese===
35. 米德尔斯伯勒

===Original===
36. Southend

===Chinese===
36. 绍森德

===Original===
Taking the initials in order read: Silurianonedegreewestnineteenfathoms, or dividing it into its obvious words﻿—“*Silurian* one degree west nineteen fathoms.”

===Chinese===
按顺序取首字母连读，便是：Silurianonedegreewestnineteenfathoms；再按显明的词断开——“西卢里亚号，向西一度，十九英寻”。

===Original===
The three men stared at one another.

===Chinese===
三个人你看看我，我看看你。

===Original===
“Nineteen fathoms!” Price gasped at last. “But if she’s in nineteen fathoms that gold will be salvable!”

===Chinese===
“十九英寻！”普赖斯终于喘着气说道。“可她要是只沉在十九英寻深的水里，那批金子就捞得上来！”

===Original===
French nodded.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇点了点头。

===Original===
“And I guess Dangle and Company have gone to salve it. They wouldn’t want a salvage boat for gold. They’d get it with a diver’s outfit.”

===Chinese===
“我猜丹格尔一伙就是去打捞它了。捞金子用不着打捞船。他们带上潜水员的行头就办了。”

===Original===
“But,” Cheyne went on in a puzzled tone, “I’ve not got this straight yet. If she’s in nineteen fathoms, why has she not been salved by the Admiralty? Look at the *Laurentic*. She was put down off the Swilly in Ireland, and they salved her gold. Five million pounds’ worth. Salved practically every penny, and in twenty fathoms too.”

===Chinese===
“可是，”切恩困惑地接着说，“这一层我还没想通。她要是只沉在十九英寻的水里，海军部怎么没去打捞？看看劳伦蒂克号（the Laurentic）吧。她沉在爱尔兰的斯威利（the Swilly）湾外，他们把她的金子捞了上来。价值 500 万英镑。几乎一个便士不落全捞了回来，而且那还是在二十英寻深的水里。”

===Original===
Price was considering another problem.

===Chinese===
普赖斯正琢磨着另一个问题。

===Original===
“One degree west,” he murmured. “What under heaven does that mean? One degree west of what? Surely not the meridian of Greenwich. If so, what is the latitude: there’s no mention of it?”

===Chinese===
“向西一度，”他喃喃道。“老天爷，这到底是什么意思？从什么东西向西一度？总不会是从格林尼治子午线起算吧。果真如此，纬度又是多少：怎么只字不提？”

===Original===
French could not answer either of the questions, and he did not try. Instead he picked up his telephone receiver and made a call.

===Chinese===
这两个问题弗伦奇一个也答不上来，他也不试。他拿起电话听筒，拨了个电话。

===Original===
“Hallo! Is that Lloyd’s? Put me through to the Record Department, please﻿ ﻿… Is Mr. Sam Pullar there? Tell him Inspector French of Scotland Yard wants to speak to him﻿ ﻿… Hallo, Sam!﻿ ﻿… Yes﻿ ﻿… Haven’t seen you for ages﻿ ﻿… Look here, Sam, I want you to do me a favor. It’s rather urgent, and I’d be grateful if you could look after it just now.﻿ ﻿… Yes, I’ll hold on. I want to know anything you can tell me about the sinking of the *Silurian*. You remember, she had two and a half millions on her in gold, and the U-boats got her somewhere between this country and the States, I think in ’17﻿ ﻿… What’s that?﻿ ﻿… Yes, all that and anything else you can tell me.” He took the receiver from his ear. “Friend of mine in Lloyd’s,” he explained. “We ought to get some light from his reply.”

===Chinese===
“喂！是劳合社（Lloyd’s）吗？请给我接档案部。……萨姆·普拉（Sam Pullar）先生在吗？请告诉他苏格兰场的弗伦奇探长找他……喂，萨姆！……是啊……好久没见你了……听着，萨姆，我想请你帮个忙。事情相当急，你要是能立刻办一办，我很感激。……好，我握着听筒等。西卢里亚号沉没的事，你能告诉我什么都想告诉我。你记得的，她船上带着 250 万英镑的金子，是在我国和美国之间的海面上被 U 型潜艇干掉的，我想是在 ’17 年……什么？……对，那些都要，另外还有什么也一并列给我。”他把听筒从耳边拿开。“劳合社的一个朋友，”他解释道，“他的回话应该能给我们一些启发。”

===Original===
Silence reigned for a couple of minutes, then French spoke again. “Let me repeat that,” he said, seizing a pad and scribbling furiously. “Latitude 41 degrees 36 minutes north, longitude 28 degrees 53 minutes west. Right. How was that known?﻿ ﻿… But there was no direct information?﻿ ﻿… Was the gold insured?﻿ ﻿… Well, it’s an involved business, I could hardly tell you over the phone. I’ll explain it first time we meet﻿ ﻿… Thank you, Sam. Much obliged.”

===Chinese===
沉默持续了两分钟，随后弗伦奇又开了腔。“让我复述一遍，”他说着抓过一叠便笺，飞快地涂写。“北纬 41 度 36 分，西经 28 度 53 分。对。这是怎么知道的？……可是并没有直接的信息？……金子上保险了吗？……唉，这事牵扯甚多，电话里跟你说不清。等咱们见面我再从头解释……谢谢你，萨姆。多谢。”

===Original===
He rang off and then made a departmental call.

===Chinese===
他挂上电话，又拨了一个内部电话。

===Original===
“Put me through to Inspector Barnes﻿ ﻿… That you, Barnes? I’m on to something a bit in your line. Could you come down here for half an hour?”

===Chinese===
“请给我接巴恩斯探长……是巴恩斯吗？我手上有点事，正对你的路子。你能到这儿来半个钟头吗？”

===Original===
“Barnes is our authority on things nautical,” he told the others. “Began life as a sailor and has studied all branches of sea lore. We always give him shipping cases. We’ll wait till he comes and then I’ll tell you what I learned from Lloyd’s.”

===Chinese===
“巴恩斯是我们那儿航海事务的权威，”他对另外两位说。“他从当水手起家，航海学问门门都钻研过。航运案子我们总是交给他。等他来了，我再把从劳合社听来的讲给你们。”

===Original===
“Isn’t it a strange thing,” Cheyne remarked, “that Schulz should have chosen England for his map and English for his cipher. Wouldn’t the natural thing have been for him to have chosen Germany and German? He could have headed it, for instance, ‘Deutschland über Alles,’ and used the initials of German towns for his phrase.”

===Chinese===
“你们说怪不怪，”切恩说，“舒尔茨画地图偏偏挑了英国，编密码偏偏用英文。他挑德国、用德文，岂不顺理成章？比如说，他满可以在开头写上‘Deutschland über Alles’（德意志高于一切），再用德国城镇的首字母拼他那句话。”

===Original===
“I thought of that,” French returned, “but we have to remember he prepared the cipher to mislead Germans, not English. In that case I think he was right to use English. It made the thing more difficult.”

===Chinese===
“这一点我想过，”弗伦奇答道，“不过别忘了，他编这密码是要迷惑德国人，不是迷惑英国人。既然如此，我认为他用英文用对了。这就把事情弄得更难了。”

===Original===
He had scarcely finished speaking when the door opened, and a tall, alert-looking young man entered the room. French introduced him as Inspector Barnes and pointed to a chair.

===Chinese===
他话音未落，门开了，一个高个子、神情机警的年轻人走进屋来。弗伦奇介绍说是巴恩斯探长，指了指一把椅子。

===Original===
“Seat yourself, Barnes, and listen to my tale. These gentlemen are concerned with a curious story,” and he gave a brief résumé of the strange events which had led up to the existing situation. “Now,” he went on, “when we found it was connected with the *Silurian* I rang up Sam Pullar at Lloyd’s, and this is what he told me. The *Silurian* sailed from this country on the 16th of February, 1917. She was bound for New York, and she had two and a half millions on her in bullion as well as a fair number of passengers. She was a big boat﻿—an Anchor liner of some 15,000 tons. You remember about her?”

===Chinese===
“请坐，巴恩斯，听我讲这段故事。这几位先生正牵扯在一桩离奇的事里。”他把引出眼前这步局面的种种怪事择要讲了一遍。“是这样，”他接着说，“我们发现事情跟西卢里亚号有关，就给劳合社的萨姆·普拉挂了电话，他告诉我的如下。西卢里亚号于 1917 年 2 月 16 日从我国启航。她此行开往纽约，船上装着 250 万英镑的金条，还有为数不少的乘客。她是一条大船——安卡班轮公司的，约 15,000 吨。她的事你还记得吧？”

===Original===
“Well, I should think so,” Barnes returned, as he lit a cigarette. “Why, I was on that job﻿—getting her away, I mean. All kinds of precautions were taken. A tale was started that she would load up the gold at Plymouth and would sail﻿—I forget the exact date now, but it was three days after she did sail. It was my job to see that the German spies about Plymouth got hold of this tale, and we had evidence that they did get it, and moreover sent it through to Germany, and that the U-boats were instructed accordingly. As a matter of fact the *Silurian* came from Brest, where she had landed army stores from South America, and the bullion went out in a tender from Folkestone, and was transferred at night in the Channel in the middle of a ring of destroyers. While preparations were being made at Plymouth for her arrival she was away hundreds of miles towards the States.”

===Chinese===
“嗯，那还用说，”巴恩斯点起一支烟，答道，“嘿，那桩差事我就沾过手——我是说送她出航那一手。当时采取了各式各样的预防措施。有人放出风声，说她将在普利茅斯装载金条，然后于某日启航——确切日期我如今记不得了，反正比她真正启航的日子晚三天。我的差事，就是让普利茅斯一带的德国间谍把这套风声听进去，而且我们有证据表明，他们确实听了进去，还发回了德国，U 型潜艇也奉命作了相应布置。实际上，西卢里亚号是从布雷斯特开来的——她刚在那儿卸下从南美运来的军需品——金条则由一条驳船从福克斯通（Folkestone）运出，夜里在海峡中央、一圈驱逐舰的环护当中过驳。普利茅斯那边还在为迎接她做准备，她本人已经朝美国开出几百英里去了。”

===Original===
“But they got her all the same.”

===Chinese===
“可他们到底还是把她干掉了。”

===Original===
“Oh yes, they got her, but not all the same. She escaped the boats that were looking out for her. It was a chance boat that found her, somewhere, if I remember rightly, near the Azores.”

===Chinese===
“哦，是呀，他们把她干掉了，可不是照原先那套路数。她躲过了那些专事守候她的艇。发现她的是一条碰巧撞上的艇，地点嘛，如果我没记错，是在亚速尔群岛（the Azores）附近。”

===Original===
“That’s right,” French answered. “Instead of going directly west, so Sam Pullar told me, she went south to avoid those submarines you spoke of and which were supposed to be operating off the Land’s End. Her course was followed by wireless, down to near the Spanish coast, and then across fairly due west. She was last seen by a Cape boat some thirty miles west of Finisterre. Then a message was received from her when she was some 250 miles north of the Azores, that a U-boat had come along, and had ordered her to stop. The message gave her position and went on to say that a boat was coming aboard from the submarine. Then it stopped, and that was the last thing that was heard of her. Not a body or a boat or a bit of wreckage was ever picked up, and it was clear that everyone on board was lost. Then after a time confirmation was obtained. Our intelligence people in Germany intercepted a report from the commander of the submarine who sank her, giving details. She had been sunk in latitude 41° 36′ north, longitude 28° 53′ west, which confirmed the figures sent out in her last wireless message. Four boats had got away, but the commander had fired on them and had sunk them one after another, so that not a single member of the passengers or crew should survive.”

===Chinese===
“正是，”弗伦奇答道。“据萨姆·普拉说，她没有径直西行，而是向南绕，避开你说的那些潜艇——据估计它们在兰兹角（Land’s End）外海活动。她的航踪由无线电一路追到西班牙海岸附近，然后才大体取道正西横过去。最后一次有人见到她，是一条开普航线的船，在菲尼斯特雷（Finisterre）以西约 30 英里处。随后，在她驶至亚速尔群岛以北约 250 英里处时，收到了她发出的一通电讯，说一条 U 型潜艇赶了上来，勒令她停船。电讯报出了她的方位，接着说潜艇正放一条小艇到她船上去。然后就中断了，那是人们听到她的最后一点音讯。此后没有捞起过一具尸体、一条小艇或一块残骸，船上人员显然全部罹难。过了一段时间，事情得到了证实。我们在德国的情报人员截获了击沉她的那艘潜艇艇长的报告，其中详述了经过。她沉没在北纬 41° 36′、西经 28° 53′，与她最后一次无线电讯发出的数字吻合。有四条救生艇逃了出去，但那艇长朝它们开火，把它们一条接一条击沉，好叫乘客和船员无一幸存。”

===Original===
“Dirty savages,” Barnes commented. “But people in open boats wouldn’t have had much chance there anyway, particularly in February. If they had been able to keep afloat at all, they would probably have missed the Azores, and it’s very unlikely they would have made the Spanish or Portuguese coast﻿—it would have been too far.”

===Chinese===
“畜生，”巴恩斯评论道，“不过，乘敞艇漂在那里的人，横竖也没多大生机，何况是在 2 月。就算他们能把艇浮住，多半也会错过亚速尔群岛，要想撑到西班牙或葡萄牙海岸更是难上加难——路程太远了。”

===Original===
French pushed forward his atlas.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇把地图册往前推了推。

===Original===
“Just whereabouts did she sink?” he inquired.

===Chinese===
“她究竟沉在哪里？”他问道。

===Original===
“About there.” Barnes indicated a point north of the Azores. “But this atlas is too small to see it. Send someone to my room for my large atlas. You’ll see better on that.”

===Chinese===
“差不多就在那儿。”巴恩斯指了指亚速尔群岛以北的一点。“只是这本地图册太小，看不出名堂。派个人到我房间把我那本大的取来。那上面看得清楚。”

===Original===
French having telephoned his instructions Barnes went on.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇打电话交代下去之后，巴恩斯接着讲。

===Original===
“She’s evidently lying on what is called the Dolphin Rise. The Dolphin Rise is part of a great ridge which passes down the middle of the Atlantic from near Iceland to well down towards the Antarctic Ocean. This ridge is covered by an average of some 1,700 fathoms of water, with vastly greater depths on either side. It is volcanic and is covered by great submarine mountain chains. Where the tops of these mountains protrude above the surface we get, of course, islands, and the Azores are such a group.”

===Chinese===
“她显然躺在所谓的海豚海隆（Dolphin Rise）上。海豚海隆是一条巨大海岭的一部分，那海岭纵贯大西洋中部，从冰岛附近一路向南，直伸到邻近南极海的深处。岭上平均没在约 1,700 英寻的水下，两侧的海渊还要深得多。它属火山成因，覆着一条条巨大的海底山脉。这些山的山顶露出水面的地方，自然就成了岛屿，亚速尔群岛便是这样一群。”

===Original===
A constable at that moment entered with the large atlas, and Barnes continued:

===Chinese===
正在这时，一名警员抱着那本大地图册进来，巴恩斯接着说：

===Original===
“Now we’ll see in a moment.” He ran his finger down the index of maps, then turned the pages. “Here we are. Here is a map of the North Atlantic Ocean: here are the Azores and hereabouts is your point, and﻿—By Jove!” the young man looked actually excited, “here is what your cipher means all right!”

===Chinese===
“这就马上见分晓。”他的手指顺着地图索引一路下移，随后翻动书页。“找到了。这是一幅北大西洋地图：这是亚速尔群岛，你们说的那个点就在这一带，还有——天哪！”这年轻人看上去当真激动了，“这儿就是你们那密码的意思，一点儿没错！”

===Original===
The other three crowded round in almost breathless excitement. Barnes pointed with a pencil slightly to the east of a white spot about a quarter of an inch in diameter which bore the figure 18.

===Chinese===
另外三个人几乎屏住呼吸，激动地围拢过来。巴恩斯用铅笔点在一个白点稍偏东的地方，那白点直径约四分之一英寸，标注着数字 18。

===Original===
“Look here,” he went on, “there’s about the point she is supposed to have sunk. You see it is colored light blue, which the reference tells us means over 1,000 fathoms. But measure one degree to the west﻿—it is about fifty miles at that latitude﻿—and it brings us into the middle of that white patch marked 18. That white patch is another mountain chain, just not high enough to become an island, and the 18 means that the peaks come within 18 fathoms of the surface. So that your cipher message is probably quite all right, and your Antwerp party are more than likely working away at the gold at the present time.”

===Chinese===
“瞧这儿，”他接着说，“她估计沉没的那个点，差不多就在这地方。你们看，这里着的是浅蓝色，图例告诉我们，那是 1,000 英寻以深。可是朝西量一度——在那个纬度约合 50 英里——就正落到标着 18 的那块白斑中央。那块白斑是又一道山脉，只是高度差一点儿，够不着成为岛屿；18 的意思是山顶距水面不足 18 英寻。可见你们那份密码讯息多半一点不差，你们安特卫普的那伙人，此刻十之八九正在那儿对着金子卖力干呢。”

===Original===
French swore comprehensively.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇痛痛快快地大骂了一通。

===Original===
“You must be right,” he agreed. “One can see now what that blackguard of a U-boat commander did. He evidently put some men aboard the *Silurian* to dismantle their wireless, then made them sail on parallel to his own course until he had by the use of his lead maneuvered them over the highest peak, and then put them down. The whole thing must have been quite deliberate. He returned to his own government a false statement of her position, which he knew would correspond with the last message she sent out, intending it to be believed that she was lost in over 1,000 fathoms. But he sank her where he could himself afterwards recover her bullion, or sell his secret to the highest bidder. The people on the *Silurian* would know all about that two or three hours’ steam west, so they must be got rid of. Hence his destroying the boats one after another. No one must be left alive to give the thing away. To his own crew he no doubt told some tale to account for it, but he would be safe enough there, as no one except himself would know the actual facts. Dirty savage indeed!”

===Chinese===
“你必定说对了，”他表示同意。“那个恶棍 U 型潜艇艇长干的事，如今看得明白了。他显然派了几个人登上西卢里亚号，拆掉他们的无线电，然后命令他们沿同他自己航线平行的方向行驶，直到借测深锤的指引把船引到那座最高峰的峰顶上，这才下手把船弄沉。整个事情必定是蓄谋为之。他向自己的政府谎报了她的沉没方位，而且他知道这假方位恰好能同她最后发出的电讯对上号，好教人相信她葬身在了 1,000 英寻以深的水底。实际上他却把她沉在了日后自己能捞起金条的地方，要不然，也能把秘密卖给出价最高的买主。西卢里亚号上的人完全知情——真实地点就在往西两三个钟头的航程之外——所以必须斩尽杀绝。于是他便一条接一条地击沉那些救生艇。不能留下一个活口走漏真相。对他自己的艇员，他无疑编了套说辞交代缘由；不过这方面他大可放心，因为除了他本人，谁也不掌握实情。真是畜生！”

===Original===
With this speech of French’s a light seemed to Cheyne suddenly to shine out over all that strange adventure in which for so many weeks he had been involved. With it each puzzling fact seemed to become comprehensible and to drop into its natural place in the story as the pieces of a jigsaw puzzle eventually make a coherent whole. He pictured the thing from the beginning, the submarine coming up with the ship in deep water, but comparatively close to a shallow place where its treasure could be salved: the desire of the U-boat commander, Schulz, to save the gold, quite possibly in the first instance for the benefit of his nation. Then the temptation to keep what he had done secret so as, if possible later, to get the stuff for himself. His fall before this temptation, with its contingent false return to his government as to the position of the wreck. Then, Cheyne saw, the problem of passing on the secret in the event of his own death would arise, with the evolution and construction of the cipher as an attempted solution. As a result of Schulz’s fatal wound the cipher was handed to Price, and Schulz was doubtless about to explain how it should be read, when he was interrupted by the nurse. Before another chance offered he was dead.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇这一席话，在切恩心头仿佛骤然点亮一盏灯，照彻了他缠身这么多星期的整桩离奇历险。随着这道光，每一件费解的事似乎都变得可以理解，各自落回故事里它本来的位置，正如拼图的碎片终究拼成一个连贯的整体。他从头把这桩事想了一遍：潜艇在深水里追上那条船——但离一处可以把船载财宝打捞上来的浅水地方相当近；U 型潜艇艇长舒尔茨想把金子保存下来，起初很可能还是为他的国家着想。接着是诱惑：把自己干下的事瞒住，好等日后有可能把金子弄到自己手里。他在这诱惑面前跌了跟头，随之而来的，便是向自己的政府谎报沉船方位。再往后，切恩看到，就会生出那个难题：万一自己死了，秘密如何传下去——于是便有那份密码的构思与编制，权作一次求解的尝试。舒尔茨致命伤发作，密码交到了普赖斯手上；他无疑正要讲解这密码该怎么读，却被护士打断。还没等到下一个机会，他就死了。

===Original===
Given the fact that Dangle overheard the dying man’s story, and that Dangle’s character was what it was, Cheyne now saw that the remainder of his adventure could scarcely have happened otherwise than as it had. To obtain the cipher was Dangle’s obvious course, and there was no reason to doubt his own statement of how he set about it. A search among Price’s papers showed the latter had sent the document to Cheyne, and from Cheyne Dangle had evidently decided to obtain it. But nothing could be done till after the war, nor, presumably, without financial and other help. In this lay, doubtless, the reason for the application to Blessington and Sime, and these two being roped in, the unscrupulous trio set themselves to work. Susan Dangle assisted by obtaining a post as servant at Warren Lodge, and thus gained detailed information which enabled the others to lay their plans. And so in a quite orderly sequence event had followed event, until now it looked as if the climax had been reached.

===Chinese===
既知丹格尔偷听了垂死者的那段讲述，又知丹格尔的为人就是那样一种人，切恩如今看清：他的历险后半段，几乎不可能不照实际发生的那样发生。夺取密码是丹格尔顺理成章的一步，他自己如何着手行事的交代，也没有理由怀疑。搜查普赖斯的文件，查明后者已把那份文书寄给了切恩，于是丹格尔显然决定从切恩手里把它弄到手。可大战结束之前无事可为，而且想必也离不开财力与其他方面的支援。向布莱辛顿和赛姆求助，缘由无疑在此；这两个家伙入伙之后，无法无天的三人组便着手干了起来。苏珊·丹格尔出力谋得沃伦小筑女仆的差事，由此探得详细情报，让其余的人得以布置计划。就这样，一桩事接着一桩事，次序井然，直到如今，眼看高潮已然到来。

===Original===
Like a flash these thoughts passed through Cheyne’s mind, and like a flash he saw what depended on them. Now they knew where Joan Merrill had been taken. If she was still alive﻿—and he simply could not bring himself to admit any other possibility﻿—she was on that boat of Merkel’s some two hundred and fifty miles north of the Azores! From that something surely followed. He turned to French and spoke in a voice which was hoarse from anxiety.

===Chinese===
这些念头闪电般掠过切恩的脑海，他也闪电般看清了系于其上的分量。现在他们知道琼·梅里尔被带到了哪里。如果她还活着——他实在无法让自己承认还有别的可能——她就在默克尔那条船上，在亚速尔群岛以北约 250 英里处！由此必有下文可循。他转向弗伦奇，开口说话，嗓音因焦虑而沙哑。

===Original===
“What about an expedition to the place?”

===Chinese===
“派一支远征队到那地方去，怎么样？”

===Original===
French nodded decisively.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇果断地点了点头。

===Original===
“We must arrange one without delay,” he said. “I think the Admiralty is our hope. That gold wasn’t insured﻿—it was a government business. I’ll go and tell the chief about it now, and get him to see the proper authorities. Meanwhile,” he looked, for French, quite sharply at the others, “not a word of this must be breathed.”

===Chinese===
“必须毫不耽搁地安排一支，”他说。“我看咱们的指望在海军部。那批金子没有上保险——那是政府的买卖。我这就去把这事禀明上司，请他去见主管当局。在此期间，”他以弗伦奇少见的严厉目光扫了另外两人一眼，“此事一个字也不许外泄。”

===Original===
Intense interest was excited in the higher circles of the Admiralty by the news which reached them from the Yard. Great personages bestirred themselves to issue orders, with the result that with enormously more promptitude than the man in the street can bring himself to associate with a Government Department, a fast boat, well equipped with divers and gear, was got ready for sea. French put in a word for both Cheyne and Price, and when, some eight hours after their reading of the cipher, the boat put out into the Thames from Chatham Dockyard, it carried in addition to its regular crew not only Inspector French himself, but also his two protégés.

===Chinese===
苏格兰场传去的消息，在海军部高层激起了莫大的兴趣。大人物们发号施令，忙得不亦乐乎，结果是：一条快速舰船连同齐备的潜水员和器械整装待发——其雷厉风行，远非街头百姓肯于相信的政府部门的做派。弗伦奇替切恩和普赖斯都进了一句言。在他们读出密码约莫八个钟头之后，这条船从查塔姆船厂开出，驶入泰晤士河，船上除正式船员外，不但载着弗伦奇探长本人，还载着他照应的两位年轻后进。

## XX The Goal of the *L’Escaut* / 二十 莱斯考号的归宿

===Original===
Inspector French had gone to bed in the tiny but comfortable stateroom which had been put at his disposal by the officers of the Admiralty boat while that redoubtable vessel was slipping easily and on an even keel through the calm waters of the Straits of Dover. He awoke next morning to find her plunging and rolling and staggering through what, in comparison with his previous experiences of the sea, appeared to be a frightful storm. To his surprise, however, he did not feel any bad effects from the motion, and presently he arose, and having with extreme care performed the ticklish operation of shaving, dressed and climbed with the aid of railings and handles to the companionway, and so to the deck.

===Chinese===
当那艘威风凛凛的军舰正以平稳的船身、轻快地在多佛海峡平静的水面上滑行时，弗伦奇探长已在海军部船只的军官们拨给他使用的那间狭小却舒适的舱室里就寝。第二天早晨他醒来，发现船正颠簸着、翻滚着、踉跄着，闯过一片照他先前海上阅历看来简直是骇人的风暴。然而令他意外的是，他并没有因颠簸感到任何不适，于是不久便起身，极其小心地完成了刮脸这门险活，穿好衣服，借助栏杆和把手攀上升降口梯道，来到甲板上。

===Original===
The sight which met his eyes on emerging made him hold his breath, as he clung to the rail at the companion door. It was a wonderful morning, clear and bright and fresh and invigorating. The sun shone down from a cloudless sky on to a dark sapphire sea of incredible purity, flecked over with foaming patches of dazzling white. As far as the eye could reach in every direction out to the hard sharp line of the horizon, great waves rolled relentlessly onward, wavelets dancing and churning and foaming on their slow-moving flanks. The wind caught French and, as if it were a solid, held him pinned against the deckhouse. He stood watching the bluff bows of the boat rise in the air, then crash back into the sea, throwing out a smother of water and foam some of which would seep over the fo’c’sle, and after swirling through the forward deck hamper, disappear through the scuppers amidships.

===Chinese===
一出舱门映入眼帘的景象令他屏住了呼吸，他紧抓着升降口门边的栏杆。这是一个美妙的早晨，晴朗、明媚、清新而令人振奋。太阳从万里无云的天空照下来，照在一片纯净得出奇的深蓝宝石色海面上，海面缀满一块块耀眼的白色浪沫。放眼四望，一直到地平线那道硬朗锋利的线条，巨浪滚滚，无情地向前推进，小浪花在它们缓缓移动的侧腹上跳舞、翻腾、起沫。风扑住弗伦奇，仿佛一团实体，把他牢牢钉在甲板室上。他站着看那船的钝艏翘上半空，再轰然砸回海里，甩出一大片水和泡沫，有些漫过船艏楼，打着旋穿过前甲板上的筐具，然后从船中部的排水孔流走。

===Original===
For some moments he watched, then moving round the deckhouse, he glanced up and saw Cheyne and Price beckoning to him from the bridge, where they had joined the officer of the watch.

===Chinese===
他望了好一会儿，随后绕过甲板室，抬头一看，只见切恩和普赖斯正在舰桥上向他招手，两人已和值班军官站在一起。

===Original===
“Some morning this, Inspector,” Price cried, as he joined them in the lee of the weather canvas. “This will blow the London cobwebs out of our minds.”

===Chinese===
“今早这天气真叫绝，探长。”普赖斯在挡风帆的背风处与他聚到一起时喊道。“这一下能把伦敦积在我们脑子里的蛛网统统吹个干净。”

===Original===
He was evidently keenly enjoying himself, and even Cheyne’s anxious face showed appreciation of his surroundings. And soon French himself, having realized that they were not necessarily going to the bottom in a hurricane, but merely running down Channel in a fresh southwesterly breeze, began to feel the thrill of the sea, and to believe that the end of his quest was going to develop into a novel and delightful holiday trip.

===Chinese===
他显然正玩得尽兴，就连切恩那张焦虑的脸也显出对眼前景致的欣赏。不久，弗伦奇自己也明白了：他们并不是要在这场合葬身海底，不过是在乘着一股清劲的西南风沿海峡航驶，于是开始感到大海的振奋，并且相信，他这趟搜寻的结局，眼看要变成一次新奇而愉快的假日旅行了。

===Original===
The same weather held all that day and the next, but on the third the wind fell, and the sea gradually calmed down to a slow, easy swell. The sun grew hotter, and basking in it in the lee of the deckhouse became a delight. Little was said about the object of the expedition. French and Price were content to enjoy the present, and Cheyne managed to keep his anxieties to himself. The ship’s officers were a jolly crowd, immensely excited by their quest, and conducting themselves as the kindly hosts of welcome guests.

===Chinese===
这样的天气一直持续到当天和第二天，但第三天风势减弱了，海面渐渐平息成一阵缓慢、从容的长涌。太阳越来越热，在甲板室背风处晒着太阳成了件乐事。关于此行的目的，大家很少谈起。弗伦奇和普赖斯安于享受当下，切恩也设法把焦虑闷在心里。船上的军官们是一群快活人，为这趟搜寻兴奋不已，个个以殷勤主人的姿态，款待着这几位受欢迎的客人。

===Original===
On the fourth day it grew still warmer, indeed out of the breeze made by the ship’s motion it was unpleasantly hot. French liked to get away forward, where it was cooler, and leaned by the hour over the bows, watching the sharp stem cut through the water and roll back in its frothing wave on either side. Dolphins were now to be seen swimming in the clear water, and two hung at the bows, one on each side, apparently motionless for long periods, until suddenly they would dart ahead, spiral round one another and then return to their places.

===Chinese===
第四天更热了，在船行带起的凉风之外，简直热得难受。弗伦奇喜欢到前面较凉快的地方去，一连个把钟头俯在船艏上，看那锋利的船艏柱劈开水面，两侧翻卷起带白沫的波浪。这时可以看到海豚在清澈的水里游动，有两尾悬在船艏，一左一右，常常长时间看似一动不动，随后突然向前窜去，彼此螺旋般绕转一圈，再回到原处。

===Original===
That fourth evening the captain joined his passengers as the trio were smoking on deck.

===Chinese===
第四天晚上，三个人正在甲板上抽烟，船长过来陪伴他的乘客们。

===Original===
“If we carry on like this,” he remarked, “we should reach the position about four a.m. But those beggars may be taking a risk and not showing a light, so I propose to slow down from now on, in order not to arrive till daylight. Come on deck about six. If they’re here we should raise them between then and seven.”

===Chinese===
“照这样走下去，”他说，“我们凌晨四点左右就该到那个位置了。但那帮家伙也许在冒险，不点灯，所以我打算从现在起减速，免得天不亮就到了。六点左右上甲板来吧。要是他们在这儿，我们该在那之后到七点之间望见他们。”

===Original===
French, waking early next morning, could not control his excitement and remain in his berth until the allotted time. He rose at five, and went on deck with the somewhat shamefaced feeling that he was acting as a small boy, who on Christmas morning must needs get up on waking to investigate the possibilities of stockings. But he need not have feared ridicule from his companions. Both Cheyne and Price were already on the bridge, and the skipper stood with his telescope glued to his eye as he searched the horizon ahead. All three were evidently thrilled by the approaching finale, and a slight incoherence was discernible in their somewhat scrappy conversation.

===Chinese===
第二天清晨弗伦奇醒得早，兴奋得没法克制，捱不到指定时刻再躺在铺位里。他五点起身上了甲板，心里有点不好意思，觉得自己活像个小男孩，圣诞节的早晨一醒就非爬起来，去看看长统袜里能有什么指望不可。不过他大可不必担心同伴笑话他。切恩和普赖斯都已到了舰桥上，船长举着望远镜贴在眼前，搜索着前方地平线。三人显然都被临近的结局激动着，三言两语的谈话里都听得出些许语无伦次。

===Original===
The morning was calm and very clear. Once again the sky was cloudless, and the soft southwesterly wind barely ruffled the surface of the long flat swells. It was a pleasure to be alive, and it seemed impossible to associate crime and violence with the expedition. But beneath their smiles all concerned felt it might easily develop into a grim enough business. And that side of it became more apparent when at the captain’s order the covers of the six-pounders mounted fore and aft were removed, and the weapons were prepared for action by their crews.

===Chinese===
早晨风平浪静，天色极其晴朗。天空又一次万里无云，柔和的西南风只是微微吹皱平缓长涌的表面。活着便是一桩乐事，此行似乎怎么也难以同罪恶和暴力联想到一起。但在他们的笑容底下，所有相关的人都感到，事情很容易演变得相当严酷。而当船长一声令下，船上前后所装六磅炮的炮衣被卸下，炮手们将武器作好战斗准备时，这一面就显得更加分明。

===Original===
