The hands of French’s watch had just reached the quarter hour after six, when Captain Amery, who had once again been sweeping the horizon with his telescope, said quietly: “There she is.” He handed the glass to French. “See there, about three points on the starboard bow.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇的表针刚过六点一刻，一直在用望远镜扫视地平线的阿默里舰长轻声说：“她就在那儿。”他把望远镜递给弗伦奇。“看那边，右舷船头约三个罗经点的方位。”

===Original===
French, with some difficulty steadying the tube, saw very faint and far off what looked like the upper part of a steamer’s deck, with a funnel, and two masts like threads of the finest gossamer. “She’s still hull down,” the captain explained. “You’ll see her better in a few minutes. We should be up with her in three-quarters of an hour.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇费了点劲才拿稳镜筒，看见极远处一个淡淡的影子，像是一艘轮船甲板的上部，带着烟囱和两根细若上好蛛丝的桅杆。“她的船身现在还隐在地平线下。”舰长解释道。“几分钟后您就会看得分明了。三刻钟之内我们该追上她了。”

===Original===
In order to leave them free later on, it was decided to have breakfast at once, and by the time the hasty meal had been disposed of the stranger was clearly visible to the naked eye. She lay heading westward, as though anchored in the swing of the tide, and her fires appeared to be either out or banked, as no smoke was visible at her funnel. The glass revealed a flag at her forepeak, but she was still too far off to make out its coloring.

===Chinese===
为了稍后好腾出身来，大家决定立刻吃早饭，等这顿匆匆的饭一吃完，那艘陌生的船肉眼已看得分明。她船头朝西停着，仿佛锚泊着随潮回转，烟囱上不见一丝烟，炉火似乎不是熄了就是封了。望远镜里看得出她船艏前端挂着一面旗，但距离还太远，辨认不出颜色。

===Original===
Now that the dramatic climax was approaching, the minds of the actors in the play became charged with a very real anxiety. Captain Amery, under almost any circumstances, would have to deal with a very ticklish situation. He had to get the gold, if it was salvable, and the fact that they were not in British waters would be a complication if the Belgian had already recovered it. French had to ascertain if his quarry were on board, and if so, see that they did not escape him﻿—also a difficult job outside the three-mile limit. For Price a fortune hung in the balance﻿—not of course all the gold that might be found, but the proportion allowed him by law; while for Cheyne there remained something a thousand times more important than the capture of a criminal or the acquisition of a fortune﻿—for Cheyne the question of Joan Merrill’s life was at stake. Their several anxieties were reflected on the faces of the men, as they stood in silence, watching the rapidly growing vessel.

===Chinese===
戏剧性的高潮既已临近，这出戏里各个人物的心头都充塞着十分真切的焦虑。无论情势如何发展，阿默里舰长要应付的都是一个非常棘手的局面。他必须把金子弄到手——倘若还捞得起来的话；而他们不在英国领海这一事实，万一比利时人已经把金子起出了水，便要平添一层麻烦。弗伦奇必须查明他的猎物是否在船上，若在，就得看住他们不叫逃脱——在三海里界限之外，这同样是件难办的差事。对普赖斯来说，一场富贵悬而未决——当然不是可能找到的全部金子，而是法律容许他得的那一份；而对切恩来说，还有比抓获罪犯或得到富贵重要一千倍的东西——对切恩而言，悬于一线的是琼·梅里尔的性命。各人的焦虑都写在他们脸上，他们默默站着，望着那艘迅速变大的船。

===Original===
Presently an exclamation came from Captain Amery.

===Chinese===
过了一会儿，阿默里舰长发出一声惊呼。

===Original===
“By Jove!” he said, “this is a rum business. I can see that flag now, and it’s our red ensign. What’s a Belgian boat doing with a British flag? And what’s more, it’s jack down﻿—a flag of distress. What do you think of that?” He looked at the others with a puzzled expression, then went on: “I suppose they’re not armed? You don’t know, Inspector, do you? If they were armed it would be a likely enough ruse to get us close by, so as to make sure of hitting us in a vital place.”

===Chinese===
“天哪！”他说，“这事可真邪门。我现在看清那面旗了，那是我们的红船旗。一条比利时船挂英国旗干什么？还有，艏旗降着——那是遇险的旗号。你们怎么看？”他一脸困惑地看看众人，接着又说：“他们该没带武器吧？您不知道吧，探长？他们要是带了武器，这倒很可能是个诱我们靠近的圈套，好稳稳当当击中我们的要害。”

===Original===
French shook his head. He had heard nothing about arms, though for all he knew to the contrary the *L’Escaut* might carry a gun.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇摇了摇头。关于武器，他毫无耳闻，不过就他所知，也不能排除莱斯考号上装着炮的可能。

===Original===
“I don’t see one,” the captain continued, “but then if they have one they’d keep it hidden. But I don’t like there being no signs of life aboard her. There’s no smoke anywhere, either from her boilers or her galley. There’s no one on the bridge, and I’ve not seen a movement on deck. It doesn’t look well: in fact it looks as if they were lying low and waiting for us.”

===Chinese===
“我没看见炮，”舰长接着说，“不过他们真要有，也会藏起来。可我不喜欢她船上毫无生气的样子。哪儿都没有烟，锅炉房没有，厨房也没有。舰桥上没有人，甲板上我还没见谁动过一下。这情形不妙：看那样子，倒像是他们潜伏着，在等我们。”

===Original===
They were now within a mile of the stranger, and her details were clear even to the naked eye.

===Chinese===
这时他们离那陌生的船已不到一英里，她的细部连肉眼也看得分明。

===Original===
“It’s the *L’Escaut* anyway,” Captain Amery went on. “I can see the name on her bows. But I confess I don’t like that flag and that silence. I think I’ll see if I can wake her up.”

===Chinese===
“反正她就是莱斯考号。”阿默里舰长接着说。“我看清她船头上的船名了。不过我承认，我不喜欢那面旗和那片沉寂。我看我来试试，能不能把她叫醒。”

===Original===
He put his hand on the foghorn halliard and blew a number of resounding blasts. For a few seconds nothing happened, then suddenly two figures appeared at the deckhouse door, and after a moment’s pause, rushed up on the bridge and began waving furiously. As they passed up the bridge ladder they came from behind the shelter of a boat and their silhouettes became visible against the sky. They were both women!

===Chinese===
他把手按上雾角的拉索，鸣了好几响洪亮的笛声。几秒钟里毫无动静，随后甲板室门口突然出现两个人影，略一迟疑，便奔上舰桥拼命挥手。她们上舰桥梯时，从一条小艇的遮蔽后转了出来，身形映在了天空的背景上。那竟是两个女人！

===Original===
A strangled cry burst from Cheyne as he snatched the captain’s telescope and gazed at them, then with a shout of “It’s she! It’s she!” he leaped to the end of the bridge and began waving his hat frantically.

===Chinese===
切恩一把夺过舰长的望远镜凝望她们，喉咙里迸出一声哽咽的喊叫，随即大叫一声“是她！是她！”，纵身奔到舰桥尽头，疯狂地挥起帽子来。

===Original===
At this moment two other figures appeared on the fo’c’sle and, apparently moving to the vessel’s side, stood watching the newcomers. Amery rang his engines down to half speed and, slightly porting his helm, headed for some distance astern of the other. Then starboarding, he swung round, and bringing up parallel to her and some couple of hundred yards away, he dropped anchor.

===Chinese===
这时船艏楼上又出现两个人影，他们似乎挪到船舷边，站着打量这些新来的人。阿默里把车钟打到半速，稍稍左转舵，朝对方船尾后方驶去一段距离，随即反打右舵兜过弯来，在与她相隔约两百码处并行停住，抛下了锚。

===Original===
Without loss of a moment a boat was lowered, and French, Cheyne, Price, the first officer, and a half dozen men, all armed with service revolvers, tumbled in. Giving way lustily, they pulled for the Belgian.

===Chinese===
一刻也不耽搁，一条小艇放了下去，弗伦奇、切恩、普赖斯、大副和六名水手——人人佩着制式左轮手枪——一拥而上跳进艇里。众人奋力荡桨，朝那条比利时船划去。

===Original===
It was by this time possible to distinguish the features of the women, and French was not surprised to learn they were Joan Merrill and Susan Dangle. Evidently they recognized Cheyne, who kept waving furiously as if he found the movement necessary to relieve his overwrought feelings. The two figures forward were those of men, and these stood watching the boat, though without exhibiting any of the transports of delight of their fellow shipmates on the bridge.

===Chinese===
这时已分辨得出那两个女人的面目了，得知她们是琼·梅里尔和苏珊·丹格尔，弗伦奇并不意外。她们显然认出了切恩，切恩则一直狂挥着手臂，仿佛唯有这个动作才能宣泄他过度紧张的心情。船头那两个人影是男人，他们站着观望小艇，却丝毫没有舰桥上同船伙伴那种欣喜若狂的表示。

===Original===
As they drew closer Joan made signs to them to go round to the other side of the ship, and dropping round her stern they saw a ladder rigged. In a few seconds they were alongside, and Cheyne, leaping out before the others, rushed up the steps and reached the deck.

===Chinese===
小艇再划近时，琼做手势叫他们绕到船的另一侧去，他们绕过船尾，看见那里悬好了一副梯子。几秒钟后艇便贴上了船身，切恩抢在众人之前跃出，蹬上梯级直奔甲板。

===Original===
If there had been any doubts as to the real relations between himself and Joan, these were set at rest at that moment. Instinctively he opened his arms, and Joan, swept off her feet by her emotion, threw herself into them and clung to him, while tears of joy and relief ran down her cheeks. As far as Cheyne was concerned, Susan Dangle, the figures on the fo’c’sle, French, and the men behind him might as well not have existed. He crushed Joan violently to him, covering her face and hair with burning kisses, as he murmured brokenly of his love and of his thankfulness for her safety.

===Chinese===
倘若对他和琼的真实关系还有什么疑问的话，此刻也烟消云散了。他本能地张开双臂，琼被情感冲垮了脚跟，扑进他怀里紧紧依偎着他，喜悦和宽慰的泪水顺着双颊流下。在切恩看来，苏珊·丹格尔、船艏楼上的人影、弗伦奇，以及身后艇里的水手们，尽可以不存在。他把琼紧紧搂在怀里，滚烫的吻密密地落在她的脸上和发上，一面断断续续呢喃着自己的爱，和她平安无事带给他的庆幸。

===Original===
French, anxious to learn the state of affairs and seeing nothing was to be got from Joan, turned expectantly to Susan Dangle. What could these unexpected developments mean? Was Susan, the enemy, now a friend? Where were the others? Were the ship’s company friends or foes? Could he ask her questions which might incriminate her without giving her a formal warning?

===Chinese===
弗伦奇急于了解情况，又看出从琼那里问不出什么，便满怀期待地转向苏珊·丹格尔。这些意外的变故意味着什么？苏珊这个敌人，如今成了朋友？其他人都在哪儿？船上的那些人是友是敌？他能不能不作正式警告，就向她提出可能使她自陷罪责的问题？

===Original===
But his curiosity would brook no delay.

===Chinese===
可他的好奇心一分钟也等不得。

===Original===
“I am Inspector French of Scotland Yard,” he announced, while Price and the first officer stood round expectantly. “You are Miss Susan Dangle. Where are the other members of this expedition?”

===Chinese===
“我是苏格兰场的弗伦奇探长。”他郑重宣布，普赖斯和大副在旁期待地站着。“你是苏珊·丹格尔小姐。这次远航的其他成员都在哪里？”

===Original===
The girl wrung her hands, and he noticed how terribly pale and drawn was her face and what horror shone in her eyes.

===Chinese===
姑娘绞着双手，他注意到她的脸色多么惨白憔悴，眼睛里闪着怎样的惊恐。

===Original===
“Oh!” she cried, with a gesture as if to shut out the sight of some hideous dream. “Oh, it’s been awful! I can’t speak of it. They’re dead! My brother James, Charles Sime, Mr. Merkel, most of the crew, dead﻿—all dead! Mr. Blessington wounded﻿—probably dying! They got fighting over the gold!” She began suddenly to laugh, a terrible high cackling laugh, that made her hearers shiver, and attracted the attention even of Joan and Cheyne.

===Chinese===
“哦！”她叫道，做了个手势，仿佛要挡住某个狰狞梦魇的景象。“哦，太可怕了！我说不出口。他们都死了！我哥哥詹姆斯、查尔斯·赛姆（Charles Sime）、默克尔（Merkel）先生、还有大部分船员，死了——全都死了！布莱辛顿先生受了伤——多半快要死了！他们为了金子打起来了！”她突然笑起来，一阵尖利刺耳的可怕笑声，听得听的人直打寒噤，连琼和切恩都被惊动了。

===Original===
French stepped quickly forward and seized her arm.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇抢上一步，抓住她的手臂。

===Original===
“There now, Miss Dangle,” he said kindly but firmly. “Stop that and pull yourself together. Your terrible experiences are over now and you’re in the hands of friends. But you mustn’t give way like this. Make an effort, and you’ll be better directly.” He led her to a hatchway and made her sit down, while he continued soothing her as one would a fractious child.

===Chinese===
“好了，丹格尔小姐，”他语气又温和又坚决地说道。“别这样，定定神。你的可怕经历已经过去了，你现在在朋友们手里。可你不能就这样垮下去。努力一把，你马上就会好些的。”他领她到舱口边让她坐下，一面继续安抚她，像哄一个闹脾气的孩子。

===Original===
But so great was the agitation of both girls that it was quite a considerable time before the tragic tale of the *L’Escaut*’s expedition became fully unfolded. And when at last it was told it proved still but one more illustration of the old truth that the qualities of greed and envy and selfishness have that seed of decay within themselves which leads their unhappy victims to overreach themselves, and instead of gaining what they seek, to lose their all. Shorn of incoherent phrases and irrelevant details the story was this.

===Chinese===
但两个姑娘激动得厉害，过了好一阵子，莱斯考号这趟远航的悲惨故事才得以和盘托出。等到终于讲完，它不过是再一次印证了那条古老的真理：贪婪、嫉妒与自私这些品性，自身便带着腐烂的种子，会诱使它们不幸的受害者机关算尽，非但得不到所求，反而输个精光。撇去语无伦次的字句和无关的枝节，这故事是这样的。

===Original===
On the 24th of May the *L’Escaut* had left Antwerp with twenty-eight souls aboard. Aft there were Joan, Susan, Blessington, Sime, Dangle, and Merkel, with the captain, first officer, and engineer﻿—nine persons, while forward were three divers, six assistants, a cook, a steward, four seamen, and four engine-room staff, or nineteen altogether. Once clear of the Scheldt Joan’s treatment had changed. Her food was no longer drugged, and when in a few days she got over the effects of the doses she had received, she found her jailers polite and friendly and anxious to minimize the inconvenience and anxiety she was suffering. They told her they did not wish her evil, and were taking her with them simply to prevent information as to themselves or their affairs leaking out through her. This, of course, she did not believe, since she did not possess sufficient information about them to enable her to interfere with their plans. But later their real motive dawned on her. Gradually she realized that Blessington had fallen in love with her, and though he was circumspect enough, her distrust of him was such that she felt sick with horror and dread when she thought of him. Nothing, however, had occurred to which she could take exception, and had it not been for her fears as to her own fate and her anxieties as to Cheyne’s, the voyage would have been pleasant enough.

===Chinese===
5月24日，莱斯考号驶离安特卫普，船上共二十八条性命。船尾有琼、苏珊、布莱辛顿、赛姆、丹格尔和默克尔，连同船长、大副和轮机长——九个人；船前部则是三名潜水员、六名助手、一名厨师、一名服务员、四名水手和四名机舱人员，合计十九人。一驶出斯海尔德河（the Scheldt）河口，琼的待遇就变了。她的饭食里不再下药，几天后她熬过了先前所服药物的反应，发现看守她的人客客气气、和和气气，一心想减轻她所受的不便和忧虑。他们对她说，他们并无恶意，带她同行只是为了防止关于他们本人或他们事务的讯息从她这里泄露出去。这话她当然不信，因为她掌握的关于他们的情报，还不足以妨碍他们的计划。但到后来，他们真正的动机在她心里渐渐明朗。她逐步意识到，布莱辛顿爱上了她；他虽然行事够谨慎，可她对他的戒心已深到一想到他就恶心害怕的地步。好在并没有发生任何她可以指摘的事，若不是她为自身命运担惊、为切恩的安危受怕，这一路航程本可以相当惬意。

===Original===
The *L’Escaut* was a fast boat, and four days had brought them to the spot referred to in the cipher. After three days’ search they found the wreck, and all three divers had at once gone down. A week was spent in making an examination of the vessel, at the end of which time they had located the gold. It was in her stern, low down and not far from her port side. The divers recommended blowing her plates off at this spot, and ten days more sufficed for this. Through the hole thus made the divers were able to draw in tackle lowered from the *L’Escaut*, and the ingots of gold were slung to cradles and drawn up with really wonderful ease and speed. They had, moreover, been favored with a peculiarly fine stretch of weather, work having to be suspended on only eight days of the thirty-seven they were there.

===Chinese===
莱斯考号是条快船，四天便把他们送到了密码所指的那处海域。搜寻了三天，他们找到了沉船，三名潜水员立即下去了。查勘沉船用了整整一个星期，期满时他们定出了金子的位置。金子在船尾，位置很低，离左舷不远。潜水员建议在这一点炸开船壳板，这又用去了十天。从炸开的洞口，潜水员把莱斯考号上放下来的吊索拖了进去，金条被绑上吊架，以实在惊人的轻松和速度被吊了上来。何况老天还格外赏脸，他们在那里的三十七天里，只有八天不得不停工。

===Original===
On reaching the wreck in the first instance the captain had mustered his crew aft and had informed them﻿—what he could no longer keep secret﻿—that they were out for gold, and that if they found it in the quantities they hoped, every man on board would receive at the end of the trip a gift of £1,000 in addition to his pay. The men at first seemed more than satisfied, but as ingot after ingot was recovered the generosity of the offer shrank in their estimation. Four days before the appearance of French’s party the divers had reported that another day would complete the work, and then appeared the first hint that all was not well. On that last evening before the completion of the diving the men came forward in a body and asked to see the captain. They explained that they had been reckoning up the value of the gold, and they weren’t having £1,000 apiece: they wanted an even divide all round. The captain argued with them civilly enough at first﻿—told them that they couldn’t get the metal ashore and turned into money in secret, that the port officers or coastguards wherever it was unloaded would be bound to learn what they were doing and that then the government would claim an enormous percentage of the whole, so that the £1,000 per man was an extremely liberal gift. The men declared that they would look after the unloading, and that they were going to have what they wanted. Hot words passed, and then the captain drew a revolver and said that he was captain there, and that what he said would go. Susan was watching the scene from the quarterdeck behind, but she could not be quite sure of what followed. One of the crew pressed forward and the captain raised his revolver. She did not think he meant to fire, but another of the men either genuinely or purposely misunderstood his action. He raised his hand, a shot rang out, and the captain fell dead. The mutineers were evidently terribly upset by a murder which they had apparently never intended, and had Blessington and Sime acted intelligently, the trouble might have gone no further. But at that moment these two worthies, who must have been in the chart-house all the time, began firing through the windows at the men. A regular pitched battle ensued, in which Sime and five of the crew were hit, three of the latter being killed. It was then war to the knife between those who berthed forward and those who berthed aft. All that night sporadic shots rang out at intervals, but at daybreak on the following day matters came to a head. The crew with considerable generalship made a feint on the fo’c’sle with some of their number while the remainder swarmed aft below decks. The defenders, taken in the rear, were shot down, and the mutineers were masters of the ship.

===Chinese===
初到沉船处时，船长曾把全体船员集合到船尾，告诉了他们一件他再也无法隐瞒的事——这一趟是为金子而来，倘若找到的数量如他们所望，航程结束时船上每个人除薪水外还能得到1,000 英镑的赏金。起先众人似乎再满意不过，可随着一根又一根金条被捞起，这份许诺的慷慨在他们眼里不断缩水。弗伦奇一行出现前四天，潜水员报告说再有一天即可完工，这时便冒出了第一丝不妙的苗头。潜水作业完工前的最后一个傍晚，船员们成群结队上前求见船长。他们解释说，他们一直都在盘算金子的价值，一人1,000 英镑他们可不干：他们要全体均分。船长起初还算客气地同他们讲理——说这批金属没法偷偷运上岸变成钱，无论在哪儿卸货，港口官员或海岸警卫队必定会打听到他们在干什么，到那时政府会索赔总额的一大截，所以每人1,000 英镑已是极其大方的赏赐。船员们却宣称卸货的事自有他们张罗，他们要的东西就一定要拿到。话赶话地说僵了，随后船长拔出左轮手枪，说这里他是船长，他说了算。苏珊当时在后面的艉楼上看着这一幕，但接下去的事她看不十分真切。一名船员往前逼近，船长举起了左轮枪。她不认为他真要开枪，可另一个船员——或出于真心或有意为之——误解了他的动作。那人一抬手，一声枪响，船长倒地身亡。哗变者显然被这桩他们似乎从未料到的杀人案吓得魂飞魄散，倘若布莱辛顿和赛姆当时处置得聪明些，事态原可能到此为止。可就在那一刻，这两位好汉——他们想必一直待在海图室里——开始隔着窗户朝船员们开火。一场地地道道的对阵恶战随之而来，赛姆和五名船员中弹，后者之中三人丧命。从此，住船前部和住船尾的两拨人势不两立，你死我活。那天夜里零星的枪声隔一阵响一阵，到第二天破晓，事态终于摊牌。船员们颇有些将才，派出一部分人在船艏楼佯攻，其余的人则从甲板下面蜂拥扑向船尾。守方腹背受敌，纷纷被击倒，哗变者成了全船的主宰。

===Original===
All that next day Joan and Susan, terror-stricken, clung to each other in the latter’s cabin. The men were reasonably civil: told them they might get themselves food, and let them alone. But that night a further terrible quarrel burst out between, as they learned afterwards, those who wished to murder the girls and go off with the treasure and those who feared murder more than the loss of the gold. Once again there were the reports of shots and the groans of wounded men. The fusillade went on at intervals all night, until next morning one of the divers﻿—a superior man with whom the girls had often talked﻿—had come in with his head covered with blood, and asked the girls to bandage it. Susan had some slight surgical knowledge, and did what she could for him. Then the man told them that of the entire ship’s company only themselves and seven others were alive, and that of these seven four were so badly wounded that they would probably not recover. Among these was Blessington. Sime and James Dangle were dead.

===Chinese===
接下来的那一整天，琼和苏珊吓得魂不附体，在苏珊的舱房里相拥而守。船员们还算规矩：叫她们可以自己去弄吃的，此外并不来纠缠。可当天夜里，一场更可怕的火并又爆发了——她们后来才知道——一边是要杀掉两个姑娘、带着财宝远走高飞的人，一边是比起失金更怕沾血的人。枪声再度响起，夹杂着伤者的呻吟。断断续续的交火持续了一整夜，直到第二天早晨，一名潜水员——是个有些教养的人，两位姑娘常同他交谈——满头是血地进来，请姑娘们替他包扎。苏珊懂一点外科常识，尽其所能替他料理。然后那人告诉她们，全船上下活着的只剩她们俩和另外七个人，而那七人中有四个伤得怕是活不成了。布莱辛顿便在其中。赛姆和詹姆斯·丹格尔都已死了。

===Original===
The slightly injured men threw the dead overboard and cleaned up the traces of the fighting, while the girls ministered to the seriously wounded. Of course, in the three days up till the arrival of the avengers﻿—who had by a strange trick of fate become the rescuers﻿—one man had died. Of the eight-and-twenty who sailed from Antwerp there were therefore left only nine: the two girls and four slightly and three seriously wounded men. None of those able to move understood either engineering or seamanship, so that they had luckily decided to remain at anchor in the hope of some ship picking up their flag of distress.

===Chinese===
轻伤的把死者抛下海，清除了厮杀的痕迹，姑娘们则照料着重伤员。自然，在兴师问罪的一行——他们造化弄人，竟成了救援者——赶到之前的那三天里，又死了一人。从安特卫普出航的二十八人，至此只剩九个：两个姑娘、四个轻伤和三个重伤的男人。能走动的人里没有一个懂轮机，也没有一个懂航海，所幸他们决定就地锚泊，指望有过往船只望见他们的求救旗。

===Original===
“There is just one thing I should like to understand,” said Cheyne to Joan, when later on that day a prize crew had been put aboard the *L’Escaut* and steam was being raised for the return to England, “and that is what happened to you on the night that we burgled Earlswood. You got back to your rooms, then left again with Sime and Blessington?”

===Chinese===
“有一件事我想弄明白，”当天晚些时候，一支押船队已被派上莱斯考号、锅炉正生火升汽准备返回英国时，切恩对琼说，“那就是我们夜盗厄尔斯伍德的那个晚上，你到底出了什么事。你回到了住处，然后又跟赛姆和布莱辛顿一起走了？”

===Original===
“There’s not much to tell about that,” Joan answered, smiling happily up into her lover’s eyes. “I was, as you know, standing like a watchman before the door of Earlswood, when I saw Susan and her brother coming up. I rang and knocked and kept them talking as long as possible. Then when they opened the door I slipped away, but I heard your footsteps and realized that you had got out by the back way. I heard you run off down the lane with Dangle after you, then remembering your arrangement about throwing away the tracing, I climbed over the wall, picked it up and went back to my rooms. The first thing I did was to photograph it, then I hid it in my color box. I had scarcely done so when Sime called. He said you had met with an accident﻿—been caught between two motorcars and knocked down by one of them﻿—and that you were seriously injured. He said you were conscious and had given him my address and were calling for me. I went down to find Blessington driving a car, though I didn’t know then it was Blessington. As soon as we started Sime held a chloroformed cloth over my mouth, and I don’t remember much more till we were on the *L’Escaut*.”

===Chinese===
“那没什么可讲的。”琼答道，幸福地含笑仰视着恋人的眼睛。“你知道，我那时正像个更夫似的守在厄尔斯伍德门前，忽然看见苏珊和她哥哥走来。我按铃、敲门，尽量拖着同他们说话。后来他们开了门，我便溜了，但我听见了你的脚步声，明白你已从后路脱身。我听见你顺着小巷跑去，丹格尔在后面追你，于是我记起你们关于扔掉描图的约定，翻过墙去，把它捡起来，回到了我的住处。我头一件事就是把它拍了照，然后把描图藏进我的颜料箱。我几乎刚藏好，赛姆就来了。他说你出了事故——被夹在两辆汽车中间，让其中一辆撞倒了——伤得很重。他说你神志还清醒，把我的地址给了他，正在叫我去。我下了楼，看见布莱辛顿在开一辆车，虽然当时我并不知道那是布莱辛顿。车一开动，赛姆就把一块蒙了氯仿的布捂在我嘴上，再往后的事，我记得的就不多了，直到我们已上了莱斯考号。”

===Original===
“But how did Sime find your rooms?”

===Chinese===
“可赛姆是怎么找到你住处的？”

===Original===
“Through Susan. Susan told me all about it afterwards. She went out after James and saw me climbing over the wall with the tracing. She followed me to my rooms and immediately telephoned to Sime. When Sime called she was with him, and while I changed my coat Sime let her into the studio and she hid behind an easel until we were gone. She searched till she found the tracing and then simply walked out. The gang had intended to go to Antwerp the following week in any case, but this business upset their plans and they decided to start immediately. Dangle went on and arranged for the *L’Escaut* to leave some days earlier. The rest of us put up at Ghent till she was ready to sail.” But little further remains to be told. The few bars of gold still left on the *Silurian* were soon raised and the two ships set sail, reaching Chatham some five days later. All the bullion theoretically belonged to the Crown, but under the special circumstances a generous division was made whereby twenty-five percent was returned to the finders. As Price refused to accept the whole amount an amicable agreement was come to, whereby Cheyne, Joan, and Price each received almost one-third, or £200,000 apiece. Of the balance of over £20,000, £10,000 was given to Susan Dangle by Joan’s imperative directions. She said that Susan was not a bad girl and had turned up trumps during the trouble on the *L’Escaut*. £1,000 went to Inspector French﻿—also Joan’s gift, and the remainder was divided among the officers and men of the Admiralty salvage boat.

===Chinese===
“通过苏珊。苏珊后来把一切都告诉了我。她跟着詹姆斯出去，看见我带着描图翻墙。她一路跟到我的住处，立刻打电话给赛姆。赛姆上门时她和他在一起，趁我换外衣，赛姆放她进了画室，她躲在一座画架后面，直到我们离开。她搜到描图，随后扬长而去。团伙本来无论如何也打算下星期去安特卫普，这件事打乱了他们的计划，他们决定立刻动身。丹格尔先行一步，安排莱斯考号提前几天启航。我们其余的人在根特住下，等船备航。”要讲的剩下也不多了。仍留在西卢里亚号上的那几根金条不久便捞起，两船扬帆，约五天后抵达查塔姆。全部金锭理论上归王室所有，但在特殊情况下作了一次宽厚的分配，百分之二十五发还给发现者们。普赖斯拒绝接受全份，于是达成了一项友好的协议：切恩、琼和普赖斯各得近三分之一，即每人200,000英镑。余下的20,000多英镑中，10,000英镑按琼的坚决主张给了苏珊·丹格尔。她说苏珊不是坏姑娘，在莱斯考号的祸乱里关键时刻大显了身手。1,000 英镑给了弗伦奇探长——也是琼的赠礼，其余的由海军部打捞船的军官和水手们分了。

===Original===
A few days after landing Maxwell Cheyne and Joan Merrill had occasion to pay a short visit to the church of St. Margaret’s in the Fields, after which Cheyne whirled his wife away to Devonshire, so that she might make the acquaintance of his family and see the country where began that strange series of events which in the beginning of the story I alluded to as **The Cheyne Mystery**.

===Chinese===
登陆几天后，麦克斯韦·切恩和琼·梅里尔有事往田野圣玛格丽特教堂作了一次短暂的造访；此后切恩便携着他的妻子直奔德文郡，好让她拜识他的家人，看看那片乡野——那一系列离奇事件正是从那里发端的，也就是我在故事开头提到过的**切恩疑案**。
